| `sa_insurance_type_reprice.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @InsuranceReprice @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.12 (#3535) correcting a VO's Versicherungsart between PKV and Privat Basis must reprice every already-documented session, at the corrected type's price **on that session's own treatment date**, behind a confirmation. **One commit ships both halves (`9a8dee2a`, 2026-08-29, `release/3.12.0`) and on staging only the app half behaves: the confirmation is live and gated exactly right; the repricing does not happen** → AC1/AC2 `fixme`'d, AC3–AC6 green. **The false-pass trap is the whole story of this ticket.** The ticket's own QA step is "check the VO's Behandlungsdetails or Preis view before and after", and the form's "Gesamtumsatz (Automatisch)" DOES move — 4355-1 goes **257,85 → 224,65** — which is how the PM's note records 6 of 6. It moves for a pre-existing reason: a VO's revenue is the **snapshotted** treatment lines plus a **live** calculation of the per-treatment/one-time fee lines, and only the fee half follows the current insurance type. Full repricing gives **178,33**; the €46,32 difference is the three PNF sessions still billing the PKV tariff 61,95 instead of 47,69 / 45,92 / 45,92. **Read the per-session rows, never the total.** The rows are `ActivityTreatment.resolvedTariff`, and that this is the right column is not assumed — a green **control test re-proves it every run**: a PRIVAT price entry effective 2025-06-01 moves those same rows 61,95 → 61,96 (130 across staging, #3378's recompute) and the DELETE moves them back, so the failure is specific to the insurance-correction path, not the column, the mechanism or the environment. **Fixture design does the work:** `PRIVAT` is one flat entry per treatment (2025-01-01) while `PRIVAT_BASIS` is a quarterly ladder (2025-01-01 / 04-01 / 07-01 / 10-01 / 2026-01-01) at different prices, so only a VO whose sessions straddle one of those steps separates "priced at its own date" from "priced today" — hence 4355-1 (3 sessions across **2025-07-01**, no invoice, 0 billing batches, all 18 checks passing) out of 257 candidates; note 2025-07-01 and 2025-10-01 are equal for most codes. AC2 has **one** possible fixture: only 4 `privat_basis` VOs exist on staging and two have no sessions (905301-2, one session documented natively at the Basistarif 29,63 → must become the flat PKV 39,00; it reads 29,63). AC3–AC6 are asserted on the form: an invoiced VO raises no dialog, a VO whose only invoice is **cancelled** does (correct — `prescription.invoice` IS `getActiveInvoice()`, which skips cancelled invoices and Stornos, so the frontend's `Boolean(prescriptionData.invoice)` implements "non-cancelled" exactly), a sessionless VO raises none in either direction, and GKV→PKV / GKV→BG / PKV→GKV raise none. **Traps:** the dialog has **no `role="dialog"` and no `aria-modal`** — a role query finds nothing and reads as "the dialog is missing"; its buttons are **Abbrechen / Ja**; **"Speichern" is inert** unless every creation-validation check auto-passed (#3340) — it POSTs `check-creation-validation` and, short of `allPassed`, sends nothing and shows nothing, which is why the file tries it and falls back to **"Zur Korrektur speichern" → "Speichern bestätigen"** (that route writes `creationValidationStatus: for_fixing`, restored by the test); the same VO can go either way between runs, so neither path may be assumed; `insuranceType` is only in the `billing:read` group and is **omitted when null** (8,772 of 34,240 VOs); and one `openVoForm()` per test — a second navigation between edit URLs does not remount the form. **Findings** → `fixme`: besides AC1/AC2, (a) **declining the confirmation leaves the field showing the new type** — "Abbrechen" correctly leaves the form VALUE at PKV (no `detect-lhb-bvb` fires, and a save writes `private`), but the dropdown goes on displaying "Privat Basis", so an admin who declines is looking at a corrected field that will silently save uncorrected; and (b) the dialog's count is `activityCount`, not the documented-session count (3 of 30 sampled VOs disagree — 4207-2 says 10 against 14). Deployment is deliberately ambiguous from a client: `recalculateForPrescription()` failures are **logged and swallowed** by design, so "API image predates the commit" and "it threw" look identical — staging's access-token TTL is 3600s, which only `ca915270` (later the same day) sets, so check the API log for **"Repricing after an insurance-type correction failed"** and re-run. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.insurance-reprice.page.ts` |
| `sa_german_translation_cleanup.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @GermanTranslation @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 (#3337) 58 German strings across the admin screens — 22 that showed English, 29 missing an umlaut or eszett, 7 half-translated. **Deployed; all four ACs pass and all 58 are verified, not a sample of 5–10.** **A translation ticket has exactly one authoritative surface: the dictionary the deployed bundle ships.** i18next reads nothing else, so the file locates the served `entry-*.js`, evaluates BOTH locale modules out of it and asserts every key — then walks the screens only for the part a dictionary cannot settle, namely whether the key the ticket names is the key that screen uses. **That distinction breaks one AC row:** AC1's pagination row names `controls.rows_per_page`, which is **dead** — every table reads `datatable.rows_per_page`, and that key already said "Zeilen pro Seite" at `39b280b4~1`, so the row describes a defect no screen ever showed and the PM's Admin-Board evidence for it would have passed against the pre-fix build too. **The before/after is exact**: at the parent of the fix commit all 58 keys held precisely the ticket's "Current Text Shown", today all 58 hold precisely the "Correct German", none of the 52 distinct old values survives anywhere else in the file, and the diff is `de.json` only (57 lines in `39b280b4` + the one-line `controls.view` follow-up `241c1711`). **Dead-key count confirmed with a wrinkle:** all 12 flagged keys are absent from the bundle, but three MORE have no whole-path reference — `diagnosis_list.filters.LO`, `document_center.bulk_status.type.ib`, `document_center.bulk_download.skipped_reason.ib_not_supported` — because they are built as template literals (`` `…type.${type}` ``); two are read off real screens here, so a whole-path scan alone would have called them dead. Live: Diagnosis List ("N von M Einträgen" **polled until the total is non-zero**, since it paints "0 von 0" first and a zero proves no interpolation; plus the "Logopädie" filter), Document Center ("Infoblätter"), Billing → Validierung ("Ausgewählte validieren"/"Ausgewählte schließen"), the "Ankündigungen" nav entry, Arzt Management ("Arzt hinzufügen") and TO Verwaltung ("Nicht abgerechnete VO"). **Findings** → `fixme`: (a) **two of the 58 fixed strings still render in English** — TO Verwaltung shows the column "Call to Action" and the Problemtyp option "Attitude", because `PerformanceDashboard/columns.tsx:183` and `types.ts:79` **hardcode** those labels while the corrected keys are read by other components; a flatten-and-diff of de.json vs en.json is structurally blind to a string in neither file, and the same screen also hardcodes "All/Open/Completed", "Red/Yellow/Green/Gray", "Clear Filters", "Therapist", "TO Staff"; (b) the pagination AC row above; (c) `columns.organizer` is correct ("Planungsstatus") but `ff486c92` removed the Organizer column from **Therapist Board v2** two days after this ticket merged, so the Testing Guidance's "Therapist Board v2 included" can only be followed on the LEGACY board now; (d) two English values remain in de.json — `vo_management.form.save_error`/`save_success` — inside the namespace the ticket excludes as "already-shipped" (both unreferenced, so nothing renders them). **Traps:** the bundle escapes non-ASCII (`L\xe4dt...`), so grepping the served text for "Lädt" finds nothing — the literal must be evaluated; the locale modules are content-hashed and renumbered every build, so they are found by shape, never by offset; and a third `<v>.exports={…}` module of the same shape parses cleanly with no `controls.loading`, so the German/English discriminator must require the key to EXIST, not merely differ. **This ticket also broke two suite selectors, fixed here**: `admin.arzt-management.page.ts` clicked "Add Arzt" and `sa_announcement.spec.ts` navigated to /Announcements/ — both now accept either spelling, because Production is still on the pre-3.12 build. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.translations.page.ts` |
| `sa_login_throttling.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @LoginThrottling @Security @Slow` | RC 3.12 (#3462, SEC-03) `POST /auth` accepted unlimited credential attempts; the fix adds Symfony `login_throttling` (`max_attempts: 5`, `interval: '1 minute'`, DoctrineDbal pool) plus a listener returning **429 + `Retry-After`** instead of a 401 that looks like a wrong password. **Deployed and working, with one AC that does not hold and one weakness no AC covers.** Green: 5 → 429 with `Retry-After: 60` and a body naming no user (AC2/AC9); a throttled username leaves a second username at 401 and a real account at 200 (AC4); the per-IP secondary trips at **exactly 25** distinct usernames = `5 x max_attempts`; AC3 is argued from ARITHMETIC rather than a burst — a per-container store would show 5 x task_count (2-4 tasks per the migration note), and the measured ceilings are exactly 5-6 per username and exactly 25 per IP; the window clears at **t+68s** with the full budget of 5 back (AC5). **Finding 1 — AC6 fails:** a successful login does not clear the failure budget. On admin.jhen: 2 failures → login **200** → 401, 401, 401, **429** — the 429 lands on the 6th failure counted from BEFORE the success, so the counter ran straight through it (reproduced on sa.jhen with 4 failures first: 401 then 429 right after the success). Symfony ships the reset (`LoginThrottlingListener::onSuccessfulLogin`), so it looks like the reset not reaching the same limiter key rather than a missing feature. `LoginThrottlingTest::testSuccessfulLoginUnaffectedBySubThresholdFailures` cannot catch it — it only asserts a correct login still works after 2 sub-threshold failures, never that the counter reset. **Finding 2 — the limiter has no lock, so a burst walks through it:** 20 SIMULTANEOUS guesses on one fresh username returned **15x401/5x429** then **10x401/10x429**, two to three times `max_attempts` — and an **already-throttled** username leaked **10 of 20** the same way, so even the 429 state does not survive a burst; the per-IP ceiling softens identically (25 sequential, but 40 of 40 accepted in batches of 10 concurrent). Symfony builds these limiters with `lock_factory: null`, so `consume()` is an unserialised read-modify-write; the DBAL pool shares the value but does not serialise updates. It matters because the ticket's goal is "stopping automated spraying" and a script sprays concurrently by default. **The measurement trap that makes this ticket easy to get wrong:** the threshold depends on how attempts are sent — **5** over one keep-alive connection, **6** over a fresh TCP connection per request, **10–15 of 20** fired at once. A test that parallelises and asserts "exactly 5" is flaky; one that only goes sequentially misses Finding 2 — and an AC3 test written as a burst measures Finding 2 rather than task sharing, which is how this file's first AC3 attempt failed. **And every 429 has two possible causes** — the username limiter or the 25/min per-IP one — so any measurement must start from a quiet window or a stray "threshold = 3" appears (seen once, from leftover IP budget). `waitForQuietWindow()` is a deliberate flat sleep: the duration IS the precondition and polling would consume the budget being waited for. **Etiquette, because these are real login attempts:** throwaway `@example.invalid` usernames for all bad-password work, real accounts only ever sent their correct password (except the `serial` AC6 test, which logs in again at the end). AC1/AC7/AC8 are source-level: config present; the functional test covers 429/Retry-After/no-username-echo/per-username but not the reset; and **no new env parameter was introduced**, so AC8's five-layer chain is vacuous — the PM's citation of `services.yaml: max_attempts: 5` is the **AWS S3 SDK retry** setting, unrelated. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.login-throttling.page.ts` |
| `sa_storno_classification.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @StornoClassification @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 (#3449, PR #3451) `Invoice::isStorno()` inferred Storno-ness from the invoice NUMBER (`str_starts_with(…, 'S')`) while `setOriginalInvoice()` held the relational truth; the fix makes the relation authoritative and converts the readers that had re-implemented the prefix in DQL. **Deployed; everything the change touches is consistent, and one reader was left behind.** **The honest difficulty: a converged predicate is invisible on data where the two already agreed** — no before/after exists, and manufacturing a drifted row means writing an invoice with a mismatched number, which is irreversible and exactly the state the ticket abolishes. So the file checks what IS decidable from outside. **The sharpest test is the "Art" column:** `InvoiceOrderFilter::addTypeSort` ranks in SQL with `CASE WHEN i.originalInvoice IS NOT NULL THEN 2 …` while `getInvoiceType()` computes the same ranking in PHP and the comment requires them to "stay in lockstep" — sorting the whole book and checking the serialized types come back monotonic compares the two row by row over all **555** invoices: **0 disagreements** both directions, stornos at 545..554 asc and 0..9 desc. **The direction nobody had checked:** the dev verified "57 rows with `original_invoice_id`, 0 violating the prefix"; the reverse — an **S-numbered invoice with NO original**, which the old predicate called a Storno and the new one calls ordinary — is where the fix *removes* Storno-ness, and it is also 0 of 555. Both directions now measured, plus `invoiceType` agreeing with the relation on every row. Also asserted: `getActiveInvoice()` never returns a Storno across the 9 VOs that carry one — **3 of them reproduce the #3448 arrangement exactly** (a Storno at a LOWER id than the live replacement: 4517-17, 9634-5 with two cancel/storno pairs, 3277-4), which is what `#[ORM\OrderBy(['id'=>'ASC'])]` on `Prescription::$invoices` now pins; and the ticket's "concrete stake" — the VO-rooted `getCancelledInvoices()` and the invoice-rooted `cancelledRegister=true` register return **the same 10** invoices. **Trap:** the Art sort parameter is **`order[type]`**, not `order[invoiceType]` — the field serializes as `invoiceType` but the filter registers `type`, and API Platform ignores an unknown `order` key **silently**, so `order[invoiceType]` returns the identical unsorted page for asc AND desc and a monotonicity check reports ~26 bogus violations. Read `search.template` on the collection when in doubt. **Extended for #3656 (2026-09-11):** the active-invoice assertion now also forbids a **CANCELLED original**, not only a Storno — that pair is the rule `InvoiceRegenerationService`'s entity-restore exists to preserve (a cancellation whose transaction fails leaves CANCELLED on the object while the DB rolled back, and `getActiveInvoice()` skips CANCELLED, so the VO loses its draft and grows a phantom row in `getCancelledInvoices()`). **The phantom is within-request and never persisted, so no stored-data check can detect it** — the detector is `InvoiceRegenerationServiceTest`; this pins the invariant it would violate. Measured live: 9 VOs carry a cancelled invoice and the served active invoice is **absent** on the 5 whose every invoice is cancelled-or-Storno (omitted, not null) and the live replacement on the other 4. **Finding** → `fixme`: `InvoiceRepository::findLegacyPkvPendingDatevSync()` (the #3440 old-format PKV arm) still excludes reversals with `NOT LIKE 'S%'`, untouched by the PR — and its own docblock says it "has no Storno arm … a cancelled original delivered here would raise a receivable no reversal ever clears". 84 invoices satisfy its number rule on staging (44 PKV + 40 GKV), 0 carry an originalInvoice, so it is latent exactly like the original ticket. The two remaining prefix uses in `CorrectStornoInvoiceAmountsCommand` are deliberate and correct — `reportHalfMatches()` compares BOTH predicates by design (it is the drift detector) and `findStornoIds()` requires both to agree before writing. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.storno-classification.page.ts` |
| `sa_patient_address_migration.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @PatientAddressMigration @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 (#3373) the one-time split of free-text patient addresses into `street`/`postalCode`/`city`, with the #3370 country marker stripped on the way, the original text kept, and a new billing check `billing_address_complete` (**validation id 53, timing `billing`, category `formalities`**) blocking a billing document when postal code and city are empty. **Run on staging 2026-08-29; all 7 ACs verified.** **A migration is verified by re-deriving it, not by sampling it:** the file carries a faithful JS port of `PatientAddressSplitter::split()` + `AddressNormalizer::stripCountryMarker()` and uses it as an INDEPENDENT ORACLE — for every stored row it computes what the fields should hold and compares. **2,345 addresses re-derived, 0 mismatches** (2,310 clean / 12 no-house-number / 23 unsplittable), which settles AC2/AC3/AC4/AC5 together. Also 0 postal codes violating #3372's `Assert\Regex(/^\d{5}$/)` and 0 rows with postal+city but no street. Two fixtures carry the rest: **3745-6** (raw `"An der Dahmebrücke, 15754 Heidesee, D"` → split despite no house number, `, D` stripped from the fields but kept in the raw, validation 53 = **true**) proves AC3+AC4+AC7 in one row; **964201-4** (raw `"123 Test, Berlin"` → no 5-digit anchor → fields empty, raw preserved, validation 53 = **false**) proves AC5+AC6. **Two traps that produce false findings.** (a) A loose `\bD\b` leftover-marker check flags `Hönower Str. 53 d` and `An de Geest 22` — both CORRECT preservations, because the normalizer's lookbehinds are `^`, `,` and `,\s`, deliberately **not** a bare space; the detector must mirror those anchors. (b) Rows with an EMPTY raw address are not the migration destroying the fallback — its query is `WHERE address IS NOT NULL AND TRIM(address) <> ''`, so it never read them. **Fetching is the hard part:** there is no `/patient_addresses` collection (404), addresses ride inside `/patients` at ~14 KB per patient, `groups[]=patient_address:read` drops `patientAddresses` entirely and no `properties[]` filter is registered — a straight walk **504s around page 7**, so the sample takes pages spread across the id space with retries. **Finding** → `fixme`: a text-less address is invisible to the migration but not to the check, so it can block billing while being absent from the review list AC1/AC5 promise. Of 4 text-less rows in the sample, 3 are guardian contact rows (#3187) that `getResidenceAddress()` correctly steps over (it prefers a Care Home row with NO contactPerson); the 4th — patient 875499, address 9727, its only address, care_home, isBilling, no contact person — genuinely fails the check with nothing on any list naming it. **Also observed:** only **5 VOs on staging carry a stored `billing_address_complete` verdict**, so AC6 is exercised on a handful of rows. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.patient-address-migration.page.ts` |
| `sa_monday_facility_sync.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @MondayFacilitySync @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 (#3344) the nightly Monday.com sync must not rename a facility that already has patients or VOs. **The guard IS deployed** — commit `03a93bfc` on `release/3.12.0`, *not* the `6cc3f431` the ticket's own comment says is still awaiting a cherry-pick; that comment is stale and says the issue is unclaimed. **The sync is a console command**, so the file tests its inputs and outcomes: which facility each Einrichtungs-ID resolves to, what is attached, and whether `mondayItemId` was written (the command sets it on **every** match, so its presence is a footprint of a row the sync reached). AC1 verified live — RR51 (id 235) now carries Monday's name and has **0 patients / 0 VOs**; AC2's precondition verified — ST1 (id 208) 21 patients/29 VOs and HH38 (id 222 "Tagesstätte Falkenbek") 10/11 — though whether Monday proposed a different name is console-only. Coverage: **212 of 245** facilities are in use, so the automatic rename is now the exception. **Finding** → `fixme`: the ID→facility match is not one-to-one. `$echMap[$ech->getEchId()] = $ech` over `findAll()` lets one row overwrite the other, and the gap report is built from the DEDUPLICATED keys (`array_diff(array_keys($echMap), $seenEchIds)`), so the dropped facility is not matched, not renamed, not skipped **and not gap-reported** — it appears nowhere in the run, and a gap row would print the survivor's name anyway. **E23**: facility 12 holds **243 VOs across 37 patients** and has NO mondayItemId (never matched) while facility 37 holds 0/0 and is the row the map keeps — the guard evaluates 0/0 and would rename freely. **Re-tested 2026-09-02** (after the PM's 2026-09-01 note and Jarn's 29 Aug run): unchanged, and HH38 has since become the more revealing twin — **both** its rows now carry a mondayItemId (221 "PFLEGEN & WOHNEN FINKENAU" 17 VOs/15 patients, 222 "Tagesstätte Falkenbek" 11/10), so which row the map keeps is **not stable**, and the PM's AC-2 evidence records only the 11-VO row. 2 of 243 IDs collide; 1 (E23) currently hides a used facility. Coverage today: 216 of 245 facilities carry a mondayItemId, 29 do not (the PM's run reported 30 gaps). **Measurement trap, pinned by an assertion:** `countAttachedRecords()` counts patients AND prescriptions, but **`/patients` registers no facility filter** and API Platform ignores an unknown `order`/filter key **silently** — `?elderlyCareHome=` returns the full **8,364** total, reading exactly like "this facility holds every patient". Only `/prescriptions?elderlyCareHome=` filters (and `?patient.elderlyCareHome=` is a DIFFERENT filter — a VO carries its own facility). So `vos > 0` proves the guard applies; `vos === 0` does not prove it does not. A 2026-09-01 staging dump was parsed once to close that gap and showed **0 facilities with patients but no VOs**, so the proxy was exact there — but not by construction. AC3/AC4 → `fixme` (console-only; AC4 satisfied structurally by the skip row's four fields). Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.monday-facility-sync.page.ts` |
| `sa_cancelled_datev_pairs.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @DatevStornoPairs @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 (#3502) a cancelled invoice and its Storno must reach DATEV as a zero-sum pair; before, `datevSyncable()` excluded CANCELLED so the original never went, its Storno had nothing to pair against, and DATEV got neither half. **Deployed and observable — because DATEV sync is now switched ON for staging**, a change from #3440's `DATEV_SYNC_ENABLED=false` (2026-08-24): **105 of 563 invoices carry a `datevSyncedAt`, 0 failed attempts**, and the cancelled pairs were pushed 2026-08-29…09-02. Any spec assuming staging never pushes (the #3440/#3499 files) is reasoning about the old environment. **The shipped design differs from the ticket's Developer Reference, for the better:** rather than widening `datevSyncable()`, it adds `datevSyncableWithStornoPair()` — derived from the first "so the two lists cannot drift" — used only on the paired path, with a docblock warning that a caller which cannot push the reversal (the #3440 legacy PKV arm, which has no Storno arm — the very finding recorded on #3449) must keep the narrow list or it "books a permanently unbalanced cancelled receivable". Verified: **10 of 10 cancelled invoices have a Storno**, 9 pairs fully in DATEV with the **original always synced before its Storno** (asserted on real timestamps), and the 10th held. **AC3 cannot be read off `invoiceAmount` — doing so produces a false finding:** both halves are stored as MAGNITUDES (35,63 + 35,63 = 71,26, not 0), and `PostingProposalMapper::mapStornoInvoice()` negates the reversal at booking time, so the client-checkable half is that the magnitudes match to the cent (all 10 do). **AC5 verified precisely:** the single held pair R726-22/S726-1 belongs to **Curano Stuttgart GmbH**, one of the two entities the ticket names as not yet activated, and sits at **0 attempts** — held, not failing, which is the distinction (`datevSyncAttempts` is a FAILURE counter, so 0 never means "not pushed"). AC7: 0 of 10 cancelled invoices are marked paid. **Trap:** pair by the `originalInvoice` RELATION, never the `S` prefix (#3449). AC4/AC6 → `fixme`: the "~28 pairs" is a production figure (staging's whole book holds 10) and no run log distinguishes a backlog sweep from per-cancellation pushes; and AC6 needs a failure, of which staging has **0 across all 563**. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.storno-classification.page.ts` (extended with `datevState()` / `cancelledPairs()`) |
| `sa_auth_token_security.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @AuthTokenSecurity @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 #3460 (SEC-01.4 transparent refresh + TTL cut) and #3463 (SEC-04 token material out of plaintext storage), **web target**. **Both deployed** — #3460 as `857d30e19`/`4de74fd4a`/`ca9152701` (PR #3542), #3463 as `4d6feab18`. **AC1 measured from the token, not the yaml**: `token_ttl` reads `%env(int:JWT_TOKEN_TTL)%` in every block, so the grep the AC describes can only ever show a variable — staging's issued JWT has `exp - iat` = **3600 s on both the login and the refresh path**. Rotation verified as a CHAIN (4 consecutive refreshes, 5 distinct tokens) rather than one hop, a spent token is refused 401, and **replaying a spent token revokes the whole family** — the live descendant, checked working immediately before, dies too. That property is worth proving because #3463's accepted-risk note leans on it. #3463 AC3 verified the way the AC demands, by INSPECTING the stored value: `auth-state` holds only `{user, fetching}` with no JWT anywhere in the payload, `auth-refresh-token` holds the opaque refresh token, `_auth_token_migration_done` is set — matching `packages/auth/README.md`'s documented web strategy (access token in memory, refresh token in localStorage as an accepted risk reviewed by 2026-11-30). The web client therefore calls `/token/refresh` exactly **once on boot**, which is asserted. **Traps:** replaying a spent token destroys the session you are holding, so tests must log in fresh per case and never share a token chain (this is also why `.auth/*.json` is single-use); and a boot refresh is not evidence of the 401 path — count refreshes inside a window. **Findings** → `fixme`: (a) **#3460 AC6 is NOT verified here** — the ticket calls 5+ concurrent 401s collapsing into one refresh "the highest-risk regression in the whole epic", and the best in-app burst reachable from a browser put only 1–2 requests in flight, so the test **skips with the count** rather than passing on weak evidence (1 refresh for 2 concurrent 401s was observed, which is consistent but not a demonstration); (b) the README's accepted-risk note leads with "the access-token TTL is 1h" as what bounds the web exposure, but the refresh token beside it in localStorage mints a fresh access token on demand — this suite's own `mintUiSession()` is the working demonstration — so what actually bounds it is rotation + family reuse detection, both verified above; (c) #3463's AC1/AC3-native/AC4/AC5/AC9 and #3460's Android half of AC5 need a device, with the repo-side evidence recorded inline (`expo-secure-store ~15.0.8`, the 2048-byte SecureStore size guard, the OTA-runtime fingerprint note). Also covers **#3516** (`useRoles` loading never resolved with no user, fixed in `cefde109d`/PR #3605): a hook has no endpoint, so the deployed bundle is the surface — the minified effect body reads `n(e.user?.roles||[]),l(!1)`, i.e. the loading setter runs **unconditionally**, with no `e.user&&` guard. **Finding** → `fixme`: the bug had **no user-visible symptom on web** — its one consumer that branches on `loading` (`app/(tabs)/admin/messenger.tsx`, which renders `null` until it resolves) is unreachable signed out, because the app's auth guard redirects every route to the login form first (measured: `/admin/messenger`, `/therapist`, `/dashboard` all land on `/` with the login form). Real bug, correctly fixed, lower severity than "spins indefinitely" implies. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.auth-refresh.page.ts` |
| `sa_personio_absences_epic.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @PersonioAbsences @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 **epic** #3394 (therapist absences from Personio) — an epic has no ACs of its own, so this file tests the **shared rules that cut across its three sub-tickets** (#3395 sync + T Board, #3396 Orga board, #3397 Management board, all shipped in `d10e8f075`) rather than re-testing each. **Deployed and consistent.** History/horizon verified to the day: **9,481 absence days spanning 2026-01-01 … 2027-09-03** — the backfill start and exactly 12 months ahead of the run — with **0 rows outside either bound** (the horizon is computed at run time, not pinned). Categories: exactly three buckets (KRANK 3,182 / URLAUB 4,198 / SONSTIGE 2,101), seven distinct Personio type names under SONSTIGE, and **no Home Office row**, which is a working day and must never be synced. The absence-rate formula is **reconstructed from minutes rather than compared to itself** — `/kpis/management/working-hours` gives Krank 23,784 + Urlaub 28,800 over Soll 241,740 for 2026‑06‑29…07‑05, matching the served krankenquote 0.0983866964507322 and fehlquote 0.21752295855050882 to 10 places, with `sonstigeAbwesenheitMinutes` 12,396 carried separately and excluded. **The epic's own sequencing rule ("E2 owns the definition, E3 matches it") is the sharpest test**: the Orga trend's bucket for that week and the Management card agree to 12 decimal places. **Traps:** the route is **`/absence-days`, not `/absence_days`** — the entity sets an explicit `uriTemplate`, so the snake_case form every other collection uses answers **404** and reads exactly like "not deployed" (pinned by a test); the collection filters only by `user`, with no date filter, so a window means walking all ~9.5k rows; and the quotas are 0..1 ratios, not percentages. **Findings** → `fixme`: (a) **the half-day rule has no live instance** — all 9,481 rows are `dayPortion: FULL`, so the "0.5 of a day" rule binding on all three sub-tickets is unexercised everywhere it applies, although each PM note reports its ACs verified; (b) **the Orga board never got #3398's Zeitraum fix although `d10e8f075`'s own message says it did** — `OrgaTrendProvider` has no `rangeFrom` (it always calls `buildWindows`) and `useOrgaTrend` omits it, so `orga-trend?level=woche&to=2026-07-05&rangeFrom=2026-06-29` returns **12 buckets spanning 2026-04-13…07-05** instead of the week asked for; on the Therapeuten-Orga board in Zeitraum mode the Verlauf chart therefore still describes a different window from its cards — #3398's exact bug, and it now carries this epic's absence quotas too; (c) the approved-only rule is undecidable from a client (Flow stores only what the sync wrote, so a pending request is absent either way). Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.absences.page.ts` |
| `sa_optica_export_problems.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @OpticaExportProblems @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 (#3343) the six Optica export checks now surface BEFORE the .rz download — an amber badge on the GKV-Abrechnung batch list, a warning marker with an inline issue panel inside a batch, and a non-blocking warning when a batch is marked Ready to Send. **Deployed (PR #3414); AC3–AC9 verified live.** All three surfaces read one endpoint, **`GET /billing_batches/export-problems`**, which returns one item per UNSENT batch with a failing VO carrying `OpticaExportValidator`'s groups verbatim plus `doctorId`/`practiceId` for the fix links — **sent batches are excluded server-side**, so AC4 is a property of the data, not a UI filter, and both halves are asserted. Live: **8 unsent batches, all with problems, 38 problem VOs**, against 45 sent with no badge; the badge SET is compared to the endpoint id-for-id (63–70) and each badge's text to `problemVoCount` ("Export-Probleme · 13 VOs"), so a badge on the wrong row cannot pass. AC5/AC6/AC9 on batch 63: the badge opens the batch, every marker belongs to a flagged VO, and of its **9 clean VOs the 5 painted on the page carry no marker at all** (the cell renders `null`, so absence is the assertion). AC7 is verified on BOTH meanings of "same wording": the API strings are byte-identical between the pre-send endpoint and the authoritative download-time check on all **5** codes, and the screen renders the German map (`Versichertenstatus fehlt` …) that also feeds the download-blocked dialog. **Traps:** the detail table paginates (13 problem VOs, 5 markers on page 1); the badge sits in an `aria-disabled` cell inside a horizontally scrolling table, so click `{force:true}` with a timeout; **`aria-expanded` is never rendered** on the marker — assert the panel's CONTENT; `getByText('Change Status').first()` opens a DIFFERENT row's menu; the Actions column is off-screen below ~2400px wide; and the bundle escapes non-ASCII (`w\xfcrde`), so grep the ASCII run (#3337). **Findings** → `fixme`: (a) **every live LANR/BSNR issue has no fix-link target** — the only two such VOs (9634-1, 963403-2) have `doctorId: null` and `practiceId: null`, causally so (a VO with no doctor has no LANR and no practice), and the deployed code correctly renders no link, so AC8's promise never applies to the population it targets — and the hint "Edit the doctor to set the LANR" is the wrong instruction for a doctorless VO (same shape as #3288); (b) the marker sets `accessibilityState={{expanded}}` but **no `aria-expanded` reaches the DOM**, open or closed — the identical RNW gap #3400 found on the date-basis popover, so a screen reader is never told the panel expanded; (c) the frontend's own English copy for `MISSING_BSNR` ("Doctor practice BSNR missing" / "Edit the doctor to set the practice BSNR") drops the API's "Set the BSNR on the VO" half, which matters because the VO carries its own BSNR (#3288). AC1's live menu drive → `fixme` (the click lands but no status change follows — **0 `batch_status_change` logs ever on batch 63**, so it is a harness limit, not a defect; the warning's copy, its problem-VO list and its `confirmHidden` non-blocking shape are asserted in the bundle instead). AC2 skipped: all 8 unsent batches have problems, so no clean fixture exists. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.optica-export-problems.page.ts` |
| `sa_invoice_no_deletion.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @InvoiceRetention @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.12 (#3492) a finalized invoice can never be deleted — invoices are financial records under an 8-year retention period (§ 14b UStG). **Deployed (`bd91fa410`, 2026-08-26); AC2 and AC3 verified live, AC1 console-only and AC4 a document.** **The implementation is wider than the ticket asked and than the PM note records:** the ticket named ONE command, the commit found and guarded **three** (`app:invoice:delete-all`, `app:cleanup:billing-data`, `app:cleanup:gkv-billing-data`), with an **allow-list** (`dev`/`test`/`staging`) read from **`app.environment`** so an unset value fails closed. The PM note records the guard as "`APP_ENV !== 'prod'`" — the ticket's own Developer Reference warns `APP_ENV` is `prod` on staging too, so that would not separate the environments; the code does the opposite, correctly. AC2 is a **stated rule, not the FK**: `InvoiceRetentionListener` on `Prescription::preRemove` is the choke point for the `Patient → Prescription` cascade, console commands and future code, and `PatientDeleteProcessor` turns it into a readable 422. Verified live on three patients — `DELETE /patients/8430` → **422 "…6 invoice(s) on their prescriptions are financial records subject to statutory retention…"**, with the count cross-checked against an independent per-patient count from `/invoices` (8430→6, 877→4, 4425→3), the message naming no patient (GDPR), and the patient plus every invoice still present afterwards. AC3: `DELETE` on `/invoices`, `/invoice_logs`, `/prescriptions` and `/billing_batches` all **405**, for nonexistent AND live ids. **Traps:** a missing operation answers **405** while an existing one with a missing id answers **404**, so a single probe cannot tell "no delete exists" from "that id is gone" — the spec probes `/activity_treatments/999999999` (**404**) as the control that makes 405 meaningful; and the AC2 test issues a REAL delete whose passing outcome is the refusal, so it must only ever name a patient that HAS invoices (one without would be deleted, irreversibly) — there is deliberately no positive control. **Findings** → `fixme`: (a) AC1 is unrunnable from a client and the PM's description of the guard is wrong; (b) **AC4's document is not missing** — the PM note calls it "currently missing" and waives the AC, but the commit ships `api/docs/invoice-retention-compliance.md` (211 lines) covering all five bullets in sections 1–5, with section 6 listing what it deliberately does NOT assert (RDS snapshots, S3 versioning/object lock, dump retention, restoration rehearsal, and the § 14b UStG basis pending the tax advisor's sign-off). What is actually outstanding is the AC's own wording — the document is in the API repo, not attached to the issue where Stefan's team can take it to an auditor. Population: **561 invoices across 495 patients**, all now undeletable. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.invoice-retention.page.ts` |
| `sa_billing_exports_structured_address.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @BillingExportAddress @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 (#3375) DATEV debtors, the ETI claim, the Optica export and the IB record must read `street`/`postalCode`/`city` directly, with the old free-text parser kept as the fallback (AC5). **Deployed (`b786b073b` + `7d269599e`, PR #3418). AC2's client half is verified outright; AC1/AC3/AC4 are unreachable from a client and the file PROVES that rather than asserting it.** **The change is invisible by construction:** #3373's migration derived the structured fields with the very splitter these exports fall back to, so of 1,825 sampled address rows the **1,806 both arms can answer are byte-identical — 0 divergences**. On today's data the ticket is a pure refactor, which is why the commit itself had to corrupt the free text to attribute a path. **Near-miss worth knowing:** a hand-rolled `splitAddress` port that omits `AddressNormalizer::stripCountryMarker`'s `\s{2,}` collapse reports 4 false divergences ("Südostallee  212" vs "Südostallee 212") — reuse the #3373 helper. AC5 measured: **1,806 structured / 15 parser fallback / 4 neither**, and every fallback row is asserted to have an incomplete triple (the gate is all-three-or-nothing). The 15 are the addresses the migration could not split — multiline guardian rows, foreign addresses, `D-13407`/`D13407` PLZ prefixes, a six-digit `254697`, and a bare `"D"`. AC2 in the served bundle, verbatim: `hasUsableAddress` reads `t.street?.trim()&&t.postalCode?.trim()&&t.city?.trim()` and keeps `t.address.includes(',')` as the fallback, wired into the ETI dialog as `addressOk:(0,h.hasUsableAddress)`. **Traps:** the four consumers do NOT read the same row (DATEV provisioning + ETI read the **billing** address; DATEV bootstrap, Optica and IB read the **residence** one); #3375's gate is all three fields while #3374's letters take postal code + city, a distinction with **0 live instances** (no row carries a partial triple); and `describe.configure({ timeout })` does **not** reach a `beforeAll` — the 4-minute sample walk needs `test.setTimeout()` INSIDE the hook. Reachability re-measured each run: Optica **422** on all 5 newest batches, `/datev_debtors` `/debtors` `/datev_debtor_accounts` all **404**, and of 76 ib_records the 9 without a stored PDF answer **404 "PDF not yet available for this IB record."** — an IB renders its PDF ONCE, lazily, on the first signed-url call, so every stored one shows its signing-day render. **Findings** → `fixme`: (a) AC1/AC3/AC4 are unobservable, with the evidence above; (b) the ETI pre-check still waves through the rows it exists to catch — over 1,675 billing rows the old comma check passes 1,671 and the new one 1,672 (**net +1 patient**, `"Luckenwalder Str. 50 in 14913 Jüterbog"`), while **13 rows pass on the comma alone although NEITHER arm can produce an address**, so for exactly the patients a pre-submission check exists to catch, nothing changed. **The PM note's 5/5 is code-reading**; its "Jarn's run created 10 debtors without parsing failures" is not evidence of the structured path, since the fallback parser succeeds on those rows too, and its own AC-2 row records the dialog as untested. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.billing-export-address.page.ts` |
| `sa_management_trend_parity.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @TrendParity @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.12 (#3398) the Management board's KPI cards, Verlauf chart and Detail table must show the same revenue for the same filters and period (an admin saw 33.217,54 € on the chart against 33.046 € on the card for 5 Aug). **Deployed on both halves; all four ACs pass.** **The shipped fix is NOT the one the ticket's Developer Reference describes** — that blamed the therapist POPULATION (cards resolve it per period, the trend over the whole 12-period span) and proposed unifying them; what landed leaves that alone and changes the WINDOW: in **Zeitraum** mode the board sends **`rangeFrom`** and the series TILES `[rangeFrom..to]` instead of trailing 12 periods back from `to`. A test written to the reference's theory looks in the wrong place. **It also has no commit of its own** — searching for `task/3398` or a commit mentioning the number finds nothing; it rode in with the Personio absence work (`d10e8f075`, 2026-08-19) under a `Ref #3398` trailer. Verified: four custom ranges tile exactly and Σ buckets == the card (754.384,94 over 3 weekly buckets and over 17 daily ones; 3.020.589,76 over 3 months; 277.282,81 over one ISO week), while the same request WITHOUT `rangeFrom` returns 12 trailing periods describing a different window — the bug's shape, pinned so a regression is visible. AC1 per-period: **96 periods** (12 Tag x 5 anchors offline, plus 12 Tag + 12 Woche + 12 Monat in the file) match on treated revenue, validated revenue AND €/Stunde, at production-scale figures. AC4: card == Σ Therapeut:innen rows == Σ Gruppen rows on a week start, week end, month start, month end and a mid-period day. AC3: 8 filtered views (3 therapists, 2 teams, Ohne TO-Team, GKV, PKV). **AC2 is manufactured, because staging has no fixture** — every activity is stamped `00:00:00Z` or `10:00:00Z` (02:00/12:00 Berlin), so the test PATCHes one therapist's only session of 2026-06-30 to 23:59 and then 00:00 Berlin and restores it; both surfaces keep the 74,90 on 30.06 and no total moves. That test also MEASURES the population asymmetry the reference worried about and shows it is harmless: at 00:00 Berlin the therapist appears in the PREVIOUS day's detail table with revenue **0**. **Traps:** `/activities/{id}` is **404** — the resource declares GetCollection/calendar/bulk-Post/Delete/Patch but **no item Get**, so read a row through `?prescription=` (it reads like a permissions problem); Periode mode is NOT a mismatch, so summing its 12 buckets against a custom-range card reports a bug that does not exist; `revenuePerHour` is `null` from the trend and `0` from the cards on some empty periods; and KPI reads are cached 5 min (#3401) — the Monat trend is the board's slowest request, so run at `--workers=1`. **The PM note verifies a tautology:** its AC1 evidence compares the KPI card against the **Umsatz-Realisierung** waterfall's "Erarbeitet", which #3177 already asserts is equal by construction — the **Verlauf** chart the ticket is about is never read. **Findings** → `fixme`: (a) a malformed `rangeFrom` (`not-a-date`) answers **500**, though the provider documents malformed values as "ignored (Periode behaviour)" — the guard checks `is_string` and `!== ''` but never parseability, and inverted/empty DO fall back correctly; (b) `€/Stunde` comes back `null` from the trend where the card returns `0` on some zero-revenue days (2026-08-23/29/30, but not 08-28/31), so a chart draws a gap where the card prints 0,00 € — AC1's own third figure. The `MAX_RANGE_PERIODS` cap is verified (546 daily buckets → **400**, 300 → served) and the CSV export half is unreachable (**403**, allowlisted per #3181). Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.trend-parity.page.ts` |

AC2: PKV draft **R526-73** goes 378,00 → **354,00**, but only after `POST /generate-invoice` (the amount is frozen at issue time, #3093). AC3: the SQL engine's per-VO figure on `/kpis/management/billing-backlog` equals `totalRevenue` on **1,052 of 1,052** VOs and reports the corrected number on the 3 affected VOs in it (642,38 not 750,20; 177,78 not 217,05; 636,01 not 649,10). **Traps:** an embedded ActivityTreatment carries `@id` but **no bare `id`** (a delete by `at.id` 404s); a regenerable draft is `not_sent` **AND** `datevSyncedAt` null — R126-86 is not_sent but synced, so it answers 400 "A cancellation reason is required to regenerate an issued invoice", and only 2 of 561 invoices qualify today; KPI reads are cached ~5 min (#3401), so AC3 is parity, not a live delta; and a session with **no** AT rows at all keeps the unconditional legacy arm (0 such countable days since May). AC4 → `fixme`: `optica-export` answers 422 on all 53 batches (#3288) and `PrescriptionExportService` is console-only — with an evidence test showing **0 of the 38 affected VOs is invoiced or batched**, so nothing was reduced under an issued document. **Finding 1** → `fixme`: **92 of the 179 days are not removals at all** — the session documents the OTHER home-visit fee (HB-E where the VO prescribes HBH-E and the reverse; HB-PT against HBH-PT) across 15 VOs, so the day now bills **no** per-treatment fee: the prescribed one is dropped for want of a carrier and the documented one is never billed, because the engines read fees from the prescribed list only and the per-session loop skips non-`treatment` rows. The other 87 days over 23 VOs are genuine removals and behave exactly as the ticket asks. **Finding 2** → `fixme`: a fee left on a **planned** (or rejected) session of a day that DID have a delivered session is dropped — VO 2856-30 on 2026-05-21 goes 256,32 → 243,23 when the delivered session's row is removed while the planned one still shows HBH-PT; the commit records this as a pending PM decision, 7 such day/fee pairs exist and 0 are currently stranded. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.per-treatment-fee.page.ts` |

AND insurance_type IS NULL` (idempotent, never overwrites an admin's value). The spec re-implements exactly that: **640 patients, 640 exact matches on both fields, 0 same-date ties**. | `sa_cross_area_fee_removal.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @CrossAreaFees @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 (#3577) the one-off removal of a wrong-therapy-area fee from 93 TheOrg-imported VOs (`app:prescription:remove-cross-area-fees`, preview by default, `--execute` to apply). **Applied on staging 2026-09-03; AC2/AC3/AC4/AC5 verified, AC1/AC6/AC7 are not product surfaces.** **A delete-only correction leaves NO audit trail** — a removed `PrescribedTreatment` is simply gone and no `prescription_log` records it — so AC2's second half ("leaves every other fee/Heilmittel unchanged") cannot be diffed and is attacked from the other side: **re-derive the cross-area rule over the whole book** and see where it still fires. `/prescriptions?treatment=<id>&therapyType=<x>&itemsPerPage=1` → `totalItems` makes the entire `fee catalogue x areas` grid affordable (28 fee treatments x 2 foreign areas = 56 requests). Result: **86 of the 93 listed pairs are reachable on staging and all 86 are gone**, 7 sit on VOs that do not exist here (5656-4, 137-6, 460-5, 659-2, 578-4, 578-5, 1000015-4); the ticket's broader **End Goal** holds too — every remaining code on all 83 resolvable VOs belongs to that VO's own area, none was stripped of its Heilmittel, and no VO was emptied. **AC2's scope half is the load-bearing one:** 15 cross-area pairs survive across 4 combinations and **0 of them is on the list**, so the command was list-scoped rather than a blanket sweep — a blanket sweep would have taken the survivors too. AC3: all 7 sheet-"validated" VOs exist here but only **3** (6330-8, 6104-2, 4164-18) are validated on staging, so the population is taken from the LIVE `validationStatus`, never the sheet — and those 3 were corrected and are **still `validated`**, i.e. the EntityManager write bypassed the edit lock without lifting it. AC4: R526-55 (overdue, 581,16) is unmodified, while its VO 6330-8 now computes **571,86** — the invoice deliberately sits **9,30 above** its VO, which is the removed AB-L and exactly what the AC asks for; R526-156 does not exist here. AC5: no `complete_and_sent` batch has an `updatedAt` on/after the run (newest 2026-08-21), though the specific fixture is production-only — **0 of the 83 listed VOs sits in a staging batch**, confirmed twice (`billingBatchCount` and a walk of all 54 batches). **Traps:** **`therapyType` is `physiotherapy` / `occupational_therapy` / `speech_therapy`**, not the sheet's `PT`/`ERGO`/`SSSST` — an unknown value is accepted **silently** and answers `totalItems: 0`, which reads exactly like the ticket passing, so the first test proves the filter partitions before any zero is believed; **never take a VO's area from the spreadsheet** (staging's 2666-11 is `physiotherapy` where the sheet says SSSST, which manufactures a false End-Goal violation); the **`6330` in "patient Ingeborg Aleth (patient 6330)" is the VO-number prefix, not the API patient id** — `/patients/6330` is an unrelated person and Aleth is patient **5054**, reachable only through VO 6330-8; `billing:read` embeds the invoice **without `updatedAt`** (default group only) and the patient **without `id`**; and `/billing_batches` caps a page at 50 whatever `itemsPerPage` says, so "fewer than I asked for" is not the last page. **CORRECTION (2026-09-10, from #3576's testing):** this file's `AREA_TO_THERAPY_TYPE` mapped `ERGO` to **`occupational_therapy`**, which is not a `TherapyType` value — the filter accepts it silently and answers 0 — so **every probe against an Ergotherapie VO came back empty**. The residual was reported below as 15 pairs over 4 combinations; corrected to `ergotherapy` it is **13 combinations / 34 hits over 29 VOs**, and all 8 hidden combinations were Ergo VOs. The same constant also made the End-Goal stray-code loop **skip** every Ergotherapie VO (an unmapped `therapyType` yielded `undefined` and `continue`d), which hid a second finding: **3309-7 and 6679-3 (both `ergotherapy`, both ON the list) still carry a cross-area Heilmittel — KG [PT] and PNF [PT]**. Their listed FEE was removed correctly, so AC2's list-scoping conclusion stands; what remains is a Heilmittel the fee-scoped command never targeted, on two VOs the spreadsheet records as Physiotherapie while the API says ergotherapy. The End-Goal test is now split — the emptied/Heilmittel-present halves stay green, a green evidence test prints the strays, and the stray assertion is `fixme`'d with the two VOs; the map lookup now THROWS on an unmapped value instead of skipping. **Finding** → `fixme`: **11 of the 15 survivors are `imported: true` TheOrg VOs with the identical defect** (137-29, 1969-31, 5656-5, 2746-7 carrying AB-L; 460-30, 2517-9, 578-11, 578-12, 659-11 carrying HB-E; 2666-17, 2880-22 carrying HB-PT), several of them sibling VOs of patients whose OTHER VO is listed — not a defect in the command, which AC2 requires to stop at the list, but a question about the list, and exactly what #3576's validation will start reporting. Also: the PM's AC-5 evidence cites "AB-L on non-Logo VOs: 125 → 71", but **71 is the count of ALL VOs carrying AB-L** (65 of them legitimately speech-therapy); the non-Logo residual is **4**. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.cross-area-fees.page.ts` |
| `sa_legacy_invoice_amount_lock.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @LegacyAmountLock @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 (#3604) the 116 invoices issued 15 Jun – 16 Jul 2026 predate the amount snapshot (#3052/#3093), so `getInvoiceAmount()` recomputes them LIVE on every read and a later calculation change silently moves an already-issued total. Two console commands: `app:invoice:lock-legacy-amounts --file=… [--force]` (steps 1+2) and `app:invoice:refresh-unsent-amounts [--force]` (step 3). **Both ran on staging — steps 1+2 on 2026-09-03 08:09–08:10 UTC, step 3 on 2026-09-03 08:25 and again 2026-09-04 08:52. AC1 (applied half), AC2, AC3, AC4, AC6 and AC7 pass; AC5's rule holds but the two invoices it names by number do not (see the findings); preview mode is unreachable.** **The whole file exists because `pkvAmount`/`copaymentAmount` are in NO serialization group** — not `invoice-list:read`, not `billing:read`, not the default — so "is this invoice locked?", the question every AC turns on, has no field to read. Three surfaces answer it: (a) the **`invoice_log` note** each command writes (`GET /invoice_logs?type=note`), which carries the locked amount, the source, the DATEV posting date AND the live value at lock time — i.e. every column AC1 asks the *preview* to print, so the applied trail stands in for a preview no client can run; all **115** notes match the reference file on amount, source and posting date, across all three sources (112 `datev_accounts_receivable` / 2 `sent_amount_per_t105` / 1 `flow_live_value`); (b) **IEEE-754 float dust** — a written snapshot is a DB decimal and round-trips clean, a live recompute arrives as `35.632000000000005`; exactly **6 of 566** invoices carry dust and none of them is one the commands wrote (one-way: dust proves UNLOCKED, clean does not prove locked); (c) **divergence from the VO's live value** (`totalRevenue` for PKV, `copaymentAmount` for copayment), which is the only evidence that shows "stops changing" rather than "looks right today" — 226-2 serves **390** against a VO computing 312, 326-43 **702** against 390, R126-50 **85,44** against 126, R426-61 **256,32** against 378. **AC4/step 2 is verified precisely, in both directions:** the stored PDF's own `/CreationDate` (served as-is, #3495) puts all **6** moved invoices inside the lock window (08:09:39 → 08:10:48) while three locked-but-unmoved controls still carry their originals from **2026-08-13**, so the re-render fired for exactly the invoices whose amount changed and issue dates are untouched (all still 2026-06/07). **AC6 turns out to be observable after all:** the regeneration engine re-renders the PDF, so a fresh `/CreationDate` is its footprint — R426-72 and R126-119 (refreshed 3 Sep 08:26) re-render at 09:04:42/09:05:02 and R126-120 (4 Sep 08:52:33) at 08:52:50, the lag being the async PDF worker. AC7: all 83 refreshed invoices are `not_sent`, and no ineligible invoice was written in either refresh window. AC3: **R226-38** is the file's one already-locked row — stored 35,62 against the file's 35,63 — and the command reported the difference rather than overwriting, which is right, because the VO computes 35,62. **Traps:** there is **no `invoiceNumber` filter** on `/invoices` — `?invoiceNumber=X` is accepted and IGNORED, silently returning the unfiltered book; `updatedAt` lives only in the default group while `invoiceType` lives only in `invoice-list:read`, so the book needs two reads merged; **a step-3 write does not always bump `updatedAt`** (83 notes, 3 timestamps), so bucket step 1 by timestamp but step 3 by notes; the note's dash is an em dash (U+2014); and a PDF download through `page.request` takes 2–4 min against ~10 s for the same file over curl, so the two PDF tests carry their own long `test.setTimeout`. **Findings** → `fixme`: (a) **AC5 names 126-11 and R426-43 as the "no locked amount yet" cases step 3 must pick up, and both are still unlocked** — their patients hold copayment exemptions, so the recompute is 0 and the SKIPPED_NOT_COMPUTABLE guard refuses to write (right guard; but the two invoices the AC singles out are exactly the two still recomputing on every read, so the AC is unsatisfiable as written and needs a PM decision); (b) the file's **single `copayment_amount` row is R226-38**, the row that was skipped as already-locked, so step 1 wrote `pkvAmount` 115 times and `copaymentAmount` **zero** times — the `field`-column branch the Developer Reference insists on has no coverage from this rehearsal, and a mis-targeted snapshot would look exactly like no lock at all; (c) an evidence test prints the ticket's AC-1 table beside reality — it predicts **4** moves (R526-18, R426-57, R426-61, R126-50) but staging produced **6** (126-5, 226-2, 326-43, 426-15, R126-50, R426-61) and R526-18/R426-57, the ticket's headline drift examples, already matched. **Cross-ticket:** 126-5 and 426-15 were locked 9,30 BELOW their then-live value, and #3577's removal of the stray AB-L from VOs 4164-18/6104-2 has since brought the live figure down to exactly the locked amount — the ticket's "an unrelated fee posting error being corrected separately" is #3577, confirmed to the cent. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.legacy-invoice-lock.page.ts` |
| `sa_manual_heilmittel_billing.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @ManualHeilmittel @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 (#3603) a manually attached Heilmittel (`kind: "passiv"` — AEB, AEB-BV) must be billed everywhere the validation view shows it. Both engines skipped the kind (`ActivityRevenueCalculator`'s `TreatmentKind::TREATMENT !== …` continue, `RawKpiCalculator`'s `treatment.kind = 'treatment'`), so the line printed on the session row while the VO total, the PKV invoice, the Optica export and KPI revenue all omitted it. **Deployed; AC1/AC2/AC4/AC5/AC6/AC7/AC8 and AC9's catalogue half verified.** **The population is fully enumerable, which is what makes the ticket testable:** `/activity_treatments?treatment=<id>` is a registered filter, so every attachment of every passiv code comes out of ~455k rows in three requests — **12 rows (9 AEB, 3 AEB-BV, 0 KT-H-BV) over 10 VOs on 2026-09-05 — 14 by 09-07, since the fixture is live data**, against only 3 passiv items in the whole catalogue. **Verified with a DUAL ORACLE, never by reading the total**: `calculatePrescriptionBreakdowns()` is ported with the kind filter as the only variable, and the pre-fix arm has to reproduce the ticket's own quoted figures. It does, exactly — VO 1762-28 gives **pre 1035,25 / post = served** (1.118,51 on 2026-09-05, 1.160,31 on 09-07 as a third AEB was attached — only the PRE-fix side is pinned to the ticket's constant, the post-fix side is recomputed), and the ticket's production example **VO 8616-1 (R526-18) gives pre 1283,40 / post 1325,20**, i.e. the drifted and the booked numbers the ticket names, re-derived independently. 6 of 6 non-Blanko VOs match the post-fix engine and **0** match the pre-fix one (4 Blanko VOs skipped — duration pricing is a different branch, their passiv lines still checked by AC5). AC2: R526-18 1325,20, R426-57 1194,40 and the fresh draft R326-89 1118,51 all equal their VO total. AC5/AC9: all **10** linked attachments match `priceAt(code, insuranceType, date)` — the price-history resolver — including AEB-BV's date split (2 rows before 2026-07-01 at 47,69, 1 after at 49,65). AC9's catalogue: position **54003** (no longer shared with AEB's 54002) and the increase shipped as a dated **price-history row** written `2026-09-03T08:17:15` — between #3604's step 1 (08:09–08:10) and step 3 (08:25), exactly the prescribed order. AC6: the repro VO reads 10 units / 10 sessions / **0 remaining** with two AEBs attached on top. AC7 at scale: **1.203 VOs prescribe AEB but only 9 sessions carry one**. **Traps:** **`GET /activities/{id}` is 404** (no item Get, #3398) — date an attachment through its VO's `/activities?prescription=` collection or pay three timeouts a row; **`resolvedTariff` is a snapshot at attach time**, so a pricing fix only reaches NEW rows; **never price AEB-BV off `tariffGkv`** (it now reads 49,65 while two live rows correctly hold 47,69 — comparing to the column manufactures two false mismatches, the history is the rule); and `billingBatchCount` is **omitted, not zero**, on these VOs. **Cross-ticket:** this explains #3604's puzzle — R526-18 and R426-57 were the two invoices its AC-1 table predicted would move and which needed no correction; their VOs (8616-1, 7489-1) both carry an AEB, and this fix had already lifted their live totals to the booked amounts. **Findings** → `fixme`: (a) **two passiv rows carry no `prescription` link** (at=860781 fully dangling, at=860782 on the repro VO's FIRST session) and both price as **GKV on a PKV VO** — 41,46 instead of 41,80 — while all 10 linked rows price correctly and the sibling attached minutes later WITH a link got 41,80; that pairing points at the resolver reading insurance type via `ActivityTreatment.prescription` and defaulting to GKV when null, the "different code path that defaults to GKV" the Developer Reference flagged. 860782 IS billed (the VO total is 1035,25+41,46+41,80), so the ticket's own repro VO stays 0,34 short and its draft R326-89 carries it, with no repricing step in scope; (b) **AC1's figures are stale** — it predicts 1035,25 → 1077,05 for ONE AEB, but the fixture has since gained a second, so a QA reading the AC literally would call the higher total a failure; (c) the ticket says "regular AEB (position 54002, **43,16 EUR**)" but AEB is 41,46 GKV / 41,80 PRIVAT and 43,16 is in neither the catalogue nor the history. AC3 and AC9's export half → `fixme`: `optica-export` answers **422 on every pending batch** (#3288's standing readiness failure), so no position or price can be read from a real file even though the fixture (VO 7080-2, GKV Blanko, AEB-BV 49,65 dated 2026-07-17) exists. AC4 is thin by data: only 1 of the 10 VOs is on `/kpis/management/billing-backlog`, which only holds VOs 6+ weeks idle. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.manual-heilmittel.page.ts` (composing `sa.per-treatment-fee.page.ts`) |
| `sa_passiv_double_session.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @PassivDouble @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 (#3647) a passive Heilmittel (AEB/AEB-BV) attached to a **double** session must bill ONCE. #3603 made passive rows billable and #3602 made the doubling per-session; their intersection was wrong — `ActivityRevenueCalculator` applied the double-session multiplier to `TreatmentKind::PASSIVE` too, so an assessment on a doubled session counted twice in the VO total, the Optica quantity, the PKV invoice and KPI revenue. **Deployed between 2026-09-05 and 09-07, and this file has the before/after on BOTH halves** — only possible because the suite measured the same VO two days earlier for #3603: the API's VO 7080-2 `totalRevenue` went **2.888,80 → 2.839,15** and the client's `expandDoubleTreatmentRows` exclusion list went `['one_time_fee','per_treatment_fee']` → `[…,'passiv']`. The delta is **49,65**, the AEB-BV price — the whole ticket in one number. **The `.rz` export is the primary oracle, not a revenue model.** VO 7080-2 is Blanko, so its treatment lines price by DURATION on a separate branch; rather than port it, `solveTreatmentPrice()` solves the VO's own total for the per-session price under each rule — passive-once yields a clean **94,90**, passive-doubled an implausible 92,83. That reproduces AC1 and AC2 to the cent: 16 Jul = **112,87**, 17 Jul = **257,42** (pre-fix **307,07**), and the whole VO rebuilds to 2.839,15 over 23 countable sessions. **AC3 is read straight out of the file**, which states quantities outright: `rz2|…|03|54003|1,00|49,65|49,65|…` and daily lines on the doubled date reading **pos#01=2 (treatment doubled), pos#02=1 (fee once), pos#03=1 (passive once)**, with the `rze` trailer 474,27 equal to the positions' sum. **The correction that makes AC3 possible at all, and that other specs get wrong:** `GET /billing_batches/{id}/optica-export` **works on a `complete_and_sent` batch** (200, ISO-8859-1). Every earlier spec here records it as 422 on all batches — that was measured on **pending** batches only, where #3288's readiness check legitimately blocks it; the `fixme`s in `sa_manual_heilmittel_billing` and `sa_double_treatment_per_session` are too broad. **Traps:** decode the export as **ISO-8859-1** (a UTF-8 read mangles "Logopädie") and parse German decimals on the comma; `activity.doubleTreatment` arrives as `true`, as `false`, or omitted on the SAME VO, so only `=== true` is safe; and **enumerating passive-on-double via `activity_treatments → prescription` UNDERCOUNTS**, because that link is null on a growing share of rows — walk a candidate VO's activities instead. **Findings** → `fixme`: (a) new passive attachments are written with **no prescription link** — 2 of 12 rows on 2026-09-05, **5 of 14** on 09-07, including at=860943, the very AEB-BV that AC3 exports; billing still works (the export resolves through the activity) but any join through `prescription` silently drops them. **This also disproves the stated cause of #3603's finding**: at=860782 (orphan, GKV price on a PKV VO) looked like a null-link fallback, but at=860942 is also an orphan on the same PKV VO and prices correctly at 41,80 — so that mispricing is a stale pre-fix snapshot, not a null-link consequence; (b) AC4/AC5 have no fixture (both affected sessions are GKV, and neither VO is in the billing-backlog KPI). AC6 is data-gated with evidence: **no invoice on staging ever included a doubled passive line**. **A test-design note worth heeding:** AC6's first version asserted VO 1762-28's draft R326-89 had NOT been recalculated — it read 1.118,51 that morning and 1.160,31 by the afternoon. A draft is exactly what #3604 step 3 and #3589 refresh, so it can never evidence "not recalculated"; the same live-relationship-as-invariant mistake that broke #3603's AC2 the same day. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.passiv-double-session.page.ts` |
| `sa_deactivated_therapist_rows.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @DeactivatedTherapists @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3579) the Management board's traffic-light tiles must count **active therapists only**, and the Detail table must hide a deactivated therapist whose row carries no data — narrowing #3210 ("keep them visible, labeled (Inaktiv)") in those two places and nowhere else. **Deployed on staging (API `3.13.0`); all six ACs verified live, 9 passed.** **The filtering is CLIENT-side, which is what makes it testable end to end:** the API population is untouched, and the fix shipped as its own two-function module — `activeTileRows = rows => rows.filter(r => r.active)` and `visibleTableRows = rows => rows.filter(r => r.active || 'grau' !== r.bucket || 0 !== r.revenue || 0 !== r.validatedRevenue)` — wired as `useMemo(() => activeTileRows(t.rows))` → tiles and `useMemo(() => visibleTableRows(t.rows))` → table. `visibleTableRows` is the exact **De Morgan complement** of AC2's four-way hide condition, so the predicate is checked against the AC's own truth table as well as against data. **Why the placement matters:** `TrafficLightBuckets` is shared with the Therapeuten-Orga board and the Developer Reference warned that filtering inside it would silently change that board too. The helpers have **one call site each** (asserted by occurrence count = 2), so the TO board is untouched and **AC6 holds by construction** — both arrays derive from the same unfiltered `t.rows`, the KPI cards read a different hook, and the API still returns all 36 deactivated therapists carrying **1.099.455,95 EUR** of history. **THE TRAP:** `GET /kpis/management/therapists` with **no period params returns 122 rows of which ZERO are deactivated**, so a spec written against the default period passes whether or not the fix exists. The fixture is a historical window — `from=2025-01-01&to=2025-12-31` gives 125 rows with **36** deactivated, and the tiles move `rot 45 / gelb 32 / gruen 27 / grau 21` → **`34 / 27 / 19 / 9`**, a shrinkage of exactly 36. Staging holds **75 deactivated therapist accounts** of 239. **AC2 needs one more turn of the screw:** in any unfiltered window every deactivated therapist carries revenue, so none is grau+0+0 — but **narrowing by `patientType` splits the revenue** and leaves departed therapists at 0/0, hiding **6** rows under GKV and **11** under PKV (17 exercised live, each verified on all four conditions). The first version of this file carried a `fixme` claiming no fixture existed, written after checking only unfiltered windows; its own AC5 test disproved that, and the `fixme` was replaced with the live test. **Other notes:** `grau` means "no Personio hours", not "no activity" (#3580's relabel is also shipped — the tiles pass `labelKeys:{grau:'flowBoards.bucketGrauKeinePersonioStunden'}`), which is why AC2 needs all four conditions rather than the bucket alone; and a window spanning 2025→2026 can **504**, so keep windows to a year.  **Extended 2026-09-10 with the on-screen half, which is what QA actually looks at (15 tests).** The API tests above check the two predicates; these check the two SURFACES, and their fixture is a MONTH, because the board's stepper only walks Tag/Woche/Monat — **April 2026** (`MONTH_FIXTURE`) is the one reachable period where AC1 *and* AC2 both bite: 116 rows, 4 deactivated, **2 of them grau/€0/€0**. Verified live: the rendered tiles read **25/41/38/8/0**, exactly the active-only counts and NOT the all-rows **26/41/39/10** (a build without the fix would render the latter, so AC1 is falsifiable here); Celina Faßmann and Lena Aufderheide are absent from the Therapeut:innen view AND from Kerstin Müller's expanded team, while Jacqueline Kusche and Dominik Au stay, each painted `"<name> (Inaktiv)"`; **AC6 on screen with a margin** — "Umsatz (behandelt)" reads 1.007.888 €, the sum over ALL 116 rows and **8.924,64 € more** than the active-only sum, so the card demonstrably still carries deactivated history; and #3580's relabel on the same call site ("Grau — keine Personio-Stunden" on BOTH lines — `labelKeys` *and* `rangeKeys` are overridden, so `FlowBoardsPage.BUCKETS`'s "Grau — keine Aktivität" is correct for the Therapeuten-Orga board and **stale for this one**; `MANAGEMENT_BUCKET_LABELS` carries the current wording). **Two asymmetries measured rather than asserted, both pre-answered as by-design in PR #3584:** the tile count and the rows a bucket lists cannot agree (AC1 governs counts, AC3 rows) — **Rot: tile 25, rows 26; Grün: tile 38, rows 39** — and the team header's member count is a backend #3210 figure, so **Kerstin Müller reads 39 while the table paints 38**. **Three traps in reading the table:** `detailRowNames()` slices after the last column header, so on an unpainted table it returns the #3394 Ausfallzeiten card labels — a non-empty array of plausible names, which makes a `length > 0` readiness check pass on nothing and the assertions after it report a defect that is not there (poll for a known row instead); a deactivated row paints as ONE line with the "(Inaktiv)" suffix, so exact membership on the raw lines reports AC3 as failing when it holds (`plainNames()`); and stored names are not clean — `"Christina  Kirch"` has a double space that HTML collapses, so both sides need `normalizeName()`. **`HISTORY_WINDOW` narrowed to Q4 2025** (108 rows, 21 deactivated, tiles 28/36/33/11 → 17/29/32/9) — a cheaper read with the same coverage, but **not a cure for flakiness**: `/kpis/management/therapists` is unreliable under load, measured within one hour on 2026-09-10 going from 2.6 s on the default window to a 504, with a 2-month window 504ing in the same minute a 3-month one answered. When every window 504s the endpoint is unhealthy — re-run rather than triage the fixture. (Staging also went fully down mid-session: `/status` 502, `/auth` 500.) **Ticket state 2026-09-10:** still OPEN, milestone RC 3.13.0, PM comment 2026-09-09 records FT PASS / Ready for QA; PR #3584 merged 2026-09-01 and is on the staging build. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.deactivated-therapist-rows.page.ts` |
| `sa_working_hours_bucket_dot.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @WorkingHoursDot @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3575, PR #3606) a per-therapist traffic-light dot left of each name in the Therepeuten-Orga board's **Arbeitszeiten** table. Re-run the file when staging is healthy; nothing about it is known to be wrong. **Fixture reasoning:** the bucket map is period-independent, so only the ROW population moves with the period and that decides which colours appear. Unfiltered, the 122 rows carry three buckets (rot 115 / abwesend 6 / grau 1); **April 2026 is the best coverage — 116 rows, rot 103 / grau 6 / gruen 2 / abwesend 5, four of five.** Two AC cases have **no live fixture and neither is a defect**: `gelb` (**0 of 236** therapists are in it, in any period, so no yellow dot can render anywhere on staging — `fixme`'d, and the colour is pinned upstream by the PR's jest case) and AC5's un-bucketed therapist (the buckets endpoint returns every therapist, so the table's rows are always a subset) — AC5 is therefore produced honestly by `page.route`-ing the buckets response to an empty collection, which is the still-loading render the AC describes and sends nothing to the server. **Traps:** the testid is keyed by the ROW key, `bucket-dot-t<therapistId>`, while team rows key `g<teamId>`/`gnone` and are drawn by a different block (which is what makes AC4 structural); the **Management detail table's dot has no testid**, so AC2's parity comparison locates it by shape (10x10, 5px radius, first in the name cell) and must not assume a fixed depth because #3580 wraps the Grau dot in an `InfoTooltip`; and **read the dots only after a row has painted** — the section heading and both toggles render long before `working-hours` answers, and reading early yields zero dots, which looks exactly like the feature being absent (the first probe of this ticket did precisely that). The file therefore **captures the board's OWN responses** instead of re-issuing them (the #3471 technique): the payloads asserted are the ones the dots were drawn from, and it halves the exposure to those two slow endpoints. **Also on the record, from the PR's own notes:** the dot deliberately omits #3580's "keine Personio-Stunden" tooltip because this board still labels Grau "keine Aktivität" — whether the two boards should agree on what Grau means is an open product question; and a row with no bucket sits 18px left of its neighbours, which follows AC5's "no visual change" wording rather than reserving space. | `sa_to_board_active_tiles.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @ToBoardActiveTiles @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3705, commit `74b35a757`) the **Therapeuten-Orga** board's traffic-light tiles must count ACTIVE therapists only, with its Grau pill and tooltip reworded for this board's rolling window. #3579 did exactly this for the Management board and **deliberately stopped there** — its helpers were put in their own module with one call site each so the shared `TrafficLightBuckets` would not silently change the TO board too; #3705 is the follow-up that makes the same change on purpose. **Deployed; 6 tests pass, 0 `fixme`.** **Read-only.** The whole ticket is one asymmetry in the served bundle, and the spec pins it with a back-referenced regex so both arrays are proven to derive from the SAME source array rather than merely both existing: `S.rows.reduce((e,o)=>(e[o.therapistId]=o.bucket,e),{}),[S.rows]),N=(0,o.useMemo)(()=>(0,b.activeTileRows)(S.rows)` — the **tiles** count active therapists (AC1) while `bucketByTherapistId`, the map that colours the Arbeitszeiten dots (#3575) and drives the bucket filter, keeps **every** row (AC2), so a deactivated therapist still has a colour and stays reachable by filtering. `activeTileRows` occurrences go **2 → 3** (definition + Management + TO). **AC1 is falsifiable on screen, which is the point:** the payload is 236 rows = **162 active + 74 deactivated**, and the tiles paint `rot 121 / gelb 1 / gruen 1 / grau 31 / abwesend 8` against the all-rows `121 / 1 / 2 / 104 / 8` — Grün and Grau differ, so a build without the fix renders different numbers.
