The page object retries both the navigation and the login with growing waits: staging answered `ERR_CONNECTION_CLOSED` and `POST /auth` `ETIMEDOUT` during this run, and in a serial describe one such blip cascades every remaining test to "did not run", which reads far worse than the blip it is. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.to-board-active-tiles.page.ts` |
| `sa_patient_ik_bulk_link.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @PatientIkLink @ReadOnly @Slow` | RC 3.13 (#3560, PR #3586) the one-time bulk link of unlinked patients to their InsuranceProvider by IK (`app:link-patients-by-ik`, preview by default, `--apply` to write). 8,315 patients had no provider, which makes #2973's pre-export check block any GKV batch containing one of their VOs. **Applied on staging 2026-09-10 00:34 UTC; 9 tests pass, AC1/AC7 `fixme`.** **Read-only — every request a GET.** The command is console-only, so the file **re-derives its decisions** from the same three inputs it used and compares them to the run's own record: the PM's mapping file (fetched from the GitHub attachment and **sha256-pinned**, because every expectation is derived from it), the #2969 provider catalog, and each patient's current state. **The decision ORDER is load-bearing and ported exactly:** id-not-found → *already-linked (checked BEFORE the IK lookup)* → IK-not-in-catalog → link, so an already-linked patient with an unknown IK is reported as already-linked, not as an IK mismatch. Verified: the file is **6,295 rows / 6,295 distinct patients / 157 distinct IKs, all 9 digits, none blank**, sourced `rezepte` 4,445 + `patienten` 1,850 exactly as the ticket describes; **AC2 — all 5,876 linked patients carry exactly the provider their mapped IK names, 0 mismatches**, and "exactly once" holds literally (5,876 log entries over 5,876 **distinct** patients); **AC3** — every entry is `field_change` with `meta.field=insuranceProvider`, `meta.type=automatic`, `oldValue "null"`, `newValue` = the provider's **name**, **one run-wide batchId** (`6f0dc5a0…`) and **no author** (system), which matters because the bulk UPDATE bypasses the ORM listener so the command writes the entry itself; **AC5** — **157 of 157 file IKs resolve** against the 1,084-provider catalog and the file's informational `insurer_name` agrees with the catalog name for **every** IK (an independent check on the PM's file, which the command cannot make since it ignores that column), and every id the run could not find still **404s**; **AC6 — the sharpest evidence in the file: all 41 already-linked patients hold a provider that DIFFERS from what the file specifies** and every one survived, so the command demonstrably declined to overwrite rather than merely happening to agree; **AC4** — the log set is a strict subset of the file's patients (0 strays) and the PM's control patient 1 is still unlinked; **idempotency** — re-deriving against today's state leaves **0 to link, 5,917 already linked**, the client-side equivalent of the PM's re-run; **End Goal** — **5,917/5,917** mapped patients present on staging are linked, and a `@Slow` walk of the whole table (~170 pages) reports **5,930 linked / 2,440 unlinked with 0 of the unlinked in the mapping file**. **The ticket's 8,315 / 6,295 / 2,013 / 7 are PRODUCTION figures.** On staging the catalog covers every file IK, so the projected "7 unmatched IKs" is **0** and the unmatched report holds only "patient id not found" rows. **The off-by-one against the PM's recorded 379/40 is explained, not waved away:** patient **8484 "Naim PascualTest" was created 2026-09-10 05:21:59**, 4h47m after the run — it is in the file, so the run correctly called it not-found (379) and today it exists and is linked (378 + 41 = the same 6,295). Every count assertion is written against the log trail plus today's state, never against the PM's numbers. **Traps:** **`exists[insuranceProvider]` is accepted and silently IGNORED** (both `true` and `false` return all 8,370 patients), so counting the unlinked population needs a collection walk; **`?id[]=` DOES work** on `/patients` and batches of 50 are reliable at ~6.7 KB per patient (not #3373's 14 KB — that was with addresses); `insuranceProvider` is serialized as **`null`, not omitted**, unlike most nullable fields here; `/patient_logs` filters only by `patient` and `type`, so the run's entries are found by pulling `type=field_change` once (7,700 rows, ~4 MB) and narrowing on `meta.field` client-side; and **the mapping file needs an authenticated GitHub fetch** — a plain `curl` of the attachment 404s, so the page object uses `GITHUB_TOKEN` or `gh auth token` and the file-dependent tests gate rather than fail without one. A helper that swallows request failures will report **0 patients** and an oracle that agrees with nothing — the first attempt here did exactly that. **Observation for the PM** (not an AC): patient 8413's linked provider is Techniker Krankenkasse while its legacy free-text `insuranceCompany` still reads "Bahn - BKK" — the command trusts the IK by design, but the two fields now visibly disagree on some patients. AC1 (preview mode) and AC7 (the three report files) → `fixme`: console + S3 only, with the arithmetic corroboration recorded inline. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.patient-ik-link.page.ts` |
| `sa_personio_day_efficiency.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @PersonioDayEfficiency @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3666, commit `bc5da6887`) Effizienz and €/Stunde must count only days where Personio holds an entry, on the Management **and** Therapeuten-Orga Arbeitszeiten tables. Both figures divided the whole period's treated time by the Personio hours that exist, so a day with treatments and no entry inflated the numerator against an unchanged denominator — the ticket cites a production row at **229,4%**. **Deployed; 13 tests pass, 0 `fixme`.** **Read-only, and exactly verifiable rather than sampled:** the fix added the two qualifying-day numerators to `WorkingHoursRow`, so `GET /kpis/management/working-hours` serves **both scopes side by side** and the two candidate formulas separate from a single payload — `efficiency == personioDayTreatmentMinutes / personioMinutes` (shipped) vs `treatmentMinutes / personioMinutes` (old), `revenuePerHour == personioDayRevenue / (personioMinutes/60)` vs `revenue / …`. The denominator is arithmetically unchanged by design: a day with no entry contributes no Personio minutes either way. Verified **AC1 and AC2 at 106 rows matching the qualifying-day formula and 0 matching the whole-period one** (0 matching neither), with the correction always downward; **AC3** each of 4 team rows equals the **pooled** ratio (sum numerators / sum denominators, divided once) and **never** the average of member percentages — the two genuinely differ (81,84 vs 82,18; 69,47 vs 68,04; 73,93 vs 72,03), which is what makes the check discriminating; **AC4** on all gap rows `treatmentMinutes / personioMinutes` is *strictly greater* than the published Effizienz, i.e. the time columns kept the gap day; **AC6** with a live straddle — May 2026, **Haralampos Panagopoulos corrected 101,84% (no warning) against the old 151,68% (would have crossed the 115% arm)**; **AC8** 116 therapists on both boards with **0 disagreements**, which is a real assertion because the two tables are backed by different providers that each compute the ratio. Also: the KPI cards were corrected with the table (card 78,13 == pooled qualifying, against 79,09 whole-period), a therapist with no qualifying day reads **null, not 0** (10 rows, 5 carrying treated time), and the deliberately-unchanged trend is measured — **trend €/Stunde 96,1 against the card's 95,15** for the same month (`gesamt` carries no `efficiency` key at all). **THE FIXTURE TRAP:** the board's **default period is almost entirely non-discriminating — 2 of 122 rows**, against 22–27 of 116 in April 2026. A verification run there reads plausible Effizienz values that are *identical on either build*. **Second trap: the "Personio fehlt (N T.)" tag is NOT the set of corrected rows** — it stays whole-period (AC5) and counts *expected* working days with no entry, while the numerator drops *any* day carrying treatment without one: **9 of the 22 corrected rows read "(0 T.)"**, so finding fixtures via the tag (as the ticket's own reproduction step suggests) misses a third of them. **Third:** AC4 means `Behandlungszeit ÷ Personio-Stunden` no longer reproduces Effizienz, so checking that division and finding it "checks out" proves only that the row has no gap day. The tooltip key is **`flowBoards.effizienzPersonioTageTooltip`**, not the Developer Reference's suggested `effizienzPeriodRuleTooltip`. **RESOLVED 2026-09-14 — this file's finding became AC9 and AC9 has shipped (13 passed, 0 fixme).** The finding was: the Therapeuten-Orga legend still stated the formula this ticket removed — `flowBoards.toLegendEffizienz` = "Effizienz = Behandlungszeit ÷ Personio-Stunden", rendered by `TherapeutenOrgaLegend.tsx`, on the same board as the table it contradicted on 22 of 116 rows. The commit had been careful about precisely that invariant, rewriting the `TherapeutenOrgaKpiCards` comment which asserted it ("This comment previously asserted the opposite invariant") — but that is a code comment, and the user-facing legend was missed. The PM added **AC9** for it; the legend now reads **"Effizienz = Behandlungszeit ÷ Personio-Stunden (nur Tage mit Personio-Eintrag)"**, and the English value shipped too — `"Efficiency = treatment time ÷ Personio hours (only days with a Personio entry)"` — although the ticket's Localization Reference is German-only and no AC required it. The `FINDING` test is now an **AC9 regression test**, and two things about how it asserts are worth keeping: it matches the **formula half and the qualifier separately**, because AC9's literal text proposed a `/` divider while the shipped string correctly keeps `÷` (consistent with the sibling `toLegendDifferenz`), so asserting the AC's exact sentence would fail on a correct implementation; and it matches **escaped**, because the bundle stores `÷` as `\xf7` — a first check searched the `/` form, found 0 occurrences and read exactly like "the old wording is gone" (#3611's trap, in the direction that fakes a pass). **Observation on the FT evidence:** the PM's AC-1/AC-2 rows (0,0% / 0,2%) come from the default period, where 120 of 122 rows compute identically on either build, and their one arithmetic check divides the whole-period columns — which AC4 says should no longer reproduce Effizienz, and which appears to check out only because that row has no gap day. The fix IS live; that evidence would not have caught its absence. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.personio-day-efficiency.page.ts` |
| `sa_expiry_reason_german.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @ExpiryReason @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3651, commit `2eb764c8d` / PR #3654) an expired VO must state the deadline that actually fired (14 or 28 Tage), why it applied, and the dates, in German — for all six reason types across Physio, Ergo and Logo. The old text was English and, on the Ergo branch, hardcoded "28 days" even when the 14-day urgent/Berufsgenossenschaft deadline had fired. **Deployed since the first 3.13.0 staging deploy (2026-09-09 02:38 UTC); 8 tests pass, 2 `fixme`.** **Read-only.** `PrescriptionLog.meta.reason` (type `treatment_expired`) is the authoritative surface — the Änderungsprotokoll and the in-app notification both interpolate that same string verbatim, which is why the commit needed no frontend change, and why checking the string checks both. **THE TRAP: partition by date before asserting anything.** The ticket says existing entries are never rewritten, so "every expiry reason is German" fails on 1,157 historical rows it deliberately leaves alone. Verified as a partition instead: **43/43 post-cutover entries German, 0/1,157 historical entries rewritten** — which also guards audit history against a future translate-everything change. **The bug is detectable without knowing the rule**, because both eras' start-deadline text carries the day count AND the dates: the count must equal `Frist − Ausstellung`. That invariant passes on every post-fix entry and **fails on exactly the historical ones that carried the bug — VO 5691-6, 6390-3, 8673-3 (all urgent, all 2026-08-07), each announcing "28 days" over a 14-day span**, out of 321 historical entries that carry their own dates. A fourth, **VO 905302-3 (expired 2026-06-15)**, sits outside the 1,200-log window and was found via the accident population, so the bug hit the **Berufsgenossenschaft branch too**, not only the urgent one. AC2 is also cross-checked against each VO's own `urgentTreatmentNeed`/`insuranceType` (self-consistency alone could be consistently wrong), and the PM's screenshotted VO 965110-3 is matched against the localization table's exact standard wording. **The pre-fix population is worse than the ticket describes**, and the data shows it: of the newest 1,200 entries, **729 read a bare `Start deadline expired` with no number and no dates** (Physio/Logo) against 321 detailed Ergo ones — the Developer Reference asked whether all three areas had the "28 days" bug, and the answer is that two had no figures at all. **Finding — AC3 is unsatisfiable as written.** It names VO 5691-6 and asks its change log to show "14 Tagen" and "(dringender Behandlungsbedarf)"; its expiry was logged **2026-08-07**, a month before the fix shipped, and the ticket itself forbids rewriting old entries. The PM marked AC3 PASS but their Session-E evidence cites a **different** VO (965110-3 — physiotherapy, not urgent, a standard 28-day case), so the one case the ticket singles out was never re-checked. **Traps:** **`urgentTreatmentNeed` is NOT a registered filter** — `=true`, `=false` and `=bogus` all return the same 3,074 Aktiv VOs, so a candidate hunt built on it silently returns non-urgent VOs and reads as a fixture (it produced a bogus list here before being caught); `insuranceType` IS real (`accident` → 31 VOs); `urgentTreatmentNeed` is omitted when false, so it reads `undefined`; and the shipped qualifier separator is an **en dash** (` – `), not the ticket's proposed `--`. **Two `fixme`:** four of the six reason types (14-Tagen urgent, 14-Tagen BG, the Ergo/Blanko 16-Wochen rules, Behandlungsunterbrechungen) have **no post-fix instance** and none is imminent — no accident VO is Aktiv, never-started and past a 14-day deadline, and urgent candidates cannot be selected server-side at all; and the **Blanko 16-Wochen routing safeguard is not observable** — `ExpirePrescriptionCommand` used to route Blanko VOs to COMPLETED by substring-matching the English "16-week validity" (which translating the prose would have broken silently) and now routes on the enum, but there is no 16-week entry anywhere in the window to join a status to. AC5's two new strings are checked in the **deployed** dictionary and asserted to be referenced by the served bundle (`duplicate_col_duplikat_status` → "Duplikat-Status", `match_type_duplikat` → "VO im Duplikat-Prozess") — a translated string nothing reads is not shipped behaviour (#3337). `duplicate-check` stays `ROLE_ADMIN` (therapist 403). | `sa_ordering_status_column.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @OrderingStatusColumn @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3749, commits `5cbbf4ff6` app + `14bd9aff4` api, both on `release/3.14.0` under a `Ref` trailer with **no PR**) the Admin Board's **Bestell Status** column showed "—" for the two facility-driven ordering statuses and still opened an editor that could overwrite them. **Both halves deployed; 7 passed, 0 `fixme`.** **Two commits, two independent deploys, and `/status` answers for neither** — it gives the release not the commit (#3704) and nothing at all about the app bundle (#3705) — so each half is decided behaviourally. **The API half is a perfect dual oracle** because the two candidate orders are DISJOINT at both ends: the stock `OrderFilter` sorted lexicographically on the stored display strings (`By Admin → By Therapist → ER bestellt selbst → Praxis`), while `OrderingStatusOrderFilter` applies an explicit rank (`ER bestellt selbst → Praxis → Vom Admin → Von Therapeut`, the German labels alphabetically) — so **one page of one row settles it**, and it reads `ER bestellt selbst` asc / `By Therapist` desc on BOTH `/prescriptions` and `/v2/prescriptions`. **AC5 is then proven over the whole book in eight requests, not a walk:** `itemsPerPage=1&page=N` addresses a row by ordinal, so the four counts (By Admin 30,036 / By Therapist 3,701 / Praxis 1,150 / ER bestellt selbst 56) predict the exact block boundaries and every one lands — positions 1–56, 57–1206, 1207–31242, 31243–34943 — with the counts summing to the unfiltered **34,943**, which is also AC5's "nothing sorts as if it were empty" (there is no null to sink). A 500-row walk of the same thing times out; the ordinal probe takes ~2 s each. **The frontend half is read off the painted cell, deliberately not off the bundle:** the change is `const facilityDriven = …` plus `disabled={… || facilityDriven}`, both of which minify to nothing greppable, so a zero would mean nothing either way (#3602's lesson). Verified on screen as Super Admin: **99651-1 paints "Praxis" and 7381-4 paints "ER bestellt selbst"** — the ticket's own repro VOs, both re-read from the API first so a fixture whose status changed cannot report the board as broken — each with **no button, `cursor: auto`, no `aria-label`** (AC2: a read-only `StatusPill`, so there is nothing a later edit could re-enable), while `375-46`/`532-13` keep `button[aria-label="Status: Vom Admin"|"Status: Von Therapeut"]` with `cursor: pointer`. AC3: the editor opens and offers **exactly `["Vom Admin","Von Therapeut"]`** — #2614's rule, still holding, and the reason the display had to be fixed somewhere other than the options array — closed with Escape, nothing selected, and the stored value re-read afterwards. **AC4 needs a plain `ROLE_ADMIN` and that is where the run first lied:** `mintUiSession` replaces only the refresh token, and the project's saved `storageState` still carries the **Super Admin's** `{user:{roles}}`, which the app reads while `/me` is in flight — so the board rendered `isSuper`, every cell was editable and AC4 read as failing. `openBoardWithColumn()` now clears `auth-state` in an init script and the test **asserts the roles it is actually running with** before reading the lock; as a genuine admin, `5104-1` (By Admin + validated + Aktiv) is `aria-disabled="true"` with `cursor: default` and a **forced** click opens nothing, while the same admin gets a working editor on the unvalidated `375-46` and a read-only pill on `99651-1`. AC5 on screen: one header click puts `ER bestellt selbst` at the top and a second `Von Therapeut`, with all 30 painted cells inside the four labels. **TRAPS:** **`button[aria-label^="Status: "]` is NOT unique to this column** — Beh. Status, VO Status and Folge-VO Status use the same pattern, and a first probe grabbed `Status: Aktiv` from Beh. Status and concluded the editor showed the wrong value; every read here is scoped to the header's x-band. **The column sits at x≈2017 on a 1440px viewport**, inside the table's horizontally-scrolling port — reading works at any scroll offset but a click by coordinates silently does nothing, so go through a locator, which scrolls it in. **The editor is not a `role="menu"`** — it paints two plain leaves stacked under the cell, so it is found by position and must be POLLED for (reading straight after the press returns `[]`, which looks exactly like "the editor did not open"). **A locked cell must be clicked with `force`** — an ordinary click waits out its actionability timeout instead of answering the question, and a forced click is the stronger statement anyway. **The sort is a server round trip**, so the header click is wrapped in `settleAfter` — a fixed sleep returns the PREVIOUS ordering and reads as the sort doing nothing. **The column's rows are separated by zero-width leaves** that survive `trim()` and are truthy, so a bare `filter(Boolean)` keeps them and they read back as `""` strays. **Never `page.reload()` after `mintUiSession`** (single-use refresh token, #3460) — which is why the on-screen sort test does no search, the board's search box being reload-only to reset. **Cross-ticket:** the root cause is #3759's open hygiene ticket — `FollowupOrderingStatus` stores display strings as backing values, which is what made a lexicographic sort look plausible; the filter is keyed off `FollowupOrderingStatus::*->value` so it carries a rename automatically. **Read-only** — no ordering status is written; the one editor opened is dismissed with Escape. Uses `Pages/admin/admin.ordering-status-column.page.ts` |
| `sa_praxis_appointment_reminder.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @PraxisReminder @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.14 (#3640, commit `d278cbca5` / PR #3707, plus **`therapios/praxis#8`**) every practice appointment gets one reminder text at the practice's own lead time, with a cancel link. **Deployed on both halves; 10 passed, 1 `fixme`.** **This is the suite's first Praxis Flow ticket and the thing to know is that Praxis holds NO DATA OF ITS OWN.** `therapios/praxis` is a second front end over the same API — appointments, rooms, SMS consent, the message log and one-time links are all Doctrine entities in `api/` — so the whole of this ticket's state is readable from `api.staging.therapios.de` with an ordinary Flow token, and only AC4 needs the Praxis app itself (deployed separately at **`https://praxis-staging.curano.de`**, found in that repo's `deploy.yml`; the `.env.example`'s `staging-api.therapios.de` does not resolve). **Deployment is decided twice and neither time by `/status`:** the API half by the new serialized column `reminderLeadHours` (present on all 259 facilities), the Praxis half by the served `/login` chunks. **The Praxis login page ships BOTH translation dictionaries**, which is what makes AC4 verifiable with no Praxis credentials — all seven German/English pairs plus both sample reminder texts are asserted off the deployed build (#3337's technique on a different app). **The scheduler is STATELESS and that IS AC2:** nothing is scheduled at booking time, each hourly pass asks what is due *under the setting as it stands*, so AC2 is asserted structurally — **no `remind*`/`dueAt`/`scheduledFor` key exists anywhere on an appointment** — and arithmetically, by re-deriving the due set at 24 h and at 48 h and requiring the two to differ, plus a live `24 → 48 → 24` round trip on the test practice that leaves every already-sent reminder byte-identical. The pass runs on `cron(0 6-19 * * ? *)` and **its fingerprint is in the log**: a scheduler reminder is stamped at :01 past an hour, which is how one it sent is told from one a QA sent by hand. Verified: AC1's rule ported exactly (`startsAt <= now || remindAt > now` → not due; `bookedAt > remindAt` → booked inside the lead time; note the boundaries — starting exactly now is NOT due, booked exactly AT remind-at IS eligible) and both directions checked — **every reminder in the log was inside its appointment's due window when sent**, and the **catch-up invariant** (an appointment may only still be un-reminded if it entered its window after the last pass) holds with 0 misses; at most one reminder per appointment; the 08:00–20:00 Berlin window (sends measured at 08:01 and 16:01 Berlin, and the window is #3637's rule, not re-implemented); `Assert\Choice` refusing 36/0/12 with **422** and null with **400**, value unrestored-because-unwritten. **The fixtures are chosen so an absence has only ONE explanation**, which on this ticket is the whole difficulty — a patient without SMS consent produces no reminder whatever the rule does: AC1's "booked inside the lead time" row is demonstrated on appointments **16/17/20, all with consent**, and "a cancelled appointment is skipped" on **appointment 6**, which sat cancelled through its entire due window with a consented patient. **TRAPS:** **`/patients?smsConsentGranted=` is accepted and SILENTLY IGNORED** — `true`, `false` and unfiltered each answer all 8,401 rows — so consent can only be read per patient, and getting it wrong inverts the result (pinned by an assertion); **a blocked reminder writes NO history row by design** (`remind()` returns early on `blocked_no_consent`, because "reminder sent" against somebody never texted is the sentence a receptionist would read out), so the message log and the history are deliberately NOT the same set and a test requiring both on every message fails on a correct build; **`dropped_staging` is not blocked** and is the only status on staging that can write a history row; **de-duplication reads the MESSAGE LOG**, so any reminder row from any source suppresses the scheduled one forever; and **`date` + `startTime` are two columns meaning Berlin wall clock** (`2026-09-22T00:00:00+00:00` + `1970-01-01T08:20:00+00:00`), so reading either as UTC moves every appointment by the offset. **FINDING 1 — the default lead time is 24, the AC now says 48.** Management decided 48 on **16 Sep**; PR #3707 merged **15 Sep** and ships `DEFAULT_REMINDER_LEAD_HOURS = 24`, migration `Version20260915114322` writing 24 onto every row, and praxis#8's zod `REMINDER_LEAD_HOURS_DEFAULT = 24`. Measured live: **all 259 facilities read 24 h, none reads 48**. The PM's comment says "no action needed … unless the default lives somewhere that needs a separate change" — it lives in three places. **FINDING 2 — RESOLVED 2026-09-25, and the armed prediction is what caught it.** When written, AC1's history half had **ZERO `reminder_sent` rows across all 37 appointments**, and every candidate is accounted for, so it is a data gap rather than a defect: the two scheduler reminders were correctly blocked for consent, and the one consented appointment inside its window (appt 3) had its scheduled reminder suppressed by a hand-run #3637 probe already sitting in the log — which is also the concrete cost of log-based de-duplication. **The live prediction that was left armed has FIRED:** the logging test now reads **1 `reminder_sent` row — appointment 40, stamped 2026-09-21T12:01:52Z**, carrying `textMessage` and resolving in the message log exactly as the armed assertion required. The log now holds 7 reminders (2 not blocked, 5 `blocked_no_consent`) across 72 appointments, so the deliberate asymmetry the file documents — a blocked reminder writes NO history row — is now observable on real data rather than argued from code. AC3 → `fixme` with three measured walls: **`cancelled_via_link` occurs zero times** in the whole appointment history (all 6 cancellations are staff ones), the one-time link is only ever delivered in a text body and every staging send is dropped or blocked (`/patient_links` is 404 by design), and redeeming one is irreversible — PR #3707 ships no code for it ("AC3 needed no code at all"), it is #3638's redeemer covered by `PatientLinkPublicTest`. **Also for the PM:** the Testing Guidance says to use "a Practice Manager account for Praxis Rheinstraße", but **Praxis Rheinstraße (facility 104) has no `praxis_flow_access` row at all** — the three practices that do are 253, 254 and 206 — so that QA route cannot be followed as written. **Read-only apart from the AC2 round trip**, which moves facility 253 (`Praxis Flow Test Praxis (PM)`) and restores it in a `finally`. Uses `Pages/praxis/praxis.appointment-reminder.page.ts` |

| `sa_flow_boards_session_state.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @FlowBoardsSessionState @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3761, commit `dc0b292c6` on `release/3.14.0`, a `Ref` trailer with **no PR** — the same commit also ships #3762's Aktualisieren button) reloading Flow Boards in the same browser tab must reopen the board, the Gesellschaft and that board's filters, while a new tab starts on the defaults. **Deployed; 6 passed, 0 `fixme`.** **The whole feature is three sessionStorage keys**, which makes it both observable and seedable: `FlowBoardsScreen` swapped three `useState` calls for `useSessionState` (the `CRMScreen` pattern) — `flowBoards.selectedTab`, `flowBoards.selectedEntity` (the whole `EntityOption`, not its id) and `flowBoards.filtersByTab`. Frontend-only, so the served bundle is the only thing that can answer for deployment (`/status` is the API release, #3705): all three key names are in it, and **`useSessionState` writes on the FIRST MOUNT too**, so an untouched board already carries all three and the default slice is assertable field-for-field without a single click (`periodMode: periode`, `level: woche`, `teamMode/patientType/location: all`, the rest null, `periodAnchor` = today). **`filtersByTab` has exactly three keys** — `management`, `therapeutenOrga`, `adminPerformance` — which is the ticket's own table: Einrichtungen and Ärzte-Management own no slice, and that is the spec rather than an omission. **THE OBSTACLE IS THE TICKET'S OWN SUBJECT: this can only be tested by RELOADING, and `mintUiSession` re-injects its refresh token on every navigation** — the token is single-use, so the second load replays a spent one and lands on the login form (#3460). `seedSession()` installs the token **once per token** behind a `localStorage` marker, so the app's own rotated replacement survives the reload untouched; the marker is deliberately in localStorage, not sessionStorage, so it cannot disturb the storage under test. That helper is the reusable part of this file. Verified: **AC1/AC2** on the ticket's own recipe — Admin-Performance + Monat + `Curano Hamburg GmbH`, reloaded, with all three restored **in storage AND on screen** (level re-read through `activeLevel()`, the company off the header, and the board identified by its own content); **AC2's isolation** with two boards holding different levels at once (Management `tag`, Therapeuten-Orga `monat`, the untouched Admin-Performance still `woche`) surviving a reload and still separate when switched back — different values on two boards simultaneously is the only shape that shows the slices are separate rather than merely persisted; **AC3** in a fresh browser context, which IS a new tab (its own sessionStorage) — `selectedTab` and `selectedEntity` both null, **Woche**, **Alle Gesellschaften**, Management painted; **AC4** by seeding an unknown board key before the first navigation — it renders Management with **no error surface at all**; and a robustness case the commit's own unit test covers, here live: **corrupt JSON plus a partial slice** (`'{not json'` and a slice carrying only `level`) still opens the default board at the stored level, because `useSessionState` falls back on any parse throw and the context spreads the defaults UNDER the stored slice so a field added since is never undefined. **TRAPS:** **`selectGesellschaft()` opens the picker itself** — calling `openGesellschaft()` first leaves the list covering the trigger and the second open HANGS to the test timeout, since `actionTimeout` is 0 project-wide (that is exactly how the first probe here died at 300 s); **a board tab is NOT an `isSegmentActive()` segment** — all five report false whichever is open, so the active board is decided by a string only that board paints (`Privatpatient:innen` for Management, `Ø / Woche (letzte 4 Wo.)` for Admin-Performance) with the other's marker asserted ABSENT, so a half-rendered board cannot pass; and **`activeTab` is DERIVED and never written back**, so after AC4's fallback the stored value is still the unavailable board — AC4 has to be read off the screen, not out of storage. **AC4's permission variant has no fixture and the file says so:** every Flow-Boards account on staging is a Super Admin seeing all five tabs (an Admin gets no board at all, #3173), so an unknown key is used, which takes the same `visibleTabs.some(...)` branch. **Read-only on the server** — every request the board makes is a GET; what the file does change is its own tab's sessionStorage, which is the feature. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.flow-boards-session-state.page.ts` |
| `sa_to_overview_operativ_default.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @OperativDefault @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3770, commit `73d5801cc` / PR #3781) the Therapeuten-Orga board's working-hours table opens on the 5-column **Operativ** view, "Standard" is relabelled **"Details"**, and the section heading becomes **"Übersicht"**. **Deployed; all five ACs verified, 7 passed, 0 `fixme`.** **`GET /status` cannot answer for this ticket at all** — it reports the API release (`3.14.0` either side of the change) while the whole diff is `WorkingHoursTable.tsx` plus four dictionary values; the frontend bundle deploys independently (#3705), so deployment is decided from the served dictionary AND the columns the table paints, both asserted. **The i18n KEYS did not change, only their values** — `flowBoards.standard` now reads "Details" and `flowBoards.arbeitszeiten` reads "Übersicht"; a probe hunting for a `flowBoards.details` or `flowBoards.uebersicht` key finds nothing and concludes the rename never shipped (the commit says so outright). **The rename is SCOPED and that is asserted, not assumed:** on a ticket whose entire diff is four dictionary lines the realistic failure is a blanket find-and-replace, so two keys that legitimately still hold the old words are the control — `performanceDashboard.subTabs.workingHours` = "Arbeitszeiten" (the TO Verwaltung sub-tab) and `crm.lead_time.source_standard` = "Standard", and they are the ONLY two. **THE TRAP, which makes the AC's own wording unmatchable:** the headers are CSS-uppercased and **German ß uppercases to SS**, so `innerText` yields `MASSNAHME` — matching neither `/Maßnahme/` nor `/Maßnahme/i` (simple case folding does not equate ß with SS, #3718 hit it first) — while `textContent` keeps the shipped `Maßnahme`. | `sa_to_risk_grouping.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @RiskGrouping @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3774, commit `347274e1e`, a `Ref` trailer with **no PR**) the Therapeuten-Orga risk table groups by therapist for the billing-backlog view and gains its own CSV export; the Abrechnungs-Stau banner is retired from both boards and the Management board carries a plain summary line instead. **Deployed on both halves; 10 passed, 1 `fixme`.** **Deployment is decided twice and `/status` answers for neither** (release not commit — #3704; silent about the frontend — #3705): the API half by its two BRAND-NEW routes answering at all against a **404 control** (`GET /kpis/management/unbilled-summary` 200, `POST /kpis/orga/risks/export` 200, both `zzz-not-a-route` controls 404), the frontend half by what the board paints. **The oracle is the board's OWN payload, never the screen:** `GET /kpis/orga/risks` serves all **5,501 rows** carrying `tile`/`therapistName`/`teamName`/`revenue`, so `riskGroups.ts`'s ordering rule is ported in the page object and the screen compared against it — which is the only way AC3 is checkable at all, since the sort only shows itself across ~130 groups. Verified: **every painted group matches the payload exactly** on count AND value, painted in the oracle's order (133/133 on 2026-09-23, 136/136 on 09-25). **AC3's no-TO-Team block exists and the ticket names one of its members.** The Testing Guidance records staging as having no confirmed fixture and asks for one; it has **two (three by 2026-09-25, + Jenny Minge-Teteberg) — Frauke Wolff and Palle Spiekermann — and Palle Spiekermann is the very name the ticket's own wireframe uses** for that case, so the rule is exercised at both ends. **Sort with `localeCompare('de')`** or the umlauts (Schöner, Müller) file after Z and AC3 reads as failing. **The export contract is not quite AC5's wording:** `POST /kpis/orga/risks/export` takes `{filters, prescriptionIds}`, re-reads the provider for those filters and writes only ids it returns — **the client sets the ORDER and never the SCOPE**, asserted by sending a bogus id and watching it vanish (5 real + 1 bogus → 5 rows). It excludes every `duplikatOffen` ROW but not a VO that is also stale (9 Duplikat ids → 7 exported, exactly the 7 that carry a non-Duplikat row; a VO whose only row is Duplikat is never written). CSV header verified byte for byte, `;`-delimited with a UTF-8 BOM. **FINDING — AC5's two clauses conflict in the view the board OPENS in, and the PM's own QA step fails there.** With no tile selected the groups paint more rows than the CSV holds, because **some VOs appear under more than one tile** and the export deliberately writes each VO once. AC5 asks for "every row currently grouped … one row per VO, in the same order as the on-screen groups" — those cannot both hold, and the commit chose one-row-per-VO (the sane reading); but the Testing Guidance's "confirm the row count and therapist names match the on-screen groups" cannot be followed in the default state. **With the Fertig tile selected the two agree exactly**, so only the no-tile case diverges. **EVERY FIGURE ON THIS ROW DRIFTS DAILY AND IS DATED, NOT PINNED** — the Fertig tile read 1,179 on 2026-09-23 midday, **1,625** that evening once #3775 widened the population, and **1,649** on 09-25; the no-tile split went 5,492/4,167 → **5,949/4,596** (difference 1,325 → 1,353) and the groups 133 → **136** over the same two days. The file survives all of it because every assertion is an INVARIANT over one payload (the CSV equals the groups; the summary equals the tile to the cent) rather than a count — which is the whole reason to write it that way, and why the one test title that did hardcode "1,325" was corrected. AC7 verified twice: `unbilled-summary` equals the Fertig tile and the sum of its rows to the cent (two providers, one figure — not a tautology), and the Management board paints **"1.649 fertig behandelte VOs noch nicht in der Abrechnung · ≈ 931.859 € direkt abrechenbar"** (2026-09-25) with no chevron and no button role. AC6 on both boards. AC1's flat half on all three other tiles (0 groups, no export button) and on the **Admin-Performance board**, which shares the `RiskWorklist` component and is the realistic leak. **Traps:** `tiles.duplikatOffen` reports **8** while **9 rows** carry that tile (pre-existing; #3775 owns tile population), so the tile counts are not a safe row oracle; the group header and its subtotal are **separate leaves** (`"{count} VOs · {amount}"`), so a locator expecting both together finds nothing; the Heilmittel header reads **"HM"**, not the Localization Reference's "Heilmittel" (only the CSV uses the full word); **the board opens with NO tile selected**, which is itself one of AC1's two grouped cases; and the boards append **" (Inaktiv)"** to a deactivated therapist (#3210) while the payload carries the bare name — 8 groups, and a raw name key silently skips exactly those (a first pass reported "125 of 133 matched" without saying why). **A readiness lesson this file learned on itself:** the first `waitForRiskTable()` accepted "headers OR groups", the headers paint first, and `paintedGroups()` then returned `[]` — which reads exactly like the table not being grouped. It now takes `expectGroups`, waiting for the thing the caller is about to read; the flat tiles pass `false`, because zero groups is the assertion there and can never be waited for. **The same mistake was found and fixed in #3575's dot spec**, which waits on a therapist NAME and then reads DOTS — two different payloads (`working-hours` vs `efficiency-buckets`) — and failed with 0 dots on a build where a probe measured 102 moments later; `waitForDots()` added. **Fixed 2026-09-25:** the AC2/AC5 localization test asserted `flowBoards.duplikatVo` = "VO-Nr.", and that key is **gone from the deployed dictionary** — #3774 moved both boards onto the one shared `RiskWorklist`, so the VO column now renders from the shared `drilldownVoNumber` ("VO #"), which is why #3774's `GROUP_COLUMNS` records the header as "VO #". The test now asserts the retirement AND the shared key as a pair, so a future re-introduction of a duplikat-specific key is visible rather than silently giving one column two keys — the drift #3711 flagged. AC2's collapse/expand → `fixme`: the group header carries no testid, no `role="button"` and no `aria-expanded` (the RNW gap #3400/#3343/#3505 each recorded on this board family), and this is the slowest section on staging, so a click landing mid-repaint is swallowed and reads as "the toggle is broken" only under load; covered upstream by the commit's 409-line `riskGroupedView3774.test.tsx`. **`sa_flow_boards_billing_backlog.spec.ts` (#3180) is retired by this ticket** and was rewritten rather than deleted: its four banner tests are `fixme`'d (the banner is hidden, not removed, pending #3775) and a single green test now ASSERTS the banner's absence on both boards — because their old gate was `test.skip(banner.count() === 0, 'No billing backlog in the current filter scope')`, so a removed feature and a quiet data day produced the identical silent pass. **Run this file at `--workers=1` and expect to re-run it:** these are the slowest reads on staging and `unbilled-summary` answered **504** once mid-session while the route was demonstrably deployed — which failed the DEPLOYMENT gate and, in a `serial` describe, cascaded every remaining test to "did not run". A 5xx is the server being unhealthy, not the route being absent; only **404** means absent, which is what the 404 controls beside it are for. `status()`, `risks()` and `unbilledSummary()` all retry a 5xx with a widening pause rather than believing it. **THREE PINS IN THIS FILE WENT STALE ON 2026-09-23 when #3785/#3775 shipped in PR #3788 — all fixed here, none a defect.** `GROUP_COLUMNS` 8 → **10** (`Ausst. Datum` 3rd, `Beh. Status` 9th) and `CSV_HEADER` 8 → **11** (+ `Thema`): this ticket's AC4/AC5 govern the order of the columns THEY name, so those are now asserted as a **subsequence** with the full current set checked beside them (`ORIGINAL_GROUP_COLUMNS` / `ORIGINAL_CSV_HEADER`). And `flowBoards.abrechnungsStau` is **gone from the dictionary and the bundle entirely** (0 occurrences) — the planned sequel, since this ticket's Out of Scope deferred the deletion ("hidden, not removed… before that code is deleted") and #3788 is titled "…and retire the Abrechnungs-Stau code"; the two strings AC7 actually depends on (`backlogBannerText`, `backlogDirektAbrechenbar`) survive, and the assertion now pins the retirement instead. **The lesson, learned the slow way: after a ticket widens a table, grep the whole spec for sibling pins and RE-RUN — fixing the one assertion that failed is not the same as fixing the file** (three separate runs were needed to find all three). **Read-only** — every request a GET except the export POST, which writes nothing. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.to-risk-grouping.page.ts` |
| `sa_ready_for_billing_rule.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @ReadyForBilling @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3775, PR **#3788**, merged to `release/3.14.0` 2026-09-23T22:18Z) one shared ready-for-billing population — status ∈ {Fertig Behandelt, Abgebrochen, Abgelaufen}, ≥1 signed treatment, in no billing batch — behind the Orga risk tile, the working-hours "Fertig n. abger." column and the Admin-Performance "Bereit zur Abrechnung" tile, each keeping its own age setting. **Deployed; all six ACs verified on live data.** **The population change is measurable WITHOUT a fixture, because this suite measured the before:** `sa_to_risk_grouping.spec.ts` recorded `fertigNichtAbgerechnet = 1179` and `unbilled-summary = {1179, 815939.73}` at midday on 2026-09-23, hours before #3788 merged that evening; the same reads now answer **1625** and **{1625, 923960.98}**, and the tile's NEW `voStatus` field splits it **Fertig Behandelt 1182 / Abgebrochen 165 / Abgelaufen 278** — the two added statuses arriving together. `voStatus` is the deployment probe, not the count, which could drift for other reasons. **AC6's boundary is demonstrated on the live population rather than a built fixture:** across all 1,625 tile rows `min(daysSince) = 31`, with **0 rows at exactly 30 and 1 at 31** — both of the AC's boundary lines exercised, the second by a single specimen. **TWO TRAPS, and either one alone makes AC5 read as failing.** (a) **Comparing the three TILE TOTALS is not AC5** — 1,625 vs 2,266 is a *therapist population* difference: five **`ZZPerf-*` load-test therapists** carry **587** ready VOs on Admin-Performance and appear on the Orga board not at all (`active: true`, `isTestAccount: false`, so #3182's exclusion misses them; synthetic "ZZPerf Entity …" Gesellschaften with no working-hours data). AC5 is scoped *"for any one therapist"*, and **per therapist the sets agree exactly, 0 in either direction**. (b) **PKV must be excluded first and AC5 says so** — AC6's last row is the reason: a PKV VO with an active invoice counts on the Orga surfaces and NOT on Admin-Performance, **9 live instances, all `Fertig Behandelt`, all `isPrivate`**; comparing with PKV in yields nine spurious disagreements that are the specified behaviour. **A THIRD TRAP, and it is a field that does not exist where you look for it:** the signed-treatment gate must be read from a DIFFERENT field per surface. `activityCount` and `totalTreatments` are #3774's grouped-view columns — populated on every Orga row and **null on all 2,266 Admin-Performance rows** — so one shared check scores that surface as "none of them has a signed treatment" and reports AC1 failing on a correct build (it did here before being understood). Both surfaces DO carry `daysSince`, which is derived FROM the last signed treatment, so a VO without one could not have a value: that is the gate's observable consequence where the count is not exposed. Also verified: AC1's billing clause (`billingBatchCount` 0 across a 25-VO sample spanning all three statuses); AC2's differing age settings (Admin-Performance carries rows at/under 30 days, the Orga tile none); AC3's exception intact and NOT extended to the Orga surfaces; AC5's third surface — `completedUnbilledOver30Count` equals the risk tile therapist-for-therapist, and `completedUnbilledCount` equals the Admin-Performance set **plus** the PKV VOs rule 3 excludes (that figure is one number per therapist and cannot be narrowed by insurance type, so the comparison adds them back rather than pretending it can). **For the PM — the ticket's own Developer Reference query was wrong, twice, and the dev caught it in review** (`5d4e0ea51`): the supplied signed-treatment predicate was **not NULL-safe**, so legacy activities with unset rejection flags (**46,391** in the dev dump) read as "no signed treatment" and their VOs counted nowhere; and `DateInterval::days` is unsigned, so a **future-dated** treatment read as that many days OLD and was wrongly flagged "> 30 T". Both now go through one `ReadyForBillingVoSql::SIGNED_TREATMENT` constant shared with #3774's export and the Duplikat billing deadline, so "last treatment" is one date everywhere. Anyone implementing from that reference query would have shipped both defects. **Also observed here: #3724 shipped in the same window** — the rows now carry **`patientName`** with full names where they carried `patientInitials` on 2026-09-23, so `sa_flow_boards_patient_names.spec.ts`'s gated ACs should now run. AC4 (cache-version bumps, `orga_risks` → v6 etc.) → `fixme`: only falsifiable in the minutes AFTER a deploy, and any TTL had long expired by the time this file first read the endpoints; recorded that the first post-deploy read already returned the new population on all three surfaces. **Read-only.** Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.ready-for-billing-rule.page.ts` |

Status` 9th; **AC4** 150/150 issue dates in DD.MM.YYYY; **AC3** sessions done equals `activityCount / totalTreatments` on 150/150 and **`n / BV` on 40/40 Blanko rows**; **AC5** the download's eleven columns in the ticket's order, and — the AC's own stated worry — **"Fertig seit (Tage)" is empty on 0 of 150 rows including all 26 cancelled and 19 expired**, where before it was filled only for finished VOs; **AC6** all three statuses present in the Admin-Performance population, every painted Topic one of the three. **TRAPS:** **`Beh. | `sa_quarter_change_forecast.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @QuarterForecast @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3773, commit `17a654290` on `release/3.14.0`, cherry-picked to `main` as `9002b2f78`) a one-off READ-ONLY console report (`app:report:quarter-change-order-forecast`) listing every VO that would become ready to order between 05.10. and 11.10.2026, delivered to the PM by 29 September. **The rule is verified; the run has not happened yet — 8 passed, 3 `fixme`.** **No API surface at all**, so neither the run nor its CSV is reachable — and the ticket's own Testing Guidance says what to do instead (*"Staging data differs from production, so validate the formula itself here rather than the exact VO list"*), so `OrderReadyDateForecaster` is ported and re-derived over live VOs. **THE FAITHFULNESS CHECK THAT COULD HAVE GONE EITHER WAY, and passes:** the forecaster's Blanko path does NOT call `evaluate()` — it re-applies two gates by hand and then the 91-day rule, which is exactly where a divergence would hide. Reading `evaluate()` settles it: its order is `followupStatus` → `orderingStatus` → **`isBlankoVO()`** → treatment status → frequency, so the Blanko branch is reached BEFORE the treatment-status gate and replicating only the first two is correct; the missing third gate is asserted as absent rather than assumed. **The Blanko arm is verifiable END TO END** because every input it reads is serialized: issue dates **06.07.–12.07.2026 map exactly onto ready dates 05.10.–11.10.2026** (`PrescriptionIntervalEnum::TO_ORDER = 91`, asserted at both ends), giving a complete **9-row** staging forecast with all four AC2 columns populated per row (`VO-Nummer` / `Patient` "Nachname, Vorname" / `Praxis` / `Bestellen ab`). The `max($readyDate, $today)` arm is pinned too — a VO long past its 91 days reports as due TODAY, which the command then drops for being before the window, and it is the one line that could otherwise emit a past date. **The frequency arm is NOT client-derivable: `decisionFrequencyPerWeek` is in no serialization group** and the VO's own `actualFrequency` is served as an **empty object `{}`** — so it is driven as a pure function at its boundaries (worked by hand: lead 21 / freq 2 / remaining 7 ⇒ k=4; remaining 6 is already due at k=0 so the loop's first k wins; a longer lead time triggers EARLIER, which is #3298's intent) with its live inputs checked instead — all practice lead times inside #3298's 10–30 clamp. **AC4 is the sharpest claim and is checkable without the command.** It asks for production's rules, not RC 3.14's, and #3719 is the only 3.14 change here. Two independent facts: the forecaster calls `evaluate()` with `allowWednesdayPullForward` left at its default `false`, and #3719 changes only WHICH DAYS the pull-forward reaches, never the threshold. **A first version of this test was a tautology** — it compared the shared threshold function to itself — and was rewritten to drive #3719's actual before/after `pullForwardDays()` across a fortnight, show they genuinely DISAGREE on some days, and show the forecast is identical on all of them because it applies neither. **TRAPS, and the first would silently forecast the entire book:** **`blankoVO` is SILENTLY IGNORED as a filter** and so is `exists[followupStatus]` — `true`, `false` and a nonsense control all return the same **34,952** rows, so the Blanko population is reached through the ISSUE-DATE window (the only filter that narrows: 453) and filtered client-side; **`date[before]` is inclusive** (#3712), which is what this window needs; **`followupStatus` is omitted when null** (#3302) so the gate reads `undefined`; and **a 500-row page 504s** on this window (measured: two retries, 2.3 min, then failure) — the walk pages at 100 and asserts it collected `totalItems`, so a truncated read can never masquerade as a short forecast. **TWO BUGS THIS FILE FOUND IN ITSELF, both worth carrying:** (a) **`FollowupOrderingStatus`'s backing values are not what the case names suggest** — `BY_ADMIN = 'By Admin'` and `BY_THERAPIST = 'By Therapist'` but **`BY_PRAXIS = 'Praxis'`** and **`BY_ER = 'ER bestellt selbst'`** (#3749 records the same inconsistency as open hygiene ticket #3759). A first version used `['By Praxis','By ER']`, which matches nothing the API serves, so the gate never fired and the forecast kept VOs the report must skip — **the corrected gate cut the staging Blanko forecast from 9 rows to 7**. The gate test still PASSED while wrong, because it drove this file's own constants through this file's own port: a closed loop. It is now closed against LIVE values (every served ordering status must be one of the four, and a blocked one must actually occur in the window). (b) AC4's probe must be one **both** #3719 rules can reach, or they never fire and never disagree and the comparison is vacuous: remaining 9 needs k=11, past both rules' maxDays (4 and 5); remaining 7 needs k=4 and is within both, so each fires on its own run day. The anti-vacuity guard (`disagreements > 0`) caught that — keep it. **Observation for the PM:** the candidate query is `treatmentStatus NOT IN (:skipStatuses)`, and **no NULL satisfies a SQL `NOT IN`**, so a VO carrying no treatment status is dropped silently rather than considered — the same shape as #3731's `IN` finding. Measured and reported, not asserted either way. **Deployment is not client-decidable** (a console command has no surface; `/status` gives a release not a commit — #3704). On the record: the dev flagged the prerequisite themselves ("the command has to be in the production image before the run… it is not yet deployed") and the cherry-pick to `main` landed 2026-09-23 01:28 UTC. The production API host was not reachable from here, so production's own version was not measured. Three `fixme`, each with its own wall: the run/CSV/delivery (console + S3 only, **and the run day — 28/29 September — has not arrived**, with the post-run QA checklist recorded inline); AC4 against the real production image (needs both branches' services in one tree — the dev did this locally and got byte-identical CSVs, 2,168 candidates / 336 rows / `--today=2026-09-29`, recorded with the caveat that it was their data); and the frequency arm on real VOs. **Read-only** — every request a GET. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.quarter-change-forecast.page.ts` |

These are Article 9 surfaces, so verifying them means showing the door is shut, never walking through it: no patient name, roster row or file byte is read or logged. **THE TRAP THAT PRODUCED A FALSE FINDING before it was understood:** **#3545 needs FOUR query parameters and returns `[]` BEFORE its gate if any is missing.** `ReportsDataProvider` reads `startDate`, `endDate`, `elderlyCareHome`, `department` and early-returns on the first absent one — so an incomplete request answers **200 with zero rows and the access check never runs**. Verified live: **#3546** self 200 / colleague 403 / **naming nobody 403** (the grouping service would answer with every VO in the system) / `shared=` gets the identical rule / admin 200 — the shipped rule is self+admins, the recorded decision named a *board-viewer permission that does not exist in code* and the dev noted the narrowing on the issue. **#3545** therapist allowed at all 4 facilities where they hold an active VO (3/139/234/262) and denied at 208/222 **and at a nonexistent id** (which must be refused, not 404'd, or the gate leaks existence); admin overrides; the refusal states the rule and names no patient. **#3547** `/prescription_images` admin 17,108 vs therapist **71**, `/documents` 1,702 vs **91** — the assertion is the NARROWING, since equal totals would mean the extension never ran; item read and `signed-url` both **404 for the therapist while the admin gets 200**, which is what makes the 404 mean *denied* rather than *gone*. **#3552** all seven signed routes: unsigned+authenticated **403** (the guard is the controller's first statement, so the answer is 403 whether or not the path exists), anonymous 401, **forged `_hash` 403**; the eighth, `/prescription_export/download`, takes no signature by design and is gated in-body — admin 404 (past the gate, file absent), therapist 403. All four also have their decision recorded on the issue and their `authorization-matrix.md` rows updated. **MORE TRAPS:** **#3552's path is a URL SEGMENT** (`/document/download/{path}`, `requirements: ['path' => '.+']`) — a `?path=` probe answers 404 and reads like the route being gone, when the guard was never reached; **#3547 denies with 404, NOT 403**, deliberately, so a test expecting 403 calls a working gate broken; and **a 504 must never be scored as a denial** on these, the slowest endpoints on staging — `status()` retries a 5xx. **#3692** verified from the repo (needs an authenticated `gh`; the tests skip rather than fail without one, because an absent file and a 404 are indistinguishable): one shared `scripts/gates/secret-scan.sh` runs gitleaks `--staged --redact`, **exits 1 when gitleaks is absent (fails closed)**, is called as gate **1/6** by `api/bin/pre-commit` (first on purpose — the only gate there protecting something a later commit cannot undo) and by both `lint.yml` and `ci-selfhosted.yml`; `.gitleaksignore` records the 2026-09-19 one-off scan (**4 findings, all false positives, 0 live credentials**) with a justification comment above every fingerprint. **FINDING (#3736) — shipped as OPTIONAL, so the ticket's own Verify step does not hold.** The AC says "2FA is enforced for all admin roles" and Verify says "an admin account without a completed enrolment cannot reach an authenticated endpoint"; measured, a **`ROLE_SUPER_ADMIN` with `twoFactorEnabled: false` reaches `/prescriptions`, `/users` and `/patients` (200)**. Not a defect — the recorded product decision of 2026-09-19 was TOTP optional for everyone at launch, with enforcement as phase 2 of `docs/security/mfa-rollout.md`, **proposed 2026-11-16 and explicitly not committed**, needing a follow-up ticket that does not exist yet. The ACs and the rollout doc disagree and only the PM can close that. Deployment IS verified: all five 2FA routes answer **405** on GET (POST-only, registered) against a **404** control, and `twoFactorEnabled` is the only one of the five new user columns in a serialization group. Two `fixme`: #3692's Verify (staging a synthetic AWS key needs a **monorepo checkout**, which this repo is not — the exact commands are recorded inline) and #3736's end-to-end flows (**enrolling would lock every spec in this suite out of staging**, undoable only by a super-admin reset, and Android needs a device; the commit ships 17 RN tests). **Never enrol 2FA on a shared QA account.** Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.tech-dd-p1-security.page.ts` |
| `sa_session_double_marker.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @SessionDoubleMarker @Security @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3711, commit `871ab6cc1` on `release/3.14.0`, a `Ref` trailer with **no PR**) every list of individual sessions must mark a **Doppelbehandlung** and show its doubled minutes. **Deployed on both halves; 8 passed, 0 `fixme`.** **Two of the five surfaces the ticket names did not exist and were built by the commit** — the Patient Management session table and the Berichte session export — both reading a brand-new admin-only **`GET /prescriptions/session-report`** (`TreatmentSessionRow` + `SessionReportProvider`), so that resource answering at all is the API half's deployment probe, which `/status` could not give (release, not commit — #3704). **The row is shaped so no client can get the arithmetic wrong, and the file asserts both halves of that contract:** `durationMinutes` is `Activity::getCalculatedTreatmentDuration()` **verbatim** — already doubled for a legacy (V1) Doppel VO, already NOT doubled for a V2 one, whose doubling lives in `ActivityTreatment::quantity` — while `doubleTreatment` is the **marker, not the arithmetic**, true for V1 and V2 alike so both render the same tag though their minutes legitimately differ. **AC1/AC2/AC3 are verified against the flag the therapist actually set**, session by session: for three mixed V1 VOs the report's marker equals `/activities`'s per-session `doubleTreatment` on every row, and — the assertion that gives "doubled" content — **the doubled rows are exactly twice the single rows of the SAME VO with the SAME Heilmittel** (3568-9 3×40 vs 2×20; 1281-16 2×40 vs 9×20; 1503-25 1×40 vs 10×20). AC4 is then checked at scale over a whole Berichte selection rather than on VOs chosen for having a double — **232 sessions, 70 double / 162 single, every row carrying a real boolean and a non-null duration**, with the window required to contain both kinds or the negative half would be vacuous. **AC2 is also verified ON SCREEN** on the one fixture where the contrast fits a single page: **patient 3019 holds exactly 13 sessions, all on VO 4056-1, twelve single at 30 Min and one double at 60 — and the double is the tenth by date**, so the painted table shows nine plain rows and one carrying `Doppelbehandlung ×2 · 60 Min` with `60 Min` in the Dauer column; a final test compares **all 10 painted rows against the API row for the same date, 0 mismatches**, which is the only thing that could silently drift between the new endpoint and the new screen. Access is asserted too: the resource and the operation both carry `ROLE_ADMIN` and a therapist gets **403 "Only Admins can read the session report."** (Article 9 health data, one row per session). **TRAPS:** **the tag is CSS-uppercased** — `DoubleTreatmentTag` sets `textTransform: 'uppercase'`, so `innerText` reads `DOPPELBEHANDLUNG ×2 · 60 MIN` while `textContent` keeps the real string (#3718), and both readings are asserted; **the `×` is U+00D7, not the letter x** — the ticket writes "Doppelbehandlung x2" and the shipped string is `Doppelbehandlung ×2` (#3666's `÷`); **the new keys are NOT where the Localization Reference implies** — they live at `common.double_treatment_x2*`, `patients.form.session*` and `reports.session_export.*`, and a first probe looking under `controls.*`/`patient_management.*` reported every one of them missing, so the dictionary is searched by VALUE (#3337); **`activity.doubleTreatment` is `true`, `false` or OMITTED on the same VO** (#3602), so only `=== true` is safe when building the expectation; **the endpoint FAILS CLOSED** — no filter, a partial Berichte selection and an unknown parameter all return **0 rows**, which is the opposite of `/prescriptions`, where an unregistered filter is ignored and the whole book comes back; and **the session table has no testids**, so rows are gathered by **vertical overlap with their date cell, never by a rounded y band** — the chip has its own padding and sits a few pixels off its row's baseline, so a band key files it as a row of its own and the test reports "no row is tagged" on a build where the tag is right there (that is exactly how this file first failed); the LAST row also needs an explicit floor of one row pitch, or it swallows the app's nav chrome and reports "▾ | Berichte" as its VO and therapist. **FINDING** — reported as a green test: the Testing Guidance says to **reuse** the existing tag component and translation key from the Flow Boards calendar, but the commit added a **second** key, `common.double_treatment_x2`, holding the identical German while `therapist_board.double_treatment_x2` is left in place and still used there — two keys, one string, two surfaces, free to drift. Defensible per the component's own docblock (the calendar's is a dashed BOX wrapping two ActivityCards, this one an inline chip) but a divergence from the ticket's guidance and a PM decision. **Also recorded:** the V2 "marker yes, ×2 no" branch is **unexercised on staging** — every reachable double VO is the legacy V1 shape, consistent with #3602's finding of 0 V2 double VOs in a 1,400-VO sample and its two named V2 fixtures not existing; and the commit deliberately leaves `Activity::getDuration()` returning the undoubled activity-level duration when one is set, with a measured blast radius of zero and its own ticket. **Read-only** — every request a GET; the screen half only navigates and reads, with ONE `goto` because `mintUiSession`'s refresh token is single-use (#3460). Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.session-double-marker.page.ts` |
| `sa_blanko_flat_fee_cutoff.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @BlankoFlatFee @ReadOnly @Slow` | RC 3.14 (#3712, commit `520f4d2f8` on `release/3.14.0`, a `Ref` trailer with **no PR**) a Blanko VO issued on or after **30.07.2026** no longer receives the **VBP-BV** one-time flat fee when its first treatment is documented; VOs issued earlier keep it and nothing is corrected retroactively. **8 passed, 1 `fixme`. The rule, the code list and the whole live population are verified; the gate itself has never been reachable on staging, and the ticket's own QA step cannot detect that.** The rule is one predicate — `code ∈ {VBP-BV, VBP-BV-P} && issueDate != null && issueDate >= '2026-07-30'` — living in `App\Util\BlankoFlatFeeCutoff` and read by four call sites (`ActivityFeeService` for the attach, `ActivityRevenueCalculator` and both arms of `RawKpiCalculator` for the money, `PopulateActivityTreatmentCommand` for the re-attach). It is ported here and driven at both boundaries: **29.07 keeps the fee (AC1), 30.07 and 15.09 do not (AC2/AC3)**, a **null issue date is never suppressed** (absent data must not silently remove a charge), and every other one-time fee survives every date. **The Developer Reference left one question open and the answer is a test:** it names only `VBP-BV-P` and asks whether area-specific variants exist — the catalogue holds **exactly two** VBP one-time fees, `VBP-BV-P` (PT, 58,63) and `VBP-BV` (ERGO, 98,59), both `bv: true`, **no Logo/SSSST variant**, and the shipped `CODES` names both; a sweep over all 141 treatments pins that nothing VBP-shaped sits outside the gated list. **The gate is narrow in the other direction too**, which has real content rather than being about non-Blanko fees: `AB-E-BV`, `AB-P-BV` and `BD-BV` are themselves Blanko one-time fees and are deliberately untouched. **AC2/AC3/AC4 are asserted at full scale, not on an example VO:** `?treatment=` is a registered filter on `/activity_treatments`, so the ENTIRE attachment population comes out in a few requests (#3603's technique) — **1,694 VBP attachments across 1,692 VOs, intersected with the 152 VOs issued on/after the cutoff = 0**. **AC4 is measured rather than assumed to be absent:** the ticket's "7 Blanko VOs already carry the fee incorrectly" is a **production** figure from a 15.09 snapshot and staging has none, so there is nothing here whose survival could be checked — stated explicitly so a future reader does not misread that zero as the fix having deleted them. AC1's population is whole: **2,068 VOs prescribe a VBP fee and every one was issued before the cutoff** (newest 15.07.2026 / 14.07.2026), so the gate currently suppresses nothing on staging. **A live positive control keeps "no VBP fee" from being vacuous:** post-cutoff Blanko VOs **965111-1 / 965112-1 / 965506-1** each carry their OTHER one-time fee (`AB-P-BV` / `AB-E-BV`) exactly once, so one-time-fee attachment demonstrably still works after the cutoff and only VBP is gated. **FINDING — the ticket's PM/QA step is a guaranteed false pass.** It says to create a Blanko VO dated 30 Jul or later, document its first treatment and confirm the breakdown has no VBP-BV. But the gate is only ever reached through `Prescription::getOneTimeFees()`, i.e. the VO's PRESCRIBED treatments, and **all 14 post-cutoff Blanko VOs on staging prescribe no VBP fee at all** — three of them created on 17.09.2026, the day the fix shipped. So the breakdown is empty on a fixed build and on an unfixed one. **The ticket's premise is false on staging for the same reason:** it states "Every Blanko VO automatically carries the VBP-BV flat fee", yet **22 of 61 PT-Blanko VOs do not prescribe it, including ones issued from 01.06.2026** — carrying the fee is a property of what the TheOrg import put on the VO, not of being Blanko. **TRAPS:** **`bv` is absent from the prescription-embedded treatment** — `/treatments` serves it (41 of 141) and so does the `ActivityTreatment` embed, but `prescribedTreatments[].treatment` carries only `@id/@type/code/description/tariff*`, so "is this VO Blanko?" read there is silently always false and a first pass reported **0** post-cutoff Blanko VOs where there are 14; resolve `bv` from the catalogue by code. **`/activity_treatments` registers only `activity`, `treatment` and `prescription`** — no date filter (an attachment is dated through its activity, #3603) and **`order[id]` is accepted and IGNORED**, asc and desc returning the identical page (#3449's shape), both pinned by assertions. **`date[before]` and `date[after]` are both INCLUSIVE** — `after` being `>=` happens to match the cutoff's own semantics exactly, but the complement needs `strictly_before` or the boundary day is counted twice; and an **unknown filter is silently ignored** (`zzzNotAFilter=1` returns all 34,943), so the date filter is proven to tile the book before any zero from it is believed. **A 1000-row page on `/activity_treatments` hangs up under load** — the walk uses 500 with a retry, since without it a healthy build reports as a failing one. **Observations for the PM:** the commit is **wider than the ticket** — it changes the revenue engines too, so on a post-cutoff VO the fee now bills only if its `ActivityTreatment` row exists (which is what preserves AC4 for the 7, and what makes deleting the row the way to stop billing them); VO **8799-1** carries **three** `VBP-BV` rows for a once-per-VO fee (pre-cutoff, so out of scope, but a data anomaly); and VO **4840-1** has a mistyped issue date of **4202-07-22**, which a plain date comparison treats as post-cutoff for good. AC2/AC3 live → `fixme`: reaching the gate needs a post-cutoff VO that actually prescribes a VBP fee, which means writing one into a real VO's prescribed treatments and then documenting a session — a write whose whole purpose is to create a fee row, with no client-reachable undo for the Activity. Steps, including the control that makes a pass mean something, are in `docs/manual-test-3712-blanko-flat-fee-cutoff.md`. **Read-only** — every request a GET. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.blanko-flat-fee-cutoff.page.ts` |
| `sa_ordering_pull_forward_day.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @OrderPullForward @ReadOnly @Slow` | RC 3.14 (#3719, commit `c5adde88f`, PR #3720) the ordering pull-forward moves from the Wednesday run to the **Tuesday** run so the admin team's Wednesday round actually includes it, and the window widens by one day with the base. **Merged; the arithmetic is verified exhaustively, the live outcome is not observable on staging. 6 passed, 2 `fixme`.** **Deployment is NOT client-decidable for this ticket, and the file says so rather than guessing:** the change is two private constants on `TransitionToOrderService` read only inside a console command — nothing is serialized and no endpoint evaluates the rule, so unlike #3709 (whose job inputs happened to be exposed as `treatmentStartDeadline`/`validityDate`) there is no behavioural probe, and `/status` gives the release not the commit (#3704). **The whole behaviour change is ONE SET WIDENING BY ONE DAY**, so both rules are ported and compared **as weekday sets** rather than through example VOs: before = run Wednesday, k=1..4 → `Thu Fri Sat Sun`; after = run Tuesday, k=1..5 → `Wed Thu Fri Sat Sun`. That states AC2 (**gained exactly `["Wednesday"]`**), AC4 (nothing lost) and AC3 (Monday and Tuesday are **6 and 7 days out**, outside the cap in both rules) directly. **AC1 is asserted twice over:** the ported rule fires on **Tuesday and no other weekday** (all seven driven), and — because AC1 is a claim about Berlin business hours — the unchanged cron `cron(15 20 * * ? *)` lands at **21:15 Berlin in winter and 22:15 in summer, still Tuesday on both**; a later cron would roll the Berlin date into Wednesday and reintroduce exactly #3299's defect. Option B was chosen, so the schedule and its chain are untouched (`RecalculateOrderingMetrics` 19:15 → `TransitionOrder` 20:15 → `TrackingTransition` 21:00). **The #3298 threshold is untouched and that is asserted**, not assumed: `remaining <= ((lead + k) / 7) × freq` is identical in both versions, so a VO's projected trigger day does not move — only which of those days get pulled. Driven across `remaining` 13–22 at lead 14 / freq 7, the two rules agree on every k they share and differ only at **k=5** (`remaining=19` → Sunday, reachable from Tuesday, not from Wednesday). AC5's floor is ported exactly, including the two edges a rewrite gets wrong: it compares the issue date against **TODAY, not the target day** (right, since the flip happens today), and a VO with **no** issue date is not blocked (`null !== issueDate && …`). **FINDING** → reported: **the marker and the German reason are still Wednesday-named** — the flip stamps `meta.wednesday_pull_forward` and the reason reads "— Mittwochs-Vorzug: regulär fällig am …", so the change log a QA reads on a **Tuesday** evening says *Mittwochs*; defensible (the pull-forward is still *for* the Wednesday round, and the key keeps log history continuous) but the ticket's own QA step is to read that log. **Why the live half cannot be checked:** re-measured every run — **0 moves into `order` in the 3,000 most recent `follow_up_status_change` rows**, automatic or manual, and **0 rows have ever carried the marker**; that is #3299's standing finding unchanged, so neither the old Wednesday run nor the new Tuesday run leaves a trace here. A second, smaller obstacle: **`decisionFrequencyPerWeek` is not serialized** (the VO exposes `actualFrequency`, which the calculator may average differently), so a fixture whose trigger is exactly k days out cannot be picked exactly from the API. Staging context: all 200 sampled practices are **21d/Standard** (#3302's finding, unchanged), so every VO is evaluated at the same lead time. A guard is left armed for the day the job does run — any row carrying the marker must be stamped on a Tuesday. **Read-only.** Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.ordering-pull-forward.page.ts` |
| `sa_reminder_letter_branding.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @ReminderBranding @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3714, commit `a371c19f2`, PR #3726) a Zahlungserinnerung must print the entity's branding as it is TODAY, not the snapshot frozen on the invoice it chases; the invoice PDF keeps its own. **Deployed; AC1, AC2 and AC4 verified live. 6 passed, 1 `fixme`.** **The whole ticket is ONE COMPARISON and it needs no mutation:** the same invoice has two documents — the stored PDF (frozen) and the reminder (live) — and on staging every entity is already `isRebranded: true` while the older invoices froze Therapios, so the two must now DISAGREE. That disagreement is AC1, AC2 and AC4 in a single measurement. Nothing is written: a reminder is rendered on demand and never stored (#3559 — the controller renders and returns, no persist), and the invoice download serves the stored file (#3495). Measured on **4 of 4** eligible overdue PKV invoices: reminder `info@curano.de` / `www.curano.de` / banner / `“Hamburg, 20.09.2026”` against invoice `info@therapios.de` / `www.therapios.de` / no banner / `“16.06.2026”`. **FOUR independent markers per document, because `DocumentBrandingResolver::resolve()` sets each template variable separately** — a build that fixed the email but not the date line would pass a single-marker check. The one worth naming is `letter_date`: Curano renders **“City, DD.MM.YYYY”** and Therapios a bare **“DD.MM.YYYY”**, pure formatting and the easiest to miss. **AC4 is the control that stops AC1 being satisfied by “everything is Curano now”** — all 4 invoice PDFs still print Therapios on every marker. **Traps:** `pdfText()` drops a line of this letter (#3559) — use `pdfDocText()`; **a naive date-line regex swallows the preceding sentence** (“…zahlen Sie nichts. Hamburg, 20.09.2026” reads as a 28-character city), so `cityDateLine()` requires a capitalised word immediately before the comma; and **the branding snapshots are serialized in NO group** — not `billing:read`, not `invoice-list:read` — so “what did this invoice freeze?” is unanswerable from the API and is read from the invoice's own PDF instead. **FINDING** → reported as a green test: **only BRANDING went live.** `entity_name`, address, IBAN and BIC still come from `getIssuerNameSnapshot()`, deliberately, so the banking block keeps matching the invoice being chased — but the visible result is a reminder headed **“Therapios Hamburg 1 GmbH”** whose body carries the Curano banner and `info@curano.de`. Scope-compliant (“only the branding source”), yet AC1 reads “the letter shows Curano branding, not the Therapios branding the invoice was created with”, and a QA applying that literally will see the old company name at the top and file it. AC3 (banner currently OFF) → `fixme`: `rebrand_banner_enabled` resolves through `FeatureFlagService`, **Unleash is not provisioned in any deployed environment**, and the value arrives as the ECS env var `REBRAND_BANNER_ENABLED` — **staging ON, production OFF** by design — so producing that state is an ops change, not a test step; all five plausible client surfaces answer **404** (`/feature_flags`, `/settings`, `/system_settings`, `/configurations`, `/app_settings`), re-measured every run so the `fixme` cannot go stale. AC3's RENDERING half IS verified: the invoice documents pass `show_rebrand_banner: false` through the same resolver and template and print no banner. **Fixed 2026-09-25:** the deployment test asserted `isRebranded` over EVERY entity row and broke when #3775's load-test fixtures added two synthetic **`ZZPerf Entity …`** Gesellschaften carrying `isRebranded: false`. They issue no invoices, so they cannot weaken the comparison — but a global assertion cannot say that, and failed on a build where the ticket was still correct. The precondition is now scoped to the entities that actually ISSUE invoices, which is the property the comparison depends on; same class as #3775's own trap, where the ZZPerf rows make two tile totals look like a rule mismatch. **Read-only.** Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.reminder-branding.page.ts` |
| `sa_working_hours_frozen_column.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @FrozenNameColumn @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3718, commit `efde53f00`, PR #3727) the therapist-name column on the Therapeuten-Orga board's **Arbeitszeiten** table must stay frozen while the other ~16 columns scroll, with a visible divider. **Deployed; all three ACs verified on screen in both view modes. 7 passed, 0 `fixme`.** **The whole ticket is a computed style plus a geometry measurement** — there is no API surface (`WorkingHoursTable.tsx` and nothing else), so deployment comes off the served bundle and behaviour off the rendered page; `/status` cannot answer for a frontend change at all (#3705), let alone a CSS one. The shipped style, verbatim: `{position:'sticky',left:0,zIndex:2,backgroundColor:e,borderRightWidth:1,borderRightColor:…}`. **The behavioural assertion is invariance BETWEEN scroll offsets, and getting that wrong fails on a correct build:** `left: 0` pins the cell to the SCROLLER's edge while the row carries 16px of padding, so it sits at x=57 unscrolled and snaps to **x=41** on the first scroll, then holds — measured at 293/587/880px of scroll the name is **41, 41, 41** while a data column travels **587px for 587px of scroll**.
