An "x unchanged from rest" assertion would report a defect that is not there. **FOUR TRAPS, two of which read as "the feature is missing":** (a) **two testid prefixes, not one** — the commit adds `working-hours-name-<rowKey>` to DATA rows and `working-hours-name-header` to the header, but the TEAM rows in Gruppen view are pinned by the same style under their pre-existing **`team-toggle-g<teamId>`** id, so a locator on the `working-hours-name-` prefix finds exactly ONE element in Gruppen view and reads as "the table has no rows" (the first probe here concluded exactly that, and a first draft of the Gruppen test then asserted `testid === null` and failed the same way) — every cell is located by its **computed `position: sticky`** instead, which is also AC1's own property so locator and assertion ask the same question; (b) **the default view is Gruppen**, so polling for `working-hours-name-t…` never resolves until the view is switched — readiness is the HEADER testid, present in both; (c) **AC2's divider is a BORDER, not a shadow** — the Developer Reference proposed `V2TableSticky`'s `boxShadow` seam and what shipped is `borderRightWidth: 1` deliberately (every other separator in this table is a border, and a border sits inside the cell's width so nothing shifts), so a probe written from the reference finds no box-shadow and calls AC2 failed; (d) the column headers are `text-transform: uppercase` and **German ß uppercases to SS**, so `innerText` yields `MASSNAHME` — matching neither `/Maßnahme/` nor `/Maßnahme/i`, because simple case folding does not equate ß with SS — read headers as **`textContent`**. Also: the bundle writes `position:'sticky'` with SINGLE quotes, so a `position:"sticky"` grep returns **0** (pinned by an assertion so nobody re-derives a false negative). Verified: **118 pinned cells** in Therapeut:innen view, every one `position: sticky` / `left: 0px` / `zIndex: 2` / `border-right 1px rgb(233,233,233)` and **opaque** (the commit passes a background per call site so the columns sliding beneath cannot show through the name — header `rgb(249,249,249)`, data rows `rgb(255,255,255)`); **6 in Gruppen view** (header + 5 team toggles) with the same geometry invariance; **exactly one pinned cell per row and it is always index 0**, which is the regression guard for "does NOT change the table's other ~16 columns"; and AC3's end state — scrolled fully right to `Maßnahme`, **117 therapist names are still painted** and the name column is still on screen. The header is pinned horizontally only (`top: auto`), which the ticket scopes out ("out of scope unless it comes for free") — reported, not asserted. **Read-only** — opens the board, toggles a view, scrolls a table. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.working-hours-frozen-column.page.ts` |
| `sa_privat_basis_check_set.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @PrivatBasisCheckSet @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3708, commit `28f402375`, migration `Version20260916070000`) Privat Basis VOs must be validated with the **GKV** check set instead of the PKV one #3608 gave them, minus eleven checks that do not apply to a Basistarif patient. **DEPLOYED 2026-09-17 and every AC verified — 9 passed, 1 `fixme`.** **The whole ticket is ONE SERVED COLUMN:** `GET /validations` exposes `applicableInsuranceTypes` per check (51 checks, one request), so AC1, AC3 and AC5 are all statements about that column — no sampled VO, no screen-scraping required. **The migration's rule is arithmetic, which makes the post-deploy target exact:** it appends `privat_basis` to every row carrying `public`, `WHERE description NOT IN (…11 names…)`. Measured before the deploy: **44 rows carry `public`, 15 `private`, 14 `privat_basis`, 6 disabled (empty list)**, and **all 14 Privat Basis rows are inside the 44** — a containment the spec asserts BEFORE using the number, because without it `public − excluded` is not a superset and the target silently under-counts. So after the migration Privat Basis must hold **exactly 44 − 11 = 33** — and the deployed build serves exactly that: **33 = 15 `vo_creation` + 17 `billing` + 1 `not_needed`**, up from 14 (6 + 8). **The verdict is reached three independent ways, before and after** — the registry, the create form's own endpoint (`preview-creation-validation`, transient, writes nothing: Privat Basis went 6 → **15** creation checks against GKV's 18), and the **Validierungskonfiguration screen** at `/validation-config`, whose Privat Basis column went 14 → **32 ✓**. `/status` reported `3.14.0` both before and after and could never settle it (#3704), which is exactly why all three probes are behavioural. **AC3 is verified as a MAPPING, which nothing else states:** the AC names five categories and the migration names eleven descriptions, so `EXCLUDED_BY_CATEGORY` pins the correspondence (copayment/exemption 3, 9-month 1, Blanko/LHB/BVB 4, discharge 2, group-switch 1 = 11) and each is asserted to exist, to be off for Privat Basis, **and to be a GKV check** — an "exclusion" GKV does not have either would be a migration no-op and a sign the list had drifted (0 such). **AC4 is a real assertion, not "a column is present":** the screen is display-only and rendered from the registry, so its tick count must equal the registry's — Privat Basis **14 ✓ == 14**, GKV Standard **40 ✓ == 40**. **THE TRAP in that comparison, and it reads as a 4-row product bug:** the GKV columns are split by **VO KIND** (Standard / LHB / BVB / Blanko), so "GKV Standard" is `public` AND `standard` — three checks are kind-specific (`blank_prescription_deadline`, `lhb_icd_in_list`, `bvb_icd_or_approval`) and correctly show ✗ there — and the screen has exactly two tables, so a `not_needed` check (`no_overlapping_prescriptions`) is rendered nowhere; 44 → 41 → 40. PKV, Privat Basis and UV/BG each get one column, so no kind filter applies to them. **Findings** → reported: (a) **AC2's severity half cannot fail, and saying so is the honest version of it** — `severity` is a property of the CHECK, one column per row with no per-insurance-type override (payload shape pinned by a test), so a check applying to both types necessarily carries the same severity and message. AC2's substance is verified instead by running **the same transient VO under both insurance types**: 15 shared checks, **0 verdict disagreements, 7 of them FAILING for Privat Basis** — which matters because a set where nothing failed would satisfy the equality trivially and prove nothing about AC2's wording ("a Privat Basis VO that FAILS a GKV-specific check"). The only creation checks GKV still runs and Privat Basis does not are ids 14/25/39, all AC3 exclusions. (b) **this ticket incidentally closed #3576's open finding** — `fee_area_matches_therapy_type` (id 54) was the one PKV creation check #3608 never gave `privat_basis`; it carries `public` and is not on the exclusion list, so the migration added it and **id 54 now applies to Privat Basis**; (c) the screen's **"Zuletzt aktualisiert: 2026-06-22"** predates both #3608 and this ticket, so a QA checking "did the screen update" by that stamp would conclude nothing changed. **Also for the PM:** AC5 calls PKV "the 11 shared checks" but the registry serves **15** — #3608's legacy wording; what AC5 actually requires is that the set does not CHANGE, which is structural, since the migration matches on `public` and never on `private`. **Traps:** `/validations` needs `itemsPerPage` raised or the default page hides most of the 51; six rows carry an EMPTY `applicableInsuranceTypes` (disabled) and must not read as "missing from Privat Basis"; the config screen has no per-cell testid, so columns are read by x-band and y-order. AC2's **billing** half → `fixme`: the 17 billing-timing checks have no preview endpoint, so demonstrating one failing needs a real Privat Basis VO in a failing state — cheap for a PM (4946-8 and 7551-9 are already past their start deadline with no treatment, which is what check 11 fires on), write-heavy for this suite. **Read-only.** Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.privat-basis-check-set.page.ts` |
| `sa_discharge_expiry.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @DischargeExpiry @ReadOnly @Slow` | RC 3.14 (#3800, `c79958e3b` / PR #3813) a **Entlassmanagement** VO must expire when treatment has not started by **day 7**, or once the **12-day** window after issue is over — in German, naming the deadline that fired and its dates. **Deployed; all 8 ACs verified on two real nightly runs. 10 passed, 0 `fixme`.** **Read-only** — the job is console-only (`app:prescription:expire`, 20:02 UTC), so the file reads what the runs left behind, which turns out to be *better* evidence than a manufactured run. **THE SHARPEST RESULT, and no single run could produce it: the boundary is strictly AFTER the closing day.** VOs **99664-1** (day 7 = 2026-09-25) and **99665-1** (window end = 2026-09-25) were **not** expired by the 09-25 run — which expired 15 other discharge VOs that same night — and **were** expired by the 09-26 run. That separates `> boundary` from `>= boundary`; a spec written against one run can only ever guess. (It also means the PM's AC-2 row, recording 99664-1 as still Aktiv on 25 Sep, was right that day and is stale now — fixtures here are re-classified against today's date, never against the PM's.) **Deployment is decided from behaviour, twice, because `/status` reports `3.14.0` either side of the commit** (the release, not the commit — #3704): (a) the `Entlassmanagement:` reason exists in the log at all (**17 entries**, all `automatic` with **no author**, 8 day-7 + 9 window, earliest **2026-09-25**) while **0 of the 900+ older entries in the same tail** carry it — a PARTITION, since historical entries are never rewritten (#3651); and (b) a discharge VO serves `treatmentStartDeadline` = issue+7 and `validityDate` = issue+12 against the non-discharge control **99680-1** at **+28**, whose validity is measured from its FIRST TREATMENT rather than from issue — the other half of what makes the discharge window distinctive, and why the reason says *nach Ausstellung*. **Careful with (b): that DISPLAY change is #3830, not this ticket.** `4b1722ce7`/`a394b0af8` (both `Ref #3830`) moved the presenter onto a shared `DischargeWindow`; #3800's own Out of Scope excluded touching Startfrist/Gültig bis. So the served fields probe the window DEFINITION and the log probes the EXPIRY — recorded in the spec so the two are not conflated. **TWO SILENT-IGNORE TRAPS, and either one alone makes the fix look absent.** (1) **`isDischargeManagement` is serialized on the VO but is NOT a registered filter** — `=true`, `=false` and a bogus key all return the unfiltered **35,018**, with `date[after]` as the control proving the collection *can* be narrowed. The population is therefore DERIVED: only the discharge rule puts the start deadline exactly 7 days after issue (standard 28, urgent/BG 14), and that detector is validated rather than assumed — over a page-capped walk of 2,500 VOs it selects **7, and all 7 carry the flag**. (2) **`order[id]` is silently ignored on `/prescription_logs`** — asc and desc return byte-identical pages and the collection is id-ascending, so a `order[id]=desc&itemsPerPage=300` "newest" scan returns the **OLDEST** N: 3,600 rows of 2025 history carrying **zero** discharge reasons, which reads exactly like the job never having run. That dead end cost a pass here; `expiryLogTail()` pages from `totalItems` instead, and a test pins the ignored order so nobody re-derives the false negative. Verified: **AC1/AC3** both reason texts matched on their PARTS in order (the AC quotes prose; #3666/#3668) with the **#3651 self-consistency invariant** on all 17 — the announced day count equals `boundary − Ausstellung`, the printed Ausstellung equals the VO's own date, and the printed boundary equals `issue + 7|12` — **0 mismatches**; **AC1's discriminating case**, VO **99667-1**, the only fixture whose treatment *exists* but came **late** (09-24 against a 09-23 deadline) and still expired as `start7`, where every other day-7 expiry has no treatment at all and "not started in time" is indistinguishable from "never started"; **AC4** as a truth table off the served deadlines — `public` and `privat_basis` **apply** (+7/+12, and Privat Basis is genuinely exercised: 99669-1 expired), `private`/PKV carries **neither deadline** (excluded from expiry entirely, #3709 — pre-existing, but it is what AC4's PKV row observes), `accident`/BG keeps **+14** and no window, an unset type keeps **+28** — plus **0 of the 17 expiries on a non-applying type**; **AC6** all 17 landed on **Abgelaufen**, never Fertig Behandelt; **AC5** with an unusually clean control — **99675-1** is Fertig Behandelt with the **same issue date and window as four VOs the same run expired** (99666-1/99668-1/99673-1/99679-1) and was left untouched, so being skipped is the rule at work rather than a quiet miss. **The backlog half is the part that shows it mattered:** five REAL VOs — **9988999-5, 9489-1, 9594-1, 9612-1, 9612-2** — had been sitting open since April–July, **59 to 138 days** past their 12-day windows, and the fix's first run took all five; today **0** discharge VOs are open past their window. Finally the whole rule is **ported and re-derived** against every expiry the job made (**17 of 17 agree**, both on whether to expire and on WHICH reason), which is what would catch a rule expiring the right VOs for the wrong reason. **Traps in the fixtures themselves:** a **planned or rejected** session is not a start (99674-1 carries one activity and still expired as `start7`); `urgentTreatmentNeed` is omitted when false (#3709); and `date[after]`/`date[before]` are both **inclusive** (#3712). Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.discharge-expiry.page.ts` |
| `sa_discharge_dates.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @DischargeDates @ReadOnly @Slow` | RC 3.14 (#3830, `4b1722ce7` api + `a394b0af8` app, both `Ref`-trailered with **no PR**) a **Entlassmanagement** VO must SHOW day 7 as its start deadline and day 12 as its valid-until date on every screen, and the existing expiring warnings must follow those dates. **Deployed on both halves; every AC verified. 10 passed, 0 `fixme`.** The display counterpart to #3800, which made the nightly check close the same VOs on the same two days; it supersedes two items of #3800's Out of Scope list. **Read-only** — every request a GET except the transient creation-validation preview, which writes nothing (#3576). **`/status` answers for neither half** (the release not the commit — #3704; silent about the frontend — #3705), so each is decided behaviourally: the API by the two SERIALIZED fields reading issue+7 / issue+12 against the non-discharge control **99680-1** at +28 (mandatory — both fields are legitimately null on many VOs, so "+7/+12" means nothing on its own), the app by the bundle carrying **`start_deadline_warning:['issueDate','insuranceType','isDischargeManagement']`** — the THIRD entry is the fix, and the array survives minification as an object literal of string keys. **AC8 is the rare AC that states its own before AND after, so it is checked against the ticket's own arithmetic:** 99699-1 reads **04.10.2026 / 09.10.2026** and 99700-1 **27.09.2026 / 02.10.2026**, both exactly as predicted, while the BG (99671-1, +14) and no-insurance-type (99672-1, +28) rows are untouched although both ARE marked Entlassmanagement — so the marker alone is not what moves the dates. **99700-1 is the sharper fixture:** its validity was 22.12.2026 under the ordinary Physio rule (three months from its first session), so the discharge branch overrides a rule that was producing a perfectly plausible value ~81 days later. **THE TEST-DESIGN MISTAKE, and it is the transferable lesson: AC2's four rows are NOT mutually exclusive.** They read *no session / first session by day 7 / first session after day 7 / VO closed*, and a single-label classifier that checks "closed" first files **12 of 14 fixtures under `closed`** and leaves row 3 empty — failing as "AC2 row 3 has no fixture" when 99667-1 is right there. The fix is two INDEPENDENT axes (a session state, plus `isClosed` as its own predicate), each required to occur. Whenever an AC table's rows can co-occur, one bucket per row is wrong. All four rows then have live fixtures, **including the boundary** — 99665-1's first session is ON day 7 and 99667-1's on day 8, and both show the same dates — and AC1's own note is asserted as a CONTRAST rather than a claim about one VO: a discharge VO with no session shows day 12 where the non-discharge **99683-1** with no session shows nothing at all, because the ordinary Physio validity only starts at the first treatment. **AC6 is driven read-only through the create form's own endpoint** (`POST /prescriptions/preview-creation-validation`, transient), which accepts `date` and `isDischargeManagement` — so the boundary is exercised exactly: 7 days back **passes**, 8 days back **fails** with the exact note *"Die Startfrist ist abgelaufen. Die VO kann automatisch verfallen."*, and the DISCRIMINATOR that makes it falsifiable is that at 8 days back a **non**-discharge VO still passes, same day and same insurance type, with the ordinary boundary intact (28 passes, 29 fails). **`/validations` is keyed by `description`, NOT `code`** — there is no `code` field, so a lookup by it silently finds nothing, every verdict reads `undefined` and the whole table prints as inapplicable (that is how this test first failed); `start_deadline_warning` is **id 47** (`vo_creation`) and `treatment_start_deadline` **id 11** (`billing`). **AC7 is verified end to end AND is caused by this ticket:** 99700-1 (valid 4 days out) IS on the Therapeuten-Orga `laeuftAb` tile and 99699-1 (11 days out) is not, and under the old rule 99700-1's validity was ~85 days out so it could not have appeared before — the warnings were deliberately unchanged and follow `validityDate` for free. AC3 as a truth table (GKV + Privat Basis get the new dates; PKV carries neither, excluded from expiry entirely per #3709; BG keeps +14; no type keeps +28; a VO not marked keeps +28). AC4 over the walked population — **28 in-scope discharge VOs, 26 physio + 2 ergo, and 8 with `urgentTreatmentNeed: true` all reading day 7**, which is AC4's hardest clause (the ordinary rule has its own 14-day urgent branch, so "day 7 wins" means the discharge branch is reached first) — plus **99673-1**, a Blanko discharge VO at +7/+12, Blanko being its own ordinary validity branch. **AC5 on both screens the ticket screenshots:** the VO form's *Wichtige Termine* panel reads `Behandlungsstartfrist = 27.09.2026, Gültigkeitsdatum = 02.10.2026`, and the T Board deadlines cell reads `04.10.2026 09.10.2026` for 99699-1 and **`Begonnen 02.10.2026`** for 99700-1 — which is also AC2's closing note (a screen that shows *Begonnen* in place of the start deadline keeps doing so), asserted rather than assumed, with the pre-fix `25.10.2026` / `22.12.2026` asserted ABSENT. **TWO TRAPS IN READING THE BOARD:** the VO number lives in the **frozen rail** (`v2-rail-cell-prescriptionId`) and **`v2-cell-prescriptionId` does not exist**, so a lookup there finds nothing and reads exactly like the fixture being absent from the board; and only ~12 rows of a 130-VO caseload are painted, so the fixture must be narrowed with the board's own SEARCH — never `page.reload()`, since `mintUiSession`'s refresh token is single-use (#3460). **The day meaning differs from the ordinary rules by one day, on purpose** (the ordinary rules expire ON the shown date, #3800's rule the day after), so day 7 and day 12 are the last valid days and must equal #3800's printed `Frist`/`Ende` — a spec that "aligns" the two off by one reports a defect that is not there. **AC9 → reported as a green FINDING rather than asserted, with the wall measured:** the billing check must keep the ordinary 28/14-day deadline until #3808 via `getOrdinaryTreatmentStartDeadline()`, which is deliberately **not serialized**; the only read-only surface is a stored verdict, the two discriminating VOs (**99667-1** first session day 8, **9489-1** day 11 — late under the discharge rule, fine under the ordinary one) carry **0** verdicts, and the five that do have one all started by day 7 so they pass under either rule. Producing one means `POST /prescriptions/{id}/check-billing-validation`, which **re-creates** the VO's billing verdict rows (measured on #3675's inert anonymized VO: the returned ids are new) — a write to live billing-validation state that can move a VO on or off the billing Validierung queue, so it is left as one call a PM can make. **Observations for the PM:** the app commit also fixes a bug **no AC mentions** — `VOsExpiringBanner` compared device-local midnight to a UTC instant, so a VO exactly five days out dropped off the banner east of UTC, i.e. in Germany; AC7 lists that window as "unchanged" and it was in fact wrong at the boundary. **No Logo discharge VO exists on staging**, so AC4's speech-therapy clause is unexercised; and 4 of AC5's 6 screens (T Board V1, billing validation, billing batch details, Praxis Flow patient file) plus 4 of AC7's 5 warnings were not driven — all read the same two serialized fields, which are verified, and that is stated rather than implied. **Runs in ~1 minute** by prefetching all 33 fixtures and their sessions in two batched requests (see the batching section above) and firing AC6's ten previews concurrently; the first version took 5.6 min. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.discharge-dates.page.ts` |
| `sa_gkv_insurer_required.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @GkvInsurerRequired @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.15 (#3810) an insurer (Versicherung) is required for GKV on both VO forms, the Create Patient pop-up and the patient form, and a GKV VO also needs its OWN Versichertenstatus. **Deployed — staging has moved to `3.15.0`; 6 passed, 1 skipped.** **THE RULE IS DELIBERATELY FORM-ONLY, and that decides how the whole ticket can be tested.** Its Out of Scope rules out "a rule on every save outside these forms … it would break Praxis Flow saves, the deceased marking, the merge command and the nightly jobs" — so there is **no server-side validation to probe**, AC9 holds by construction, and AC2/AC3/AC6/AC7 exist only on screen. **Which is also what makes it safe: every save this file attempts is one the rule must REFUSE, and a refused save writes nothing** — the fixtures are chosen so that even a build without the rule would write back the values the record already holds. Verified: **AC3** — VO **5921-2** (Aktiv, GKV, no own Versichertenstatus, patient 4677 has no insurer) refuses to save and shows **both** proposed messages verbatim, with the VO's status, its Versichertenstatus and the patient's insurer and legacy `insuranceCompany` all re-read afterwards and unchanged; **AC2's discriminator, which is what makes AC3 mean something** — VO **5806-7**, whose patient DOES have an insurer, fires the Versichertenstatus message and **NOT** the insurer one, so a build that gated the save on the wrong field or reused one message for both conditions fails here while passing AC3; **AC1** — the VO form paints `Versicherungsart`, `Versichertenstatus` and `Versicherung` with the last two **on the same row** (both y=1336.5, x=322.9 and 433.0), plus `Neue Versicherung erstellen`; **AC6** — the patient form marks the field **`Versicherung *`** on a GKV patient, with the search placeholder and the create link; **AC9** — the deceased dialog still answers for a patient the form would refuse (count 1, list 1). **THREE FILTERS THIS TICKET NEEDS ARE ALL SILENTLY IGNORED**, which is why its population cannot be selected server-side and why **#3812 exists to put it on a screen**: `/patients?insuranceType=` (#3710), `?exists[insuranceProvider]=false` AND `=true` (#3560) each return the whole **8,901**-row table. Worse, **`insuranceProvider` is OMITTED when null**, so on exactly the patients this ticket is about the key is absent from the item read and a presence check cannot tell "no insurer" from "wrong serialization group" — the two patients are compared instead (4677 absent / 4560 present). It is also absent from the LIST payload entirely, so the fixtures had to be found by reading patients one at a time. Sampling note: **50 of 50 sampled GKV VOs, and 25 of 25 Aktiv ones, carry no Versichertenstatus of their own** — the ticket's production figure is 3,301 of 8,352 open GKV VOs. **AC7's refusal is SKIPPED, with the reason measured rather than asserted:** the patient form's save control reads **`Änderungen speichern`** (not the VO form's `Speichern`) and carries `aria-disabled="true"` while the form is **pristine**, so the refusal is only reachable once a field has actually changed — and the insurer search box does not render until the field is opened, so it cannot be used to dirty the form harmlessly. Reaching AC7 therefore means editing real patient data, which this file will not do; AC6's required marking is asserted instead, and the manual step is one line for a PM. **THREE READINESS/LOCATOR TRAPS, each of which reads as the feature being absent:** the patient form's section header is **CSS-uppercased** — it paints `VERSICHERUNG` but its `textContent` stays `Versicherung`, and Playwright's text engine matches `textContent`, so `getByText('VERSICHERUNG', { exact: true })` never resolves and burns its whole timeout (the #3718/#3770 family; note `sa.patient-insurance-prefill.page.ts` still uses that locator); the patient route takes **no `?id=` query**; and on the VO form **"Speichern" paints EARLY** while the insurance section lands later, so a gate on it alone hands the caller a form whose fields are not there — this file's own AC1 test passed on one run and failed on the next with identical code until the gate waited for `Versicherungsart`. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.gkv-insurer-required.page.ts` |
| `sa_privat_basis_expiry.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @PrivatBasisExpiry @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3709, commit `28f402375`) Privat Basis VOs must be expired by the nightly job exactly like GKV — the 14/28-day start deadline and the therapy-area validity windows — with only PKV still exempt. **DEPLOYED 2026-09-17, and the first nightly run under the fix landed that night — 10 passed, 2 `fixme`, every AC but one verified live.** **The job is console-only, but its two INPUTS are serialized, and that is the whole method:** `treatmentStartDeadline` (`PrescriptionPresenter::getTreatmentStartDeadline()`) and `validityDate` (`PrescriptionExpirationTrait::getPrescriptionExpirationDate()`) are both in `prescription:read`, and **both return null for an excluded insurance type before any rule runs** — so a Privat Basis VO serving null on both IS the pre-fix build. Measured before the deploy: all 10 Privat Basis VOs served neither, against 40/27 of 40 GKV VOs. **After: 11 of 11 carry a start deadline and 7 a validity date** — the probe flipped on its own. **The GKV control is mandatory** — both fields are legitimately null on many GKV VOs (no first treatment ⇒ no validity window), so "Privat Basis serves null" proves nothing alone. `GET /status` reports `3.14.0` and cannot settle it: it gives the RELEASE, not the commit (#3704). **AC1–AC4 are verified as ARITHMETIC, with the port validated before it is trusted:** `oracleStartDeadline()` / `oracleValidityDate()` re-implement the shipped rules and are first checked against what the API serves for GKV — **160/160 on the start deadline, 99 matches / 0 mismatches on the validity date** (18 `both-null`, 43 on the deliberately-unported Ergo branch, reported rather than guessed). Only then are they applied to Privat Basis. AC2's boundary is pinned exactly: 27 days → not yet, **28 days → not yet either** (the deadline is reached, not passed), 29 → qualifies. **AC5's test had to be rewritten around a trap that manufactures a false violation:** staging's history predates both exclusions, so PKV VOs carry old automatic `treatment_expired` entries — **VO 3188-1 has 192 of them** — and a naive "no PKV VO was ever auto-expired" check fails on a 2025 artifact (the #3651 lesson: old log rows are never rewritten, so partition by date). `meta.type` matters too — a `manual` entry is an admin expiring by hand, which no type is exempt from. The rewritten test is **relative and self-calibrating**: the job demonstrably ran last night (2026-09-16 20:02), GKV's newest automatic expiry is **7 days** old, PKV's is **113**, and Privat Basis's is **329** — so PKV's exclusion is in force today, and Privat Basis's staleness is itself this ticket's pre-fix evidence. Once deployed, a fresh automatic entry on a Privat Basis VO is AC1's positive proof. **AC7 measured live: 11 Privat Basis VOs, 5 the job would look at, 2 qualifying (4946-8 Ergo, 7551-9 Physio)** against the ticket's production figure of 1 — the ticket says to expect drift, but a QA comparing staging to "exactly 1" would file it. **The status filter is load-bearing** — the job skips Abgerechnet/Fertig Behandelt/Abgebrochen/Abgelaufen/Archiviert, and 8 of the 10 are terminal, so ignoring it counts 4 instead of 2. **SCOPE WARNING, and it is the thing to tell QA first:** `28f402375` is "treat Privat Basis like GKV for validation **and** expiry" — it also ships **#3708** plus migration `Version20260916070000`, which appends `privat_basis` to every `validation` row carrying `public` minus 11 named checks. So deploying #3709 also changes which validation checks a Privat Basis VO gets. That is measured here as an **independent second deployment probe** through `preview-creation-validation` (#3576, transient, writes nothing): Privat Basis is on **6** checks against GKV's **18** and PKV's **7**, reaching the same not-deployed verdict from a completely different surface. **FINDING** → reported: **AC3 names two conditions and only one can ever apply.** The rule is `isUrgentTreatmentNeed() || UV === insuranceType`, and `insuranceType` is a single field — a VO is either `privat_basis` or `accident`, never both — so for a Privat Basis VO only `dringender Behandlungsbedarf` can produce the 14-day deadline; the accident half describes a state that cannot exist (1 urgent Privat Basis VO exists, 7551-7, but it is Fertig Behandelt so out of scope). **Traps:** `urgentTreatmentNeed` is **omitted when false** so it reads `undefined` — the rule tests `=== true` and so does the port; a **Blanko** VO takes its own branch and carries `totalTreatments: 0`, so the 3/6-month Physio rule never applies to it; and `insuranceType` IS a registered filter on `/prescriptions` (unlike on `/patients`, where every value returns all 8,384). **The nightly run of 2026-09-17 20:02 is the positive proof, and it is exactly what the pre-deploy arithmetic predicted:** it expired **24 VOs — 22 GKV and the 2 Privat Basis ones this file had named the day before (4946-8 Ergo, 7551-9 Physio), with 0 PKV**, which is AC1 and AC5 in one measurement on the freshest possible evidence. AC3's reason is read back and checked for **self-consistency** rather than matched as a string: "Behandlungsbeginn nicht innerhalb von 28 Tagen nach Ausstellung (Frist: 13.08.2026, Ausstellung: 16.07.2026, heute: 17.09.2026)" — announced 28 days, `Frist − Ausstellung` = 28, the #3651 invariant that catches "28 days over a 14-day deadline" without knowing urgency. **AC6's first two thirds are verified from the run's own log rows**: `treatment_expired: Aktiv → Abgelaufen` (`meta.type: automatic`, no author) plus `field_change: null → order` in the same second, i.e. Folge-VO Status set to Bestellen because it was blank. **AC4 is now measured on real Privat Basis data**, not argued from the GKV side: four VOs with a first treatment each serve a validity date equal to the ported rule to the day (4355-1 → 2025-09-26, 4207-2 → 2026-04-23, 905301-2 → 2026-10-09, 965505-4 → 2027-03-08), covering **both** the 3-month and the 6-month branch. **Two assertions had to be rewritten to survive the run, and both are the same lesson — a pre-deploy test can encode the pre-deploy state:** AC7 asserted `qualifiers > 0`, which fails on a WORKING build once the job clears them, and is now the durable catch-up invariant (*a VO may only still be a qualifier if its deadline passed AFTER the last run*); and AC6's blank-check used `toBeFalsy()`, but **"blank" in a log row is the literal string `"null"`** (`PrescriptionLogTypeEnum::EMPTY`), so a falsy check reads the sentinel as "already had a value" and reports AC6 failing on a correct run. AC4's expiry BY the validity window and AC6's locked-editing third → `fixme`: no Privat Basis VO has reached a validity window yet (the ones past theirs are terminal; the two live ones sit at 2026-12-17 and 2027-03-08, and the GKV side of the same shared code path DID fire in the same run), and `assertOnlyWhitelistedChanges` is gated on `VALIDATED` rather than on insurance type, so there is nothing insurance-specific left to assert. **Read-only** — every request a GET except the transient validation preview. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.privat-basis-expiry.page.ts` |
| `sa_vo_delete_guardrails.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @VoDeletion @Security @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.14 (#3671, PR #3693) an Admin or Super Admin deletes a mistaken VO through a guarded confirmation step; the VO becomes recoverable for 30 days and appears on a new "Gelöscht" tab. **Deployed on both halves; 14 passed, 1 `fixme`.** **Deployment has to be decided twice** — `GET /status` reports `3.14.0` but that is the API only, so the first test reads the two preflight operations off the API AND the dialog's German out of the served entry bundle (#3705). **AC2's whole truth table is READ-ONLY**, which is what makes a destructive ticket testable: `GET /prescriptions/{id}/deletion-preflight` runs the SAME `PrescriptionDeletionService::resolveBlocker()` the confirm re-runs and writes nothing (the #3576 technique). All three blockers have live fixtures and each names its artifact — 5674-9 → `invoice_sent` "426-14", 4193-2 → `billing_batch` "E-2026-857-001", 3188-1 → `validated`. **The PRECEDENCE is measured and no AC states it:** 4207-2 is `validated` AND invoiced → reports `invoice_sent`; 4193-2 is `validated` AND batched → reports `billing_batch`, so the order is invoice → batch → validated and a QA reading the AC's table cannot predict which message such a VO shows. On screen: the action sits beside Speichern/Abbrechen; the summary's five rows; "Grund für das Löschen"; the 30-day sentence; the acknowledge checkbox with the confirm button `aria-disabled="true"` until it is ticked; the **blocked** dialog ("VO kann nicht gelöscht werden" + the batch message + "Schließen", no acknowledge behind it); AC5's warning interpolating the server-side count ("Diese VO hat 2 dokumentierte Behandlungen…"); AC6's parent warning naming 9634-10 with all three radios. AC15 is asserted by **x-order along the tab row** — the tabs are NOT `role="tab"` but leaf divs — giving `Folge-VO erhalten · Keine Folge-VO · Fertig behandelt · Zur Prüfung · Alle VOs · Alle inkl. Archivierte · Gelöscht`, with the badge equal to `dashboard-counts.deleted`. The one mutating test deletes and restores: status preserved (`Abgelaufen` → `Abgelaufen`), `restorableUntil` exactly `deletedAt + 30d`, one `prescription_deleted` Verlauf entry carrying who/when/why, the VO on the Gelöscht tab and searchable there, counts moving `allWithArchived −1 / deleted +1`, and a second delete answering 409. AC18 both ways: `treatmentStatus: "Gelöscht"` → **400 "Invalid treatment status"** while the control `Archiviert` → 200 (probed with an id matching nothing, #3561), and the frontend status enum's 10 cases carry no such label. AC9 server-side: no reason → 400, bogus reason → 400, `other` without a note → 422. AC1: a therapist gets 403 on the preflight and on `/delete`, and `exists[deletedAt]=true` **fails closed** for them (200 with `totalItems: 0`, not 403). **Traps:** `?patient=` is silently ignored on `/prescriptions` (returns the whole 34k book) — the registered filter is `patient.id=`; the VO form does **not remount** on a navigation between two edit URLs (#3535), so one form per test — `openVoForm()` waits on "Speichern" rather than the VO number, which renders as an exact leaf on some VOs and inside a larger string on others; and the Verlauf is permanent, so a fixture accumulates one delete/restore pair per run and a `find()` keeps returning the FIRST run's entry — assert the delta and the newest row. **FINDING** → reported: the **Gelöscht tab's empty state is in English** ("No prescriptions match these filters", "Clear filters", "Create VO") on an otherwise German board — shared with every other tab and pre-existing, but this is the tab that is empty by default, so it is where an admin reliably meets it (#3611's family). AC14 → `fixme`: `PrescriptionDeletionRecord` deliberately carries no `#[ApiResource]` (the DPO reads it from the database), so the permanent record has no client surface at all. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.vo-deletion.page.ts` |
| `sa_vo_deleted_exclusion.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @VoDeletedExclusion @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.14 (#3672, PR #3697) a deleted VO must disappear from every read surface in Flow, with ONE deliberate exception — the VO-number allocator, which must keep counting deleted VOs so a replacement never reuses a number. **Deployed; 3 passed, 1 `fixme`.** **The file is one round trip, and that shape is the point:** a surface that merely *does not contain* the VO proves nothing unless it contained it a moment earlier, so all twelve reads are taken three times — before → deleted → restored — on the same VO. Measured (VO 905301-1, patient 7826): patient VO list `3 → 2 → 3`, `/v2/prescriptions` the same, `dashboard-counts` `all 7017 → 7016 → 7017` / `allWithArchived 34266 → 34265 → 34266` / `deleted 0 → 1 → 0`, the Gelöscht tab `false → true → false`, both F.VO candidate lists `true → false → true`, `/activities?prescription[]=` `2 → 0 → 2`, the item read **200 for an admin throughout but `200 → 404 → 200` for the therapist** (which is the tablet arm from the other side), and the duplicate-check returning nothing. **AC4 in both directions: `next-id` answered `905301-4` in all three phases** — the number stayed reserved while the VO was deleted, which a central Doctrine SQL filter would have broken and is why the implementation used an API Platform read extension instead. **AC3 is a write that must FAIL, which is what makes it safe to run:** `POST /activities/bulk` against the deleted VO answers **422 `{"violations":[{"propertyPath":"prescription","message":"controls.activity.vo_deleted"}]}`** and writes nothing (re-checked). **Traps:** that endpoint takes a **bare JSON array** with a `therapist` IRI per row — wrapped in `{activities: […]}` it answers 400 "expected a JSON array" and a row without a therapist answers 400 too, either of which reads exactly like the constraint being absent, and `POST /activities` itself is **405**; `?patient=` is silently ignored (`patient.id=3` vs `patient=34266`, pinned by the first test); `/therapy_reports` registers **no per-VO filter at all** (only `prescription.patient` / `prescription.therapist`); and the i18n check must use the **leaf** key — `vo_deleted` occurs twice (de + en) while the full path `controls.activity.vo_deleted` occurs **zero** times, because the API returns it as the violation message and the frontend translates whatever it is handed, so a search for the dotted path reads as "never shipped". AC1's remaining four surfaces + AC2 → `fixme` with the reason per row: the tablet's own sync is device-side; CRM ordering cards and the KPI/Flow-Boards figures need a VO currently live in that surface's period (and KPI reads are cached ~5 min, #3401), i.e. real work somebody is doing; therapist notifications are generated by the nightly jobs. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.vo-deletion.page.ts` |
| `sa_vo_deleted_job_skip.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @VoJobSkip @Mutating` | RC 3.14 (#3673, PR #3700) twelve nightly jobs plus the TheOrg CSV importer each gain one deleted-VO exclusion. **Merged and on the staging build; 1 passed, 2 `fixme` — and the ticket's own Testing Guidance says UI verification is not practical.** **So this file tests the invariant the thirteen skips exist to protect, not the skips**, and says so rather than dressing it up: the End Goal is "a deleted VO's pre-deletion status stays exactly as it was", every listed job would break it by writing a status, and that IS client-observable. The fixture is deliberately `Abgelaufen` — a live target for the auto-archive and ordering sweeps, not an inert state nothing would have moved — and survives a delete → restore round trip with `treatmentStatus`, `followupStatus` and `validationStatus` identical. What it does **not** prove is that a job running inside the window would have skipped it; that needs the developer's `--dry-run` route, which the file records in full (`app:prescription:expire`, `app:transition:followup ordered`, `app:prescription:transition-order`, `app:prescription:correct-stuck-completed`, `app:prescription:cleanup-bereit`, `app:prescription:auto-archive`, `app:copayment:recompute-liability`, `app:duplikat:flag-overdue`, each `--dry-run`). The remaining three entry points have no command at all — the two listener-based jobs and the CSV importer — and are pinned upstream by `DeletedVoJobSkipTest`, `DuplikatProcessBackendTest` and `CsvImporterCharacterizationTest`. **One job arm IS reachable and is covered next door:** late-session auto-completion can never fire on a deleted VO because the session cannot be written at all — `sa_vo_deleted_exclusion.spec.ts`'s AC3. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.vo-deletion.page.ts` |
| `sa_vo_restore.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @VoRestore @Security @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.14 (#3674, PR #3702) restore a deleted VO within 30 days, with the follow-up re-linking step. **Deployed; 8 passed, 1 `fixme`.** **The round trip is genuinely lossless for a PARENT link, and the sharpest assertion is the DATE.** `PrescriptionDeletionService` snapshots `{parentId, parentFollowupStatus, parentReceivedDate, childId}` before it unwires anything; `relinkSnapshotParent()` puts the first three back and **suppresses `FollowUpStatusListener` for that one flush**, because the listener stamps the received date to NOW on any followupStatus change and would overwrite the very field "keep" exists to restore. Verified on 9634-11/9634-10: parent `received` + `2026-07-31T04:55:06+00:00` → after delete with `unchanged` **both null** (the #2539 reverse reset, correct) → after restore with `keep` **`received` and the ORIGINAL date to the millisecond**. Also verified: AC2 the exact pre-deletion status returns and no other field moves; AC4 a `prescription_restored` entry with who/when and the choice as a code + internal id; AC1 therapist 403 on both the preflight and `/restore`; the payload guards (unknown choice 422, `different` with no `parentId` 422, itself as parent 422, nonexistent parent 422) with the VO still deleted after every refusal; and the bundle carrying the dialog, all three choices and all three blocked messages — **plus `restore_pending_tooltip` at 0 occurrences, which is how you can tell #3674 landed on top of #3671 rather than the epic stopping at #3671's inert placeholder button.** **Traps:** `restore-preflight` answers **200 with `blocked: {type:"not_deleted"}`** on a live VO rather than 404, so a status check alone reads as "restore is available for every VO"; `restore()` resolves the BLOCKER before it validates the payload, so on a live VO every malformed body answers 409 `not_deleted` and a read-only payload-guard test measures the blocker and concludes the vocabulary is open — those guards are only reachable on a VO that really is deleted; and an omitted `parentChoice` defaults to `standalone`, silently dropping a parent link. **FINDING** → reported: **a restored VO never regains its follow-up CHILD link, although the deletion snapshot records `childId`.** Restore only ever re-links the parent, so deleting a VO that was itself a parent and restoring it leaves its follow-up permanently detached with nothing in either dialog saying so — #3671 AC7 announces the severing as informational, #3674 AC3 covers only the parent direction, and the epic's End Goal says the VO "returns exactly as it was". The field being written and never read is what makes it look unintended rather than decided. AC5 → `fixme`: `window_expired` needs a VO deleted more than 30 days ago and `deletedAt` cannot be back-dated from a client, so the earliest instance is 30 days after the first real deletion; the guard's other two codes, the window arithmetic and all three German messages are asserted instead. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.vo-deletion.page.ts` |
| `sa_vo_anonymization.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @VoAnonymization @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3675, PR #3703, merged 2026-09-16) a nightly job (`app:prescription:anonymize-deleted`, EventBridge 03:15 UTC) permanently scrubs a VO deleted more than 30 days ago. **Re-verified 2026-09-23 against a staging where the job has now run twice — once by hand and once BY ITSELF: 7 passed, 2 `fixme`** (3 passed when written on 2026-09-17, before anything had ever been anonymized). The fixtures exist only because the dev back-dated two QA VOs with raw SQL on 2026-09-22 — the only way a `deletedAt` older than the epic's own deploy can exist — leaving three VOs on QA patient 8950, all deleted inside the same minute: **34978/99653-2** anonymized by the hand-run command, **34979/99653-3** the control, and **34980/99653-4** left un-scrubbed and therefore the first VO the NIGHTLY job ever had to find on its own. **It found it, and that is the one thing no PM evidence on this ticket could cover** — the Go-Live Prerequisite calls scheduling "external infrastructure … a task for the infrastructure owner" and every run on record was a human typing the command. Watched across the boundary: `/prescriptions/34980` answered **200 at 03:16:02Z and 404 at 03:18:07Z**, Gelöscht tab 7 → 6. That is AC1 end to end unattended plus AC6's "every night" clause. The standing form is a **catch-up invariant** (#3709's shape): a VO may only still be on the tab if it passed its window AFTER the last 03:15 run — a flat "nothing is overdue" would fail every day between noon and the small hours. **THE CONTROLS ARE THE WHOLE METHOD, because every positive result is a ZERO** (no logs, no row, no tab entry, 404) and a zero means nothing alone: "scrubbed" and "hidden from this reader" look identical from outside. Two VOs deleted in the same minute on the same patient, one anonymized and one not, is what separates them. **A control killed the obvious test and the replacement is worth more:** a first pass searched `exact[prescriptionId]` on the plain collection, got 0 for the anonymized number and read it as AC1's nulling — but the control answered 0 too, because that collection hides *every* deleted VO (99653-1 live → 1; 99653-2/-3/-4 → 0 alike). The door that CAN see a deleted VO's number is the tab's own (`exists[deletedAt]=true` + `exact[prescriptionId]`), where the control resolves and neither anonymized VO does — still a third AC4 door, **not** AC1 evidence, since the extension drops the row whatever its number holds. **AC2 is the half nobody had checked and it IS client-visible:** `/prescription_logs?prescription=` is NOT behind the not-deleted extension, so it serves a deleted VO's Verlauf perfectly well — **the control answers 3, both anonymized VOs answer 0** — which turns the zero into evidence rather than a visibility artifact; activities 0 and therapy reports 0 on the shared patient likewise. AC4 on three doors (off the tab, badge `dashboard-counts.deleted` agreeing, **404 on the admin item read while the control is 200**, unfindable by number). AC5: all 6 survivors inside the window and still readable, `restorableUntil` exactly `deletedAt + 30d` from the one `Prescription::getRestorableUntil()` the confirmation step, the restore guard and `resolveSkip()` all read. **FINDING 1 — anonymizing a VO RELEASES its number back to the allocator, against #3672 AC4.** This resolved only because the nightly run took a VO holding the HIGHEST suffix on its patient, which an earlier version of this file had flagged as the decisive fixture it lacked: `next-id` for patient 8950 went **99653-5 (02:38, deleted not scrubbed) → 99653-4 (03:18, anonymized)**. #3672 AC4 is explicit that the allocator must keep counting deleted VOs "so a replacement never reuses a number", and a delete/restore round trip demonstrably leaves it unmoved; anonymization does not, because AC1 NULLs `prescriptionId` and the allocator maxes over the numbers that still exist. The next VO for that patient is numbered 99653-4, which an earlier — now unidentifiable — VO carried. Reachable but unexercised: AC3 keeps Invoice rows with their FK intact, so a surviving invoice could point at a VO whose number has since been re-issued. **The same measurement settles AC1's nulling positively by inference** — nothing but a cleared `prescriptionId` moves that figure. **FINDING 2 — the PM's AC-1/AC-2 evidence cannot support what it claims.** Their rows read "Patient, doctor, practice, diagnosis, notes, and VO number all removed" and "Documents and images removed" with Surface recorded as *API* and the observation a 404 — but a 404 is exactly what `PrescriptionNotDeletedExtension` produces for an anonymized row whether or not one field was cleared (it adds `anonymizedAt IS NULL` to BOTH admin doors). The 404 establishes AC4 and cannot, even in principle, establish AC1; the field-level evidence is the console output, not the API. **Traps:** an anonymized VO stays `deletedAt IS NOT NULL` on purpose — anonymizing is not undeleting, so every #3672/#3673 predicate keeps working; "anonymized ⇒ no longer deleted" is backwards. **A therapist gets 404 on a merely-deleted VO too** (#3672), so only the ADMIN door tells the states apart — verified on all three fixtures. `resolveSkip()` has exactly four arms (`NOT_DELETED` / `ALREADY_ANONYMIZED` / `WITHIN_RESTORE_WINDOW` / `BILLING_BATCH`, the last a data-integrity alarm the command exits non-zero on, since #3671 AC2 refuses to delete a batched VO at all) and all four are client-checkable, which is what let the pre-run version assert a PREDICTION about the nightly run rather than an observation. **For the PM, from the implementation rather than the ticket: invoices are deliberately KEPT with their FK intact** and reported per VO as `invoicesKept` — a sound GDPR Art. 17(3)(b) exception on the same basis as #3492, but one AC3's "no patient-identifying information remains anywhere for that VO" does not name; neither fixture carries one, so it is unexercised as well as unnamed. AC1/AC3's remaining positive half → `fixme`: **no client door reads a scrubbed row**, so apart from the number the `scrubRow()` field list (21 columns NULLed, incl. `icdCode`/`secondaryIcdCode`/`diagnosisGroup`/`leadingSymptom*`/`discharge*`/`invoiceNumber`/`versichertenstatus`, wider than AC1 spells out) is recorded inline as the DB-side checklist. AC6/AC7's output half → `fixme` (console + per-VO CSV under `reports/AnonymizeDeletedPrescriptionsCommand/`; `--dry-run`, `--limit=N`, `--batch-size=N` default 50). **Read-only.** Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.vo-deletion.page.ts` |
| `sa_insurance_pair_reprice.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @InsurancePairReprice @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3562) #3535 built confirm-and-reprice for PKV ↔ Privat Basis only; this widens the guard to EVERY insurance-type pair and adds a validation-status reset, superseding #3535's AC6. **Deployed (`d15d268e0` + `be7b69c53`); AC1 verified on real data, AC3 in the bundle, AC5 has never fired. 5 passed / 2 `fixme`.** **The headline is that #3535's repricing never actually ran, and this file proves it now does.** That guard compared the changeset value with `instanceof InsuranceTypeEnum`, but Doctrine hands back the **backing string**, so it was false on every correction and the pass was skipped with no error — exactly what this suite reported on #3535 ("the confirmation is live and gated exactly right; the repricing does not happen"). `be7b69c53`'s docblock now cites that diagnostic verbatim (`{"oldType":"string","newType":"string","oldIsEnum":false,...}`) on this very VO. **The evidence is a STRADDLING fixture, never a total.** VO **4355-1** has three PNF sessions either side of the PRIVAT_BASIS step on 2025-07-01, which separates the only three outcomes a reprice can have: priced at each session's own date → **47,69 / 45,92 / 45,92** (correct, AC1's "not today's date"); priced at today → 47,06 three times; never repriced → the old PKV 61,95 three times. It reads the first, each figure recomputed from the live price ladder rather than hardcoded, with a companion assertion that at least one session differs from today's price — otherwise "own date" and "today" would be indistinguishable on this data. **Reading the VO's total cannot tell these apart, and that is the #3535 false pass:** a VO's revenue is snapshotted treatment lines PLUS a live fee calculation, and only the fee half follows the current type, so `Gesamtumsatz` moves either way (4355-1 went 257,85 → 224,65 while still holding PKV session prices; fully repriced it is **178,33**, which is what it reads today). **Always read `ActivityTreatment.resolvedTariff`.** A third test ties the corrected prices to a CORRECTION rather than some other recompute: the VO's own log carries 11 insurance-type changes, **3 of them after the 2026-09-02 string fix**, the last leaving it on the type whose prices it now holds. AC3's dialog ships with the per-treatment-day wording ("Behandlungen neu bepreisen?", "der am jeweiligen Behandlungstag galt"). **FINDING — AC5's validation reset has NEVER fired on staging:** the reset stamps a `validation_status_change` log with reason "Validation reset after an insurance-type correction" and there are **0** of them; the PM records AC5 as PASS by code reading and their own session notes the dialog was "dismissed with Abbrechen to preserve test VO", so no correction was ever confirmed through the UI either. The reset also needs a non-null billing `validationStatus`, and the fixture's is null. **Also for the PM: their AC table is renumbered against the ticket** — their AC-3 describes the validation reset (the ticket's AC5), AC-6 the invoiced-VO guard (AC7), and AC-1/AC-2 the dialog (AC3) — so "7 of 7" does not map onto these seven ACs. AC2 → `fixme`: only ONE pair has been exercised on staging, and each other pair would need its own straddling fixture; the risk is concentrated in the guard (a single `$old !== $new`) rather than in the pairs, since `ActivityTreatmentSnapshotRecalculator` already maps all four tariff columns. AC5/AC6 → `fixme`: the reset is deliberately NOT self-restoring — changing the type back does not restore `validationStatus` — so a live run leaves a real VO unvalidated; AC6 follows from AC5 by construction. VO pool: public 24,142 / private 1,299 / accident 31 / privat_basis 10. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.insurance-pair-reprice.page.ts` |
| `sa_reactivation_notice.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @ReactivationNotice @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3561, PR #3585) reactivating an expired VO (Abgelaufen → Aktiv) whose Folge-VO Status is "Bestellen" already clears that status and the Bestelldatum (#3197) — nothing told the admin. This ticket adds the notice: per VO on a single reactivation, one summary with a count on a bulk one. The clearing itself is unchanged. **Deployed; every client-inspectable half verified. 5 passed / 2 `fixme`.** **Both deployed surfaces can be inspected WITHOUT reactivating anything, which matters because a reactivation is not reversible in the way that counts** — setting the VO back to Abgelaufen does not restore the `followupStatus`/`orderDate` it destroyed, so every live run would permanently consume a fixture. (1) `POST /prescriptions/status/bulk` now answers **`{count, clearedFollowupCount}`**, the number the summary renders; **a payload naming an id that matches nothing probes it with zero side effects** — `findBy(['id' => …])` returns an empty set and the loop body never runs, giving a live `{"count":0,"clearedFollowupCount":0}`. **That distinction is load-bearing:** the loop writes a `treatment_status_change` log for EVERY prescription it matches, even when the status is unchanged, so the tempting "post Aktiv onto an already-Aktiv VO" probe is NOT side-effect free and is deliberately avoided. (2) The single-VO notice is decided client-side by `reactivationClearedFollowup()` reading the **PATCH response** rather than recomputing — so it cannot report a clearing that did not happen — and its contract is that a cleared `followupStatus` is **omitted from the payload entirely** rather than serialized null (#3302); asserted here on both states (three Abgelaufen+`order` VOs serialize `"order"`; a VO without it omits the key). **One i18n pair serves both paths** — `reactivation_cleared_followup_count_one`/`_other` interpolating `{{count}}`, so a single reactivation renders the SINGULAR of the string the bulk summary uses ("bei 1 reaktivierten VO"); both keys ship ×2 (de + en) and the German values are present in escaped form (#3611), with the frontend confirmed to reference `clearedFollowupCount`. AC5 holds by construction: the count arrives in the response body, i.e. after the write is committed, so nothing can gate the reactivation. **Fixture pool measured live: 2,107 Abgelaufen VOs, 983 with Folge-VO Status "Bestellen", 166 with BOTH** — and the ticket's own named example, **VO 5708 (1284-17)**, is back in that state, having been reactivated on 31 Aug, cleared, expired again and re-acquired "Bestellen". **Undocumented surface worth QA time:** the developer flagged on the PR that the notice also covers the **dashboards' inline VO-status pills**, not just the edit form — that is in neither the ticket body nor the ACs. AC1/AC2 and AC3 → `fixme`: they need real reactivations, each consuming a fixture irreversibly. Manual steps, including the "clear the dropdown yourself in the same save" case the helper deliberately keeps quiet, are in `docs/manual-test-3561-reactivation-notice.md`. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.reactivation-notice.page.ts` |
| `sa_price_change_invoice_impact.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @PriceChangeImpact @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3650) a back-dated Heilmittel price must warn, before saving, which ALREADY-ISSUED invoices would change amount — a back-dated entry earlier in 2026 silently moved **65 issued copayment invoices** (122,96 EUR, written off) — and must refresh Not Sent drafts after the save. **Deployed; AC1, AC3 and AC4 verified live. 5 passed / 2 `fixme`.** **The preflight is an unusually good surface: EXACT and non-writing.** `POST /treatments/price-change-invoice-impact` (a plain Symfony route with `#[IsGranted('ROLE_ADMIN')]`, because `use_symfony_listeners: false` means a custom controller never reaches an API Platform `security:` expression — #3529) INSERTS the candidate `treatment_price_history` rows, runs the **real** `ActivityTreatmentSnapshotRecalculator`, reads the new amounts and rolls the transaction back — so the warning's figures are the figures a save would produce, and a test may fire it freely. A test re-reads the price history afterwards to prove the rollback (20 rows before, 20 after, candidate gone). Payload `{entries:[{treatmentId, tariffType, effectiveDate, price}]}`, max **200**. Verified live with KG (id 71) PRIVAT 99,00 from 2026-01-01: **24 issued invoices**, each carrying AC1's four columns, every one actually moving amount, and **no patient field anywhere** in the response (the #3325 GDPR shape it mirrors). AC4 verified on all three no-warning paths — effective today, effective in the future, and back-dated at the price already in force — all `count: 0`. **FINDING — AC3's table is not the implemented rule.** The AC names six included statuses; the code uses `InvoiceStatusEnum::issued()` = *everything except NOT_SENT and CANCELLED*, so it also covers statuses the table never mentions: the live warning returns **R426-64, status `on_hold` (Pausiert)**. Defensible (an on-hold invoice has been issued) but unsanctioned by the AC, and a QA reading the table literally would file it. **A second divergence, deliberate and worth keeping:** a **NOT_SENT invoice that HAS been DATEV-synced** is listed although AC3 says Not Sent → No — because the after-save draft refresh is draft-tier only (NOT_SENT **and** never synced), so a synced draft would otherwise fall between the two halves of the ticket and be repriced with no warning. Staging has exactly one, **R126-86**, and the warning lists it. Stornos are excluded by `originalInvoice IS NULL`. **Trap:** `tariffType` is the `TreatmentTariffType` enum (`GKV`/`PRIVAT`/`PRIVAT_BASIS`/`BEIHILFE`/`BG`), **not** an insurance type — `PKV` is rejected **400**, a welcome change from the silently-ignored filters elsewhere in this API. AC5 → `fixme`: it needs the real save, and although `POST`+`DELETE` on `/treatment_price_histories` is exactly self-restoring for #3378's `resolvedTariff`, this ticket adds a SECOND effect to that round trip (the draft re-snapshot) and nothing establishes that the DELETE reverts a draft amount too — a repriced draft would be left on real billing data. The arithmetic is nevertheless verified, since `recomputedAmount` comes from the same recalculator the save runs. AC2/AC6 → `fixme` (dialog-level, needs a real write; AC6 holds by construction because the preflight writes nothing, and the frozen-amount guarantee itself is #3093's issue-time snapshot, asserted in `sa_legacy_invoice_amount_lock.spec.ts`). **Manual QA steps** — including the fixture that exercises AC5 (KG on the **GKV** tariff, prescribed on **55 VOs carrying a Not Sent copayment draft**) — are in `docs/manual-test-3650-backdated-price-warning.md`. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.price-change-impact.page.ts` |
| `sa_absence_end_date.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @AbsenceEndDate @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3663) `AbsenceService::resolveDays()` treated Personio's `ends_at.date_time` as the last absent day; it is an **exclusive boundary**, so every synced absence carried one extra trailing day — greying out the T Board calendar, inflating Ausfallzeiten counts and (perversely) SUPPRESSING "Personio fehlt" gaps on days the therapist actually worked. **Deployed as `98dca4962` and re-synced; AC1, AC3 and AC4's premise verified over the WHOLE population. 5 passed / 2 `fixme`.** **The bug has a measurable signature and that signature is the test:** a Mon–Fri absence wrongly extended to Saturday, so pre-fix **690 of 1,856 periods (37%) ended on a Saturday while only 5 started on one** — an asymmetry no real absence data produces. Measured now across all 145 users holding absences and all **1,601** periods: last-day weekdays **Mon 213 / Tue 186 / Wed 170 / Thu 301 / Fri 701 / Sat 7 / Sun 23** — **7 (0.4%)** against 2 starting on a Saturday, near-symmetric, with the distribution peaking on Friday exactly as the dev predicted. That is AC1 and AC3 together and needs no Personio access. AC1's named example is checked directly rather than taken from the run report: a sick leave **Mon 2026-03-16 → Fri 2026-03-20 at 5 days** on multiple users, none extending to Sat 21 Mar. **A correction I made to my own first pass, worth keeping:** an earlier test asserted a sharper per-period fingerprint ("ends Saturday AND starts on a weekday AND short") and **failed on 3 periods** — a Fri–Sat sick leave, a Wed–Sat sick leave and a Mon–Sat holiday. That predicate over-reaches: all three are perfectly ordinary absences, and nothing short of Personio separates them from a residual off-by-one. The evidence for this ticket is **statistical**, not per-period; the test now applies the ticket's OWN criterion instead — its third production example is a day "marked absent even though the therapist documented a full day of treatments" — and **0 of the 7 survivors carries any treated activity on its last day**. **Traps:** the route is **`/absence-days`**, kebab-case via an explicit `uriTemplate` — the snake_case form 404s and reads like "not deployed" (#3394); the collection filters ONLY by `user` and **the serialized row carries no user at all** (`date`, `absenceType`, `dayPortion`), so the population cannot be attributed from one bulk read and needs a 285-request per-user sweep; and on `/activities` **`?date=` is accepted and IGNORED** — it silently returns the therapist's ENTIRE history so every day looks busy — only `date[after]`/`date[strictly_before]` are registered, and `date[before]` excludes the boundary day, so one day is `after=D & strictly_before=D+1`. **Scope stated rather than implied:** a "period" here is a contiguous run per user + absence type rebuilt client-side, NOT the sync's own notion (two adjacent Personio periods of the same type merge), so this file counts 1,601 where Jarn's command reported 1,863 — it asserts the DISTRIBUTION, never parity with a figure produced elsewhere; the PM's independent check sampled 10 therapists (117 periods), this is the full population. AC2 → `fixme` with evidence: **all 8,008 rows are `dayPortion: FULL`**, so the half-day branch the Developer Reference calls "more involved" has no live instance to verify against (same finding as #3394). AC5 → `fixme` (console-only; the client-side corroboration is arithmetic — the collection held 9,481 rows when #3394 measured it and holds 8,008 now, consistent with Jarn's 1,903-day deletion plus later nightly syncs). AC4 → `fixme`: true by construction (one data source), and the absence-day↔Ausfallzeiten arithmetic is already asserted to 10 decimal places by `sa_personio_absences_epic.spec.ts`; note weekend days were ALREADY excluded from the quota numerator and denominator by `AbsenceMetricsCalculator`, so the quotas were never wrong — only the raw day-count badges and the calendar showed the extra day. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.absence-end-date.page.ts` |
| `sa_vorabinfo_no_facility.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @VorabinfoNoFacility @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3512) creating a **GKV VO for a patient with no Einrichtung** must not auto-generate a Vorabinformation. #3202 stopped generation for Praxis-VO facilities, but a VO with NO facility slipped through because the guard read `PRAXIS_VO !== $vo->getElderlyCareHome()?->getOrderingMode()` and a null facility makes that true; `ce9df2b44` puts a shared policy in front of BOTH creation paths — the API processor and the CSV importer, which had never received #3202's guard at all. **Deployed; AC5 verified precisely, AC3 supported, AC1/AC2 have no post-fix instance. 5 passed / 2 `fixme`.** **The ticket says AC1/AC2 are unreproducible because the form requires an Einrichtung — true, but the data gives a sharper reason: ZERO GKV VOs with no facility have been created by ANY path since the fix shipped.** The 11 no-facility VOs created after the cutover all carry no insurance type, so they were never in scope. The fix cannot be shown working here not because a form blocks it, but because the population it governs has been empty since the day it landed. **What IS decidable is the before half, and it is on record:** VO **9656-1** (GKV, **no Einrichtung**, created 2026-09-04T04:16:52) has notice **10228** created **2026-09-04T04:17:11** — nineteen seconds later, five days before the fix reached staging. That is what makes the cutover partition meaningful instead of a count of nothing. **AC5 verified exactly: 13 notices held by no-facility patients, every one created before the cutover, all still present, none re-dated or re-pointed** — pinned as a fixture because AC5's whole content is that they do not change, and re-deriving it costs a 3,272-patient sweep to rebuild a list that must be static. **THE TRAP, which inverts the result completely:** `groups[]=billing:read` **replaces** the default serialization group (`overrideDefaultGroups: true`) and `elderlyCareHome` is not in it, so VOs read through that group report **no facility for every VO on staging** — reading exactly like "nothing is affected by this rule". A first pass here had 120 of 120 newest VOs looking facility-less; without the override it is 55 of 120. The first test pins the difference (field **absent**, not null, so a `?? null` reader is silently wrong) before any facility count is believed. **The cutover is the DEPLOY, not the commit:** `ce9df2b44` is dated 2026-08-27 but is an ancestor of `release/3.13.0` ONLY (diverged from `release/3.12.0` and `main`), and staging took 3.13.0 at **2026-09-09T02:38** — partitioning on the commit date misclassifies notice 10228 (2026-09-04) as a post-fix violation when it is the last pre-fix one. Also note the rule keys off **the VO's** Einrichtung, not the patient profile's (the CSV importer never writes the patient's, so a profile check would wrongly skip generation for patients who genuinely are at a facility) — a notice records only its PATIENT, so patient-profile facility is a documented proxy here, not the same field. **FINDING — the PM's AC-5 evidence describes a failure, not a pass:** "Queried no-facility patients (853 exist). None have a pre-treatment notice linked." AC5 requires the opposite — the pre-fix notices must still be there, and 13 are, including their own cited example. Their AC-1 example is mismatched too: patient **1721** is "Prof. Dr. Manfred Naundorf" and HAS a facility, while **"Gisela Adler" is patient 13**, has none, and holds notice 10127. Taken literally their query would mean the 13 had been deleted, i.e. AC5 failing. AC1 → `fixme` (unreproducible by design AND by data — `VoForm.tsx` makes Einrichtung unconditionally required and the PM confirmed on 2026-08-27 that it stays, all 149 no-facility GKV VOs in the system having arrived via CSV import); AC2 → `fixme` (the CSV path is console-only, `app:import`, not drivable from a browser). AC4 needs no change — the manual override uses a different service method. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.vorabinfo-no-facility.page.ts` |
| `sa_bulk_mark_sent_scope.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @BulkMarkSent @Security @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.13 (#3549, PR #3688) `POST /prescriptions/report-send/bulk` marked therapy reports "sent" for **arbitrary VO ids** — a bare `findBy` on the posted ids with no therapist predicate, while every sibling bulk endpoint (organizer, transfer, ordering-status) already narrowed non-admins to their own caseload. A **P1 write-integrity** finding from the SEC-05.4 audit (#3469), out of that task's scope as a non-read gap. **Deployed as `13f109033` (2026-09-11); both ACs verified live. 6 passed.** **The write side was the worse half and the tests assert it:** every marked report also persisted a `PrescriptionLog` attributed to the CALLER against a VO they do not own — a forged audit trail, not merely an unauthorised state change — so the foreign-VO assertions check the log count, not just the report's `sent` flag. Verified live: therapist 198 posting `{id:[34085 own, 29949 foreign]}` → **`{count: 1}`**, own report marked with exactly one entry attributed to Jhenqa Test, **foreign report still unsent with 0 entries**. **THE TEST-DESIGN TRAP, which makes a broken build look fixed:** the query also filters `tr.sentAt IS NULL AND tr.archivedDate IS NULL`, so a foreign report that is ALREADY SENT is excluded for a reason unrelated to authorization and a count of 0 proves nothing — every fixture is verified unsent+unarchived first, and **AC2's control re-posts the very same id as an admin** (→ `{count: 1}`, marked by SA Jhen), which is what rules out "the report was ineligible anyway" and turns the therapist's zero into evidence of scoping. **Silent exclusion is correct, not a weak refusal** — it matches `BulkPostOrganizerController` deliberately, because a 403 would break the partial-batch contract AND confirm that a VO id exists; a test pins that a **nonexistent** id and a **foreign** id are byte-identical in the response (both `200 count=0`), so no id is ever confirmed to exist. **Run shape:** `--grep "@ReadOnly"` runs the safe four, *including the central security test* — posting a foreign id alone writes NOTHING while the fix holds, and if it ever writes, that IS the vulnerability; `@Mutating` adds the two that consume fixtures, because marking a report sent is forward-only with no client-reachable undo (pools measured at run time: caller 11, foreign therapist 7 has 15, and both tests skip rather than fail when exhausted). Also asserted: the payload guards reject before any write (`400 Invalid payload: "id" (array) is required.`, and `Batch size cannot exceed 500 items.` at `MAX_BULK_IDS + 1`). A read-only audit sweep checks the invariant the bug would break — every `therapyReportStatus` entry was written by the VO's own therapist or an admin (5 of 5 in the newest 3,000 `field_change` logs: 2 by the owner, 3 by admins) — and it is deliberately **bounded, not a census**: the collection holds **463,418** `field_change` rows and the `therapyReportStatus` marker lives only in the meta, which no filter reaches. **Traps:** `/therapy_reports` registers only `prescription.therapist` and `prescription.patient` — there is no `sent`/`archived` filter, so the pool is narrowed client-side; and an embedded `therapist` carries `@id` but **no bare `id`** (owner comparison goes through `fullName`, which is why a first pass printed `owner None` for every row). Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.bulk-mark-sent-scope.page.ts` |
| `sa_reminder_letter.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @ReminderLetter @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3559) an Overdue PKV or Privat Basis invoice gains a ready-to-send Zahlungserinnerung (singly and in bulk) and the automatic Reminded → To Debt Collector window moves from 7 days to **14**. **Deployed; AC1, AC3, AC4, AC6, AC7, AC8, AC9 and AC10 verified live. 8 passed / 2 `fixme`.** **Downloading a letter writes NOTHING, and that is asserted rather than assumed** — the ticket's PM notes say "remindedDate field stamped once on download", which would be a defect (it would mark the invoice Reminded before the admin sent anything, starting the 14-day debt-collection clock early, and would contradict the ticket's own "does NOT change the manual action admins already use to mark an invoice Reminded"). `ReminderLetterDownloadController` checks the audit voter, calls `isReminderLetterAvailable()`, renders and returns — no `persist`, no `flush`, same for the bulk controller and the renderer — and a test re-reads the fixture after BOTH downloads to prove `status`/`remindedDate` are untouched. The guard works off the EXISTING manual stamp, exactly as specified. **`Invoice::isReminderLetterAvailable()` is three ACs in one line** — `status === OVERDUE` (AC1/AC9) `&& remindedDate === null` (AC5) `&& insuranceType.usesPkvBilling()` (AC2; copayment/GKV never — AC10) — and both endpoints apply it, so the bulk path cannot diverge; a test ports it and compares against the endpoint over a sample spanning both outcomes (8/8 agree). **AC6/AC7/AC8 are proven by a natural experiment in the log, which is strictly stronger than a status check** (a status check cannot tell "moved at 14" from "moved at 7 and nobody looked"): three seeded invoices sat at Reminded with different ages when the rule shipped — **651** (reminded 2026-08-11, **30 days**) and **654** (2026-08-27, **exactly 14 days**) both moved automatically in the SAME nightly run at 2026-09-10T00:56:34 (AC8 + AC6), while **655** (2026-08-28, **13 days** that night) did **not** move and went on the NEXT run, 2026-09-11, at exactly 14 days (AC7). Two invoices one day apart, two consecutive runs. AC4 is read out of the rendered PDF: invoice number, date, `Ursprüngliches Zahlungsziel`, open amount, patient billing address, the Gesellschaft's frozen name/IBAN/BIC, the full Inkasso warning, and a new deadline that is **generation date + 14** — checked against the letter's own printed dates rather than a clock the test reads. AC3: 3 eligible invoices → 3 PDFs in one zip, and an ineligible copayment id is **reported in `Fehlerbericht.txt`** ("Für diese Rechnungen ist keine Zahlungserinnerung verfügbar…") rather than silently dropped (#3495's convention). **Traps:** the bulk payload key is **`id`**, not `invoiceIds` (#3333's shape) — `invoiceIds` answers `400 Invalid payload: "id" (array) is required`; **`/invoices?id[]=` is SILENTLY IGNORED** and returns the whole 587-row book, so a batched "fetch these invoices" helper reports whatever the first page holds (`/prescriptions?id[]=` and `/patients?id[]=` DO work — the convention does not carry across collections, and a first pass here produced a bogus treatment-status distribution because of it); `remindedDate`/`totalOnHoldDays` are DEFAULT-group only while `invoiceType` is `invoice-list:read` only (#3604); and the transition is `>= 14` **minus `totalOnHoldDays`**, so "exactly 14" only reads cleanly at zero on-hold days. **`Pages/util/pdf-text.ts` drops a line of this letter** — `pdfText()` renders the Inkasso sentence as "…die Forderung an ein gestellt.", losing "Inkassounternehmen zu übergeben. Die dadurch entstehenden Kosten werden Ihnen zusätzlich in Rechnung"; `pdfDocText()` from `pdf-layout.ts` recovers it and the Twig template has it, so **the letter is complete and the extractor is at fault** — use `pdfDocText` for this document, and treat a missing line from `pdfText()` as suspect before reporting it as a rendering defect. **FINDING** → the PM's own open question ("PKV tab Überfällig filter shows (0) despite 118 overdue invoices … needs separate investigation") is answered and is NOT a filter bug: `pkvBilling[invoiceStatus]=overdue` returns **0** and with `treatmentStatus=Archiviert` returns **114** — nearly every overdue invoice sits on an archived VO, which the billing list hides by default (#3277). Same seam that empties #3648's Fehler tab. **Also for the PM:** their AC table is misaligned with the ticket — the AC-2 row describes the letter's Gesellschaft header and AC-3 its content (both AC4's subject), AC-9 describes the PKV-only guard (AC10) and AC-10 a "Reminded Date" column no AC mentions — so "10 of 10" covers fewer than ten distinct ACs, and the two genuinely uncovered ones are not among the rows marked PASS. AC2 → `fixme`: of 118 overdue invoices **114 are `private`, 4 `public`, ZERO `privat_basis`**, so that branch is unexercised on staging (a data gap, not a suspected defect — the rule reads the enum, and #3534 established Privat Basis follows the PKV rules). AC5 → `fixme`: it needs `overdue` AND `remindedDate != null` (the manual-correction flip-back the docblock calls out); staging's ONLY overdue invoice with a remindedDate is R426-68, which is copayment, so its 409 is already explained by AC10 and cannot attribute the remindedDate branch — and producing one means marking a real PKV invoice Reminded, which starts its 14-day clock. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.reminder-letter.page.ts` |
| `sa_eti_per_gesellschaft.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @EtiPerGesellschaft @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3473) every PKV claim must submit to ETI Experts under its own Gesellschaft's account and API key — ETI requires one account per Gesellschaft because each has its own bank account and the bookkeeping must stay strictly separate. **Deployed as `1fb526b8b` (2026-08-27, no issue number in the subject — the reference is in the docblock); AC1, AC2 and AC3's independence row verified from the submission record. 5 passed / 2 `fixme`.** **This file NEVER submits, by policy:** `POST /invoices/eti/submit` files a claim with a real debt-collection partner, and even flagged `test: true` that is an outward-facing action against a third party that cannot be undone from here.
