It does not need to — the outcome is durable, so the file reads the record other people's submissions left behind: `etiTransactionId` is serialized on the invoice and every status move is in `invoice_logs`. **AC1 is covered for all 7 Gesellschaften, which is better than the ticket's own notes claim** — the PM recorded 5 and wrote "Entity 6 (Hannover) has no To-DC invoices on staging and was not tested", but a purpose-built **`ETITEST-R1-043901 … ETITEST-R7-043901`** series exists, one invoice per Gesellschaft, and all seven reached Sent to DC with a distinct transaction id (entity 6 = `26225ca0`, entity 7 = `b0ca96bb`). That also settles empirically the Go-Live Prerequisite asking whether entities 6 and 7 map to the Rhein-Ruhr2 / Stuttgart1 accounts. Measured: **20 tracked submissions across 7/7 Gesellschaften, every id distinct** — a shared or mis-routed account would surface as a reused id, and distinctness is the strongest routing evidence obtainable outside ETI. A second test walks one submission per Gesellschaft and confirms from the LOG that it actually reached `sent_to_dc`, since an id alone does not prove the transition. AC2/AC3-row-3 re-read the PM's 13 Sep batch `[615, 655, 637, 667]`: 3 succeeded under 3 different Gesellschaften and 637 (entity 3) failed the address pre-check and stayed at To Debt Collector — per-invoice independence, still readable after the fact. **What this evidence cannot establish, stated in the file rather than glossed:** a distinct transaction id proves each submission was accepted and separately tracked; it does NOT prove the claim landed in the *right* ETI account — only ETI's portal shows that. **FINDING** → two of the PM's five AC-1 data points no longer show what they are cited for: invoices **505 (R426-70, entity 4, `6b211c6e`) and 480 (R526-56, entity 5, `2d689b33`)**, quoted as "prior successful submissions", both DID transition on submission and were both moved **back** to `to_send_to_dc` by an **automatic** status change at the identical second, **2026-09-02T05:42:25**, keeping their transaction ids — so today they contradict AC1's "transitions to Sent to DC with its own transaction ID tracked". Those entities stay covered by their `ETITEST` rows, but whatever reverted two submitted invoices is outside this ticket's ACs and worth a look. **The mirror case is innocuous and pinned so nobody reports it as a routing defect:** invoice 479 (R126-84) sits at Sent to DC with **no** transaction id — one manual `not_sent → sent_to_dc` move on 2026-07-24, never submitted. Neither status alone proves a submission happened nor its absence proves one did not. **The shipped design deliberately diverges from the ticket's Developer Reference**, which said to keep `isTestMode()`'s `sk_test_` prefix check per resolved key: the ETI portal issues one key per Gesellschaft with no test/live distinction, so whether a claim opens a real case is decided by the request's own `test` flag — environment config, never inferred from the key — and `$testMode` defaults to **true** so an unconfigured environment cannot open real cases against patients. The PM's 4 Sep note about `sk_test_*` keys therefore describes something the code no longer keys off. **Traps:** `/invoice_logs/{id}` has **no item GET** (404, like `/activities/{id}` — #3398), so a log referenced from `invoice.logs` must be read through the collection with `?invoice=<id>`; `etiTransactionId` is in the DEFAULT group only while `invoiceType` is in `invoice-list:read` only (#3604), so the book needs two reads merged; and `billingEntity` is the filter that partitions by Gesellschaft (#3493). AC3's missing-key row → `fixme`: all seven Gesellschaften have a key so `hasKeyFor()` never returns false here (the PM records the same as "PASS (partial)"), and the map lives in the SSM parameter `/therapios/staging/eti-api-keys` injected as `%env(json:ETI_API_KEYS)%` — an ops change affecting every tester, not a fixture. Noted for whoever arranges it: `EtiClient`'s constructor treats an **empty string** as "no account configured", so the path opens by blanking a value rather than deleting a key, and the blocked message is hardcoded German in the controller — **`Kein ETI-Konto für diese Gesellschaft hinterlegt`** — matching the existing ETI error style rather than a translation key, which resolves the ticket's Localization Reference where it was still "not yet defined". AC4 → `fixme` (needs a live submission; the result shape is unchanged and the #2949 UI consumes it as-is). Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.eti-per-gesellschaft.page.ts` |
| `sa_activate_owner_parity.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @ActivateOwnerParity @Security @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3550, PR #3689) `PATCH /prescriptions/{id}/activate` must enforce owner-or-admin like `/terminate`. A P2 follow-up from the SEC-05.4 audit (#3469): that task gave activate a care-relationship gate (unrelated caller → **404**, existence non-disclosure) but left a residual — an **in-scope non-owner**, a therapist covering the patient via a `SharedPatient` share, could still activate someone else's follow-up VO. **Deployed (`e6d49b03c`); all three AC clauses verified live, 5 passed.** **The ticket is a four-leg matrix and this file drives it on BOTH endpoints**, which the title ("owner-parity with terminate") asks for and the PM's notes do not cover: unrelated **404 / 403**, in-scope non-owner **403 / 403**, owner **400 / 400**, admin **400 / 400** (activate / terminate). **Every leg sends an EMPTY body, which is what makes a security test on two write endpoints safe in CI** — both controllers check authorization *before* parsing the payload and both reject `{}` (activate wants `activate`, terminate wants `immediate` + `reasons`), so "reached the body" registers as 400 and no VO is ever activated or terminated; a final test re-reads all three fixture VOs and proves `treatmentStatus`/`followupStatus` did not move. **The insight the file re-proves rather than trusting:** the dev's PR note says a test built like terminate's would have proven nothing here, because terminate's test uses an *unrelated* colleague and on activate that caller is stopped by the 404 gate before reaching the new rule — leg 1 demonstrates exactly that (unrelated → 404, never 403), so the in-scope fixture is the only way to reach the code under test. **Fixtures are discovered, not hardcoded, and use only the suite's own therapist login:** the PM verified with four accounts whose passwords they had reset for that session (users 7/8/15 on VO 3188-1), which is neither reproducible in CI nor ours to depend on; `/shared_patients` rows read `{ownerTherapist: <sharer>, therapist: <shared-with>, patient}`, so a row whose `therapist` is the caller puts them in scope for a patient whose VOs belong to someone else. Both routes were run and agree leg for leg. **Two silent-filter traps on `/prescriptions`, pointing OPPOSITE ways** — an unregistered filter returns the unfiltered 34,265 rows, which reads exactly like "this patient has every VO": by patient the registered name is **`patient.id=`** (`patient=`/`patientId=` ignored), by therapist it is **`therapist=`** (`therapist.id=`/`therapists.id=` ignored) — and that is the mirror image of `/activities`, where `prescription=` works and `prescription.id=` is ignored (#3533); neither collection's convention predicts the other's, so `assertFiltersPartition()` proves both before any count is believed. Also asserted: the refusal text is generic and contains no patient id, owner id or VO number. **FINDING** → reported, not failed: **the parity is one-directional.** An unrelated caller gets **404 on activate but 403 on terminate**, because `TerminatePrescriptionController` has no care gate at all (no `CareRelationshipChecker`, no 404 path) — so terminate confirms the VO exists to a therapist with no relationship to the patient. `authorization-matrix.md` marks that row **OK-justified** because owner-or-admin is "stricter than care relation", which is true of who may ACT but does not address who may learn the VO exists — the property #3469 established for the sibling endpoint. A decision for the audit owner. **Doc nit:** the matrix cites the owner-parity commit as `0f0eb3a43`, which **diverged** from `release/3.13.0` (pre-rebase of the stacked PR); the commit that shipped is `e6d49b03c`. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.activate-owner-parity.page.ts` |
| `sa_draft_auto_cancellation.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @DraftAutoCancel @ReadOnly` | RC 3.13 (#3649) a draft invoice (Not Sent, never DATEV-synced) whose VO has no billable sessions left must be cancelled automatically — `SERVICE_NOT_RENDERED`, an invoice-log note, no Storno — on every trigger that already refreshes a draft's amount, plus VO expiry as a seventh. **Deployed; 7 passed / 3 `fixme`. The headline result is that AC1's state does not exist on staging:** re-deriving the shipped rule over every draft-tier invoice gives **0 of 404** with zero billable sessions, and **0** "cancelled automatically: no billable sessions left." notes in all 565 invoice-log notes — so the automatic cancellation has never run here, in all of history. That is why the PM's notes read **Surface: Code on all seven AC rows** ("destructive, not performed"), and it is the gap this file closes: a code-only pass cannot separate "the rule is right" from "the rule never runs", and on this ticket both are true. **What IS decidable read-only is the negative half, at full scale** — every AC except AC1 is a statement about drafts that must SURVIVE, and 404 survivors is a large sample of exactly that. AC2: all **404** have ≥1 billable session (1…44) and every one is still Not Sent. AC4: **400** of them sit on a CLOSED VO (Archiviert 374 / Fertig Behandelt 16 / Abgebrochen 10) and keep their draft, which is a strong test almost by accident — if status leaked into the condition the draft book would be empty. AC5: all three invoiced types carry drafts (public 387 / private 13 / privat_basis 4), so "no tariff type is excluded" is exercised rather than assumed. AC3 has **exactly one discriminating fixture on staging and it is essential**: `isDraftTier()` is `NOT_SENT && datevSyncedAt === null`, and **R126-86 (VO 3899-13) is Not Sent yet DATEV-synced** (2026-07-24) — every other Not Sent invoice satisfies both halves, so a build checking only the status would pass without it. **THE TRAP, which produced a false finding here before the predicate was read from source:** "delivered" is `isDeliveredSession()` = NOT (rejected && !rejectedWithSignature) AND **`treatmentType !== PLANNED`** — a NEGATIVE test, so an **omitted** `treatmentType` counts as DELIVERED. The PM's AC6 row states the rule as "activities with status done", and an oracle written that way disagrees on **28 of 404** VOs in both directions: harmlessly high where a rejected-without-signature session carries `done`, and **low where the field is omitted** — staging serves `done` 3,177 / `planned` 10 / omitted **2**, both omissions on VO 9634-5, which the naive rule scores at **0** and the shipped rule at 2, so its draft R126-118 presents as a stranded zero-session draft and is not one. A test pins the two predicates apart and asserts that exactly one VO sits in the dangerous direction. Second trap: a rejected session is excluded only **without** a signature — VO 8954-1 carries one of each flavour and therefore has one billable session, not zero. **FINDING** → cancellation additionally requires **`validationStatus === VALIDATED`**, which appears in **no AC**; the reason is sound and documented in code (#3204 keeps a Not Sent draft through a validation reset so its reserved number is not burned) but a QA following the ACs literally would file a missing cancellation as a bug. One live draft sits in that state (R226-53, VO 6536-1, 19 delivered sessions). Also recorded: all **10** manual cancellations on file created a Stornorechnung — the behaviour AC7 changes — and all predate the fix. **Fetching:** `/activities?prescription[]=` and `/prescriptions?id[]=` both accept many ids per request, which is what makes a 404-draft sweep affordable (~14 + ~11 batches) instead of 800 sequential reads; `?prescription.id=` is silently ignored (#3533), and a truncated activities page would invent zero-session VOs, so the helper throws on `totalItems > member.length`. AC1/AC6/AC7 → `fixme`: producing AC1's state means taking a real VO's last billable session away, and **#3655 exists because an auto-cancelled draft has no supported re-invoicing route**, so it would strand a live invoice number; AC7's verifiable half (no draft-tier invoice carries any DATEV sync state, 404 of 404) is asserted in the AC3 test, while its no-Storno half needs a draft cancellation that staging has never had. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.draft-auto-cancel.page.ts` |

Read-only** (every request a GET except the export POST, which writes nothing). Before it, a VO appeared once per RISK, so a therapist's header counted and valued it twice — management reported the TO team did not trust the counts, and the team sends these screenshots to therapists. **The provider is untouched**, which is what makes this exactly checkable: `GET /kpis/orga/risks` still serves one row per VO PER TILE, so the expected grouping, the expected per-VO risk set and the expected line order are all re-derivable from it, and the change lives in the client and the export. AC8: the tile-selected download is still one row per VO of the tile (1,713) and the tile count is unchanged. AC9: the Admin-Performance board — which renders the SAME `RiskWorklist`, so it is the realistic leak — is still flat, one row per risk, with its tile totals equal to its row counts per tile. **TWO PARSING TRAPS THIS TICKET CREATED, both of which fake a failure:** (a) **the Thema cell is MULTI-LINE** — risks joined with `\n` INSIDE the quoted, `;`-delimited field — so a parser that splits on newlines before handling quotes reports **2,908 rows for 1,713 VOs**, which reads exactly like the export duplicating rows, i.e. like this ticket being broken (that is precisely how it first presented here, in #3774's spec); (b) **a group now counts DISTINCT VOs, not payload rows**, so a row-counting oracle reports Aaron Babczynski as 70 where the screen correctly shows **57** — AC5's own figure — i.e. it reports the screen as wrong when the oracle is stale. Both are fixed in `ToRiskGroupingPage` (`parseCsv` carries quote state across line breaks; `groupRows` counts distinct VOs). **FINDING** → reported as a green test: **with a tile selected the DOWNLOAD now carries risks the tile view does not show** — **1,195 of 1,713 rows** gained an `Informationsblatt (IB) fehlt` line. AC8 freezes that download ("the table and its download stay as they are") and the Developer Reference states the assumption outright — "the Fertig tile's download, where each VO has one risk and the result is unchanged" — but that premise is false: a VO on the Fertig tile can also carry an IB risk, so the shared export joins both. The ROW SHAPE is unchanged, which is what the PM's AC-8 check looked at, so it would not have been caught there. Arguably more useful, but it is a change to a surface AC8 freezes — a PM decision, not a defect. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.to-risk-grouping.page.ts` |

(Wochen)" still at column 8 and filled on every row, the two columns still measuring different things (**0 of 200 rows have weeks == days**, so the day count did not silently replace the week count), the tile rule intact (no VO on it is ≤ 30 days) and the tile count equal to its rows, plus the Admin-Performance texts surviving in the bundle. **TWO TRAPS FROM SIBLING #3803 (`d05a33860`, one row per VO), both of which fake a failure on a correct build:** (a) **the Thema cell is MULTI-LINE** — a VO's risks are joined with `\n` INSIDE the quoted field (1,195 of 1,713 rows), so a CSV parser that splits on newlines before handling quotes reports **2,908 rows for 1,713 VOs**, which reads exactly like the export duplicating rows; `ToRiskGroupingPage.parseCsv` now carries quote state across line breaks, and that fix alone turned a "failing" #3774 AC5 back to passing. | `sa_storno_overdue_exclusion.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @StornoOverdue @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3799, commit `7c2b3b030`, on `release/3.14.0` AND `release/3.15.0`, no PR) the nightly overdue check must never move a **Storno**, and the ones it already moved return to Sent at release. **9 passed, 0 `fixme`. Read-only — every request a GET.** A Storno is a credit the billing team issued, not a debt, so an Overdue Storno shows money owed that does not exist. The whole API change is **two lines** — `andWhere('i.originalInvoice IS NULL')` on each of `app:invoice:check-overdue`'s two queries — plus `Version20260925090000`, which returns already-moved Stornos to Sent at deploy, clears `overdue_date` and writes the `status_change` log by hand because plain SQL bypasses `InvoiceStatusListener`. **DEPLOYMENT IS NOT CLIENT-DECIDABLE, and the file asserts why rather than guessing:** the change adds no route and no serialized field, `/status` gives the release not the commit (#3704), and **the migration WOULD have been the probe** — it runs at deploy and writes one log per corrected Storno — except staging had **0 Overdue Stornos**, so it corrected nothing and left no fingerprint. Measured: **17 Stornos, ALL `not_sent`, and ZERO `status_change` logs on any Storno in all of history**, so the bug never fired here either; "the migration ran" and "there was never anything to run on" are indistinguishable. **So the rule is tested as a PURE FUNCTION, both versions ported** (#3719's technique): the live data cannot discriminate — with every Storno `not_sent` neither version selects any and they agree vacuously — so the two steps are driven over constructed candidates and shown to differ **only** on Stornos, with the pre-fix arm required to move the Storno or the test proves nothing about the change. Boundaries pinned too: 21-day prefilter, `REMINDER_PAYMENT_DAYS` 14, on-hold days still subtracted, and AC2's copayment clause still excluded from step 2. **AC2's control is what stops the invariant being vacuous:** **128** invoices sit in a check-produced status and **every one is a non-Storno**, so the check demonstrably still works — an invariant that only says "no Storno moved" is equally satisfied by a check that does nothing. **AC4's surface is one field in the right place:** a Storno has no billing row of its own (#2867), so every screen in AC4's table renders **`stornoStatus` from the ORIGINAL invoice's row** — 17 carriers for 17 Stornos, agreeing on all 17, with every original still `cancelled` (AC3's last clause). **THE SERIALIZATION MAZE — three groups and none carries everything:** `originalInvoice` (the embedded object) is **default only**; `originalInvoiceId` is **`invoice-list:read` only**; `sentDate`/`overdueDate`/`remindedDate` are default/`billing:read`; `totalOnHoldDays` is **default only**; `stornoStatus`/`stornoNumber` are **`invoice-list:read` only**. Every one is **omitted when null**, so a field read from the wrong group is indistinguishable from a real null — **this file's own first measurement ("no Storno has a sentDate") was taken from a group that does not serialize `sentDate` at all**; the answer happened to be right and the reading was worthless, which is why `assertSurfacesPartition()` now proves the split before anything is believed. Also `?zzzNotAFilter=` on `/invoice_logs` is **accepted and ignored** (returns all 1,599) while `type` is registered. **The ticket's staging fixture is pinned WITH the collision it warns about:** `S126-6` → `R126-84` (ids 670 → 479) — **staging's `S126-6` is a DIFFERENT Storno from production's**, whose original is `R126-20`, so reading the billing team's production numbers against staging finds a real but unrelated Storno. **The Out-of-Scope guard is bound to its call sites:** #2868's two-choice vocabulary is checked as `STORNO_SELECTABLE_STATUSES` fed to **2** Storno status controls (the PKV and copayment tabs) — a loose `['not_sent','sent']` match would pass on an unrelated literal while the column had been widened, and the constant is exported through `Object.defineProperty(…,{get:()=>_})` so its value cannot be matched next to its name. AC1's live half and AC3's correction need the development team (back-date a sent date, run the nightly check) → `docs/manual-test-3799-storno-overdue.md`; the invariant tests become the real regression guard the moment any Storno is set to Sent. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.storno-overdue-exclusion.page.ts` |
| `sa_fee_agreement_upload.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @FeeAgreementUpload @ReadOnly @Mutating` | RC 3.14 (#3790, commit `0d5bc0d27`, on `release/3.14.0` AND `release/3.15.0`, no PR) an uploaded signed Honorarvereinbarung must count as the patient's signed fee agreement everywhere. **Deployed and verified end to end; 7 passed, 0 `fixme`.** Uploading a signed fee agreement created only a `PatientDocument` and never touched the patient's `Hono`, so the Document Center, the waiting PKV VOs and the ETI contract date all still saw an unsigned agreement. `HonoUploadSigner` now moves the patient's open Hono to SIGNED — **as a status UPDATE on purpose**, because `HonoStatusListener` reacts to updates only and is the one place that stamps `signedDate`, writes the Hono log and activates the waiting PKV VOs; a Hono persisted as SIGNED would skip all three. **THE TRAP, and it silently tests the wrong person:** the ticket says "patient 8789 / 8786 / 9498", and those are the **UI patient NUMBER**, not the API id — they resolve to **7517 / 7522 / 8286**, and `GET /patients/8789` answers **200 for an unrelated patient**, so a spec written on the ticket's numbers finds none of the described state and reads as "not reproducible" (#3577's shape; the mapping is pinned in its own test, resolved through a Document Center row's `patientNumber`). **Everything the ACs name is readable and only the trigger is a write:** the fee-agreements card is `GET /document_center/documents?type=hv&status=…` (**`type=hv`**, not `honorarvereinbarung`, which answers 0), its open count is `hvActionable` on `/document_center/counts`, the Hono carries `status`/`signedDate`/`logs`/`honoDocumentId`/`pkvPrescriptionCount`, and AC3's tile is the `honoFehlt` rows of `/kpis/admin-performance/risks`. Verified read-only at full scale: **AC1's invariant over every signed fee-agreement document on staging** — a patient holding one must have a signed live Hono — partitioned on the fix's ship time, because the ticket does NOT backfill (**1 historical divergence from 2026-05-12, patient 7081, both Honos archived; 0 after the cutover**, the #3651/#3709 rule); and **AC3 over the whole tile**, 0 VOs of a signed-document patient on `honoFehlt`, which is the "already worked" half since `HonoStatusChecker` and `findHonoFehlt()` read the DOCUMENT. **The end-to-end test is env-gated because the upload cannot be undone** — `DELETE /patient_documents/{id}` is **405**, no delete route exists, and the request also archives the patient's unsigned Honos, signs one and moves PKV VOs to Aktiv. Run once on **patient 8470 `Sanji VinsmokeTest`** (deliberately a QA patient, not the ticket's real case): Hono 622 `not_sent` → **`signed` + signedDate + log**, VOs **965403-2 and 965403-3 both For Review → Aktiv** each with the `Honorarvereinbarung unterschrieben` entry, the Document Center row `hv-622` → `signed` with its `signedAt` and file, and both VOs off the tile — signed HV documents went **91 → 92** and honoFehlt rows **105 → 104** on the very next read. **That fixture also gives AC2's *Not sent* row and a free scope control**: its GKV VO 965403-1 is asserted UNTOUCHED, since the ticket's Out of Scope says only PKV VOs wait. **More traps:** the multipart create is **`POST /patient_documents/upload`**, its own `uriTemplate` — posting to the collection answers **405**, which reads exactly like the route being gone; **`?patient=` works on `/honos` and is SILENTLY IGNORED on `/patient_documents`** (it returns all 965, and the registered filter there is `documentType`, with `type` ignored), so neither collection's convention predicts the other's (#3550) and `assertFiltersPartition()` proves each before a count is believed; and **`signedDate` is OMITTED when unset**, so it reads `undefined` and a `toBeNull()` fails on exactly the state being pinned (#3302/#3709). **Deployment needed a bundle probe:** the API half adds no route and no serialized field, so it has no read-only probe and `/status` answers for neither the commit (#3704) nor the frontend (#3705) — but the app half ships in the same commit and its invalidation block survives minification verbatim (`'honorarvereinbarung'===O&&(k.invalidateQueries({queryKey:['honos']}),…)`), and the served entry bundle changed the same morning. **Patient 8286 (the ticket's "9498") is pinned in its reported state and deliberately never written to** — it is the billing team's real case and the last untouched instance, and the ticket itself records that its other repro patient was consumed by a workaround run. AC2's *Sent* and *no/archived-only* rows, AC3's new-VO clause and the ETI contract date → `docs/manual-test-3790-fee-agreement-upload.md` (the first two need another irreversible upload, the ETI one would file a real claim — this suite never submits, per #3473). Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.fee-agreement-upload.page.ts` |
| `sa_password_reset.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @PasswordReset @Security @Slow @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3737, SEC-07, commits `e21f4a7de` / PR #3748 and `c369d0709`, the SES task role) self-service password reset, plus a flagged block on an account whose password is its own e-mail address. **The reset half is deployed and verified end to end; the weak-credential half is switched OFF in every deployed environment by configuration. 6 passed, 3 `fixme`.** **Both halves of the app had to be checked separately**: `POST /password/forgot` answers on the API, and the frontend deploys independently (#3705) while `/status` gives the release not the commit (#3704) — so the screens are verified from the served bundle (`forgot-password` 6, `reset-password` 7, `password_change_required` 4, `invalid_reset_token` 1, plus both locales' strings). Verified: **known, unknown, empty, missing-key and malformed addresses all answer 202 with one BYTE-IDENTICAL body** naming no address; unknown, empty and no-password tokens all answer **400 `invalid_reset_token`**; the limiter trips near the configured **10 / 15 min** with `Retry-After` and a machine-readable `too_many_requests`, and `/password/reset` **draws on the same budget** (the commit's "shared DBAL pool" — otherwise an attacker just alternates endpoints). **THE CONSTRAINT THAT SHAPES THE WHOLE FILE: every probe spends one of ten slots per fifteen minutes, keyed on the client address.** So the file is built to need ONE window — five requests for the enumeration test, three for the token test, and the **ceiling test deliberately last** so the budget it exhausts is one the file has finished with; `PasswordResetPage.spent` counts across tests, so the trip point is measured over the whole window rather than over one test's own requests, and the timing FINDING costs nothing because its baseline is the samples the enumeration test already took. Run it at `--workers=1`; it opens with a **31-minute wait**. **The wait is a flat sleep of TWO intervals with no probe, and both halves of that are failures this file actually had:** under a SLIDING window a probe returning 202 means one slot freed, not that the budget is clear, so a poll-until-it-works wait returned after 0 s and handed the next test nine spent slots (every assertion then read 429); and Symfony's sliding window carries a **weighted share of the PREVIOUS bucket**, so one interval after a saturating run is not enough either — measured, 20 minutes after reaching the ceiling four requests succeeded and the fifth answered **429, which reads exactly like the endpoint refusing a malformed address**. The duration IS the precondition. **Traps:** **`/password/reset` validates the TOKEN BEFORE the payload** — a request with no `password` at all still answers `invalid_reset_token`, so a payload-guard test written against a bogus token measures the token check and reports the password policy as missing (pinned by an assertion so nobody re-derives it); and **a real address means a real e-mail**, so only a mailbox this suite owns is ever sent one and the link is never opened (an unused token changes no password) — every other probe uses `@example.invalid`, the #3462 etiquette. **FINDING** → reported as a green test: **the response is enumeration-safe and the response TIME is not.** A first `/password/forgot` for a real account mints a token and sends mail — measured **1,536 ms against a 324 ms warm baseline, a ~1,210 ms gap**. **The obvious confound is ruled out rather than argued away:** the first request through a context also pays TLS and connection setup, so the probes are ordered with an unknown address FIRST (spent as the warm-up, excluded from the baseline) and the known one second — and the cold request came back in **377 ms**, i.e. four times faster than the warm known one, so the gap is server work and not connection setup. Getting that ordering wrong is how a timing claim becomes unfalsifiable. Narrow in practice (ten probes per quarter-hour, and the per-account throttle makes a repeat for the same address fast, so only a FIRST request is informative — which is also why there is one sample on the known side and not five), so it is reported rather than failed: the ACs ask for identical responses and it delivers those. **The weak-credential ACs are `fixme` on the ticket's own Verify step**, and the reason is configuration, not data: `WeakCredentialEnforcement` is **`false` in BOTH the staging and production parameter files** (re-read from the repo every run, so the `fixme` cannot go stale) and the check runs when a token is minted, so the 403 `password_change_required` path cannot fire anywhere — flipping it is an ops change affecting every user, and the commit is explicit that `app:security:weak-credentials-report` runs first to inform that decision. Producing the fixture is also self-defeating: the policy that shipped with this ticket rejects a password equal to the account's e-mail address, so building one means defeating the feature under test. The password policy itself → `fixme` for the same reason plus the token-ordering wall. **AC6 ("tests cover expiry, reuse and the enumeration-safety property") IS verified, because its surface is the shipped test file** — `PasswordResetTest.php`'s 6 cases are read from the repo and each required property matched by name, which is also where the three things a client cannot reach live (single use and expiry both need a live token, and a token only ever arrives by e-mail): `testKnownAndUnknownAddressesGetTheSameAnswer`, `testTheTokenSetsANewPasswordExactlyOnce`, `testAnExpiredTokenIsRefusedLikeAnUnknownOne`, plus the two that make those mean anything — `testAWeakOrMismatchedPasswordIsRejectedAndTheTokenSurvives` (a rejected password must not burn the link) and `testASecondRequestWithinTheThrottleWindowIsAnsweredAlikeAndSendsNothing`. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.password-reset.page.ts` |
| `sa_monday_row_pairing.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @MondayRowPairing @ReadOnly` | RC 3.14 (#3783, commit `b97630f8d`, a `Ref` trailer with **no PR**) each facility must follow the Monday.com row NUMBER it keeps, not its Einrichtungs-ID — the follow-up to #3344, and it closes that ticket's standing finding. **8 passed, 0 `fixme`.** The nightly sync paired on `echId`, so when a Monday row's ID changed, whichever facility held that ID took the row's address and an empty twin was created for the row's real occupant (Hamburg 20 Sep, Hannover 17 Sep). **DEPLOYMENT IS CLIENT-DECIDABLE, through the run's own REPORT — and an earlier version of this file said the opposite.** That version probed `/elderly_care_home_logs`, `/facility_logs` and `/monday_sync_logs` (all 404) and concluded the command was unobservable; the surface is a **report**, not a log, and the ticket's own comment names it. **`GET /sync_facility_reports`** serves one row per run (`matchedCount`/`createdCount`/`gapCount`, 151 runs back to April) and **`/{id}/signed-url`** serves the CSV with AC6's seven columns (`category, echId, flow_name, monday_name, address, monday_item_id, reason`). **The probe is the CATEGORY VOCABULARY:** `conflict` is a category THIS ticket introduced, so a report carrying conflict rows was written by the fixed code — and the cutover is exact: reports **145/147/148 (19 – 22 Sep nightlies) carry `matched`/`name_update_skipped`/`gap` and NO conflict; 149 (23 Sep 11:30, the dev's apply run) onward carry `conflict: 5`**. `GET /status` still cannot answer (release, not commit — #3704). **The nightly is demonstrably running the fixed code**, not just the manual apply: report **151 is stamped 2026-09-24T19:32**, the EventBridge 19:30 UTC schedule. **AC6 is verified against the file itself** — the seven columns, and every conflict naming the OTHER facility by ID and Monday row (`… is also kept by active facility "…" (active, ID ST5, Monday row 2114947979) — neither was changed`) — with the collection's counts asserted to agree with the CSV it points at, two surfaces for one run. **THREE conflict shapes exist live, not two:** the two rule-9 pairs (216/218 `ST 5`+`ST5` on row 2114947979; **221/222 on row 1848742792**, the ticket's own Hamburg example) **and a rule-8 conflict — facility 239 keeps the ID `HH39X` because its row 2830700749 proposes `HH39` and 222 still holds it**. An earlier version of this row read `HH39X` as a half-finished human repair; the report shows it is the sync correctly declining to rename. **#3344's finding is CLOSED and now PROVEN rather than inferred:** that command built `$echMap[$echId] = $ech` over `findAll()` (one row silently overwrote the other) AND built its gap report from the **deduplicated keys**, so the dropped facility appeared nowhere in the run. The current report lists **E23 twice** — facility 37 as `matched` (it keeps row 1707662413) and facility **12 as a `gap`** (active, **243 VOs**, no row), which is the exact row that used to vanish. Also verified: rule 4 — **not one of the 11 practices keeps a Monday row**, so none can be row-paired; every facility in a conflict pinned field for field (`echId`/`name`/`address`/`mondayItemId`/`status`), which is a real guard because a nightly HAS since run under the fix; and the twin set pinned at **4 groups**, since "a new facility is not created when its ID would collide" means it must stop growing. **The staging state IS the ticket's own examples** — rule 9's is the Finkenau/Falkenbek pair *today*, rule 10's is two facilities with ID E23, one without a row number — so read the fixtures before reading anything as a defect. The pre-fix damage is still present and **explicitly Out of Scope** ("the operations team corrects it by hand before go-live"): facility **222 carries 221's address** while serving **11 VOs**, with the real address on **empty twin 239**. The consequence no AC states is that rule 9 makes 221/222 a **permanent** conflict — the sync will never change either again until a human clears one of the duplicate row numbers. **Traps:** **`order[id]=desc` is REJECTED on `/sync_facility_reports`** (a non-JSON error, not a silent ignore) so the newest run is reached by paging to the end; `/patients` registers **no facility filter** and an unknown one is ignored **silently** (#3344), so every VO count is checked against the unfiltered total first; and `apiBearerToken()` reads a browser's localStorage, so a file driving no page must mint with `POST /auth` directly (`.auth` is single-use, #3460). Rule 8's full ID CHAIN (HH38→HH43 resolving in one night) and rule 6's held ID+name change still need a write to Monday.com, a third-party board this suite does not touch — though the chain's residue is on staging (facility **257 `OLD-HH43`**, deactivated, beside a live HH44). Extends `Pages/superadmin/sa.monday-facility-sync.page.ts` (`reports` / `reportRows` / `parseReportCsv` / `countByCategory` / `duplicateMondayRows` / `twinGroups` / `idFallbackOnly` / `fingerprint`) |
| `sa_expiry_tile_startfrist.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @ExpiryTileStartfrist @ReadOnly @Slow` | RC 3.15 (#3873, commit `6deed2aa6` on `release/3.15.0`) the Therapeuten-Orga **"VO läuft ab ≤ 7 T"** tile keeps its title but changes what it lists — from VOs whose VALIDITY ends within 7 days to VOs that have **not started** and whose **Startfrist** falls within the next 7 days — with three new row texts. **Deployed; all ACs verified, 6 passed, 0 `fixme`. Read-only** — every request a GET. The old rule listed VOs *already past* the point where starting was useful; the new one is actionable, which is why the ticket also renames the Empfohlene Aktion to **"Behandlung vor der Startfrist beginnen"**. **The population change is the headline and it is enormous:** the ticket records staging listing **177** VOs under the old rule on 29 Sep; today the tile reads **6**. **Deployment is decided from the served bundle** — the whole diff is `riskTiles.ts` plus three dictionary values, so `/status` answers for neither the commit (#3704) nor the frontend (#3705): all three new texts occur once (`Startfrist in ≤ 7 Tagen`, `Startfrist in {{days}} T ({{date}})`, `Behandlung vor der Startfrist beginnen`) and **all three they replace occur 0** (`VO läuft in ≤ 7 Tagen ab`, `Läuft ab in {{days}} T ({{date}})`, `Vor Ablauf abrechnen / Folge-VO anstoßen`), which is the half that shows a REPLACEMENT rather than an addition. **THE ESCAPING TRAP, and this ticket is where it bites hardest:** the bundle escapes non-ASCII in **two** forms — `ä` as `\xe4` (<256) but **`≤` as `\u2264`** (>255) — so a helper emitting only `\xNN` reports **0 for every string containing ≤**, including the tile title the ticket explicitly does NOT change. That is why the title is one of three **unchanged controls** asserted beside the zeros: if the escaping is wrong the control reads 0 too and every "the old text is gone" assertion is worthless (#3611's trap, in the direction that fakes a pass). **AC2 is asserted as an IDENTITY, not by re-implementing the deadline rules:** its point is that the tile reuses the date Flow already shows, so each row's `statusDate` is compared against that VO's own serialized `treatmentStartDeadline` — **6/6 exact**, with `daysUntil` equal to the days from today to it. Re-deriving 28/14/7 instead would pass even if the tile kept its own copy that happened to agree. Two rules are live on the tile right now: **issue+14** (BG — 99671-1, 99647-1) and **issue+7** (Entlassmanagement, #3800's own window — 99699-1, 99701-1, 99703-1, 99709-1). Verified: AC1 every listed row `activityCount: 0`, open status, non-PKV, and `Wert` **0,00 €** on all six (the visible consequence of "not started", which the Out of Scope keeps); AC4 the rows served soonest-first (`[0,0,1,1,1,3]`); AC5 the tile number equals its row count; AC7 with two live negatives — **965505-4** (1 session) and **ZZP260-04586-00158** (8 sessions) are in range and correctly left off, which the test requires to be non-empty or "a VO in treatment is not listed" is vacuous. **The rule is then re-derived independently over the whole candidate window** (a Startfrist in [today, today+7] means an issue date in [today-28, today+7-7]; 137 of 137 collected, checked against `totalItems` so a truncated read cannot invent agreement) — **the rule selects exactly the 6 the tile lists**. **That equality took one correction and it is the lesson:** the first re-derivation said 7, naming **965903-1** as a miss. It is not — its therapist is **Sandra Zeibig (6), a marked test account**, and `DuplikatWorklistCandidates`-style joins exclude those board-wide (#3505). Pinned by its own test: therapists **6 / 31 / 198 all read `isTestAccount: true` and contribute 0 of the 5,890 rows**, while the busiest therapist (236, 128 rows) reads false — the control that stops this being "the board is empty for everyone". A re-derivation that omits the exclusion reports a correct build as defective. **Also observed:** the tiles payload now carries **`duplikatVerloren`** (3) beside `duplikatOffen` (2) — #3722's new tile, unrelated to this ticket but new since #3774's spec was written. Uses no page object; the spec is self-contained (its API surface is one endpoint plus per-VO reads). |
| `sa_practice_merge_main_bsnr.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @PracticeBsnrMerge @ReadOnly` | RC 3.15 (#3859, PR #3876 merged into `release/3.15.0` 2026-09-29) `app:practice-doctor:merge-duplicates` could leave the surviving practice with **no MAIN `practice_bsnr` row**, so the practice form refused to save (“One BSNR must be marked as main”) and #3285's `practice.practice_id` mirror stayed broken. **7 passed, 0 `fixme`.** **Read-only, and deliberately so:** the ticket and both Need Command comments say the command must not be run before the fix is live, and `--force` merges practices **irreversibly** — so nothing here runs it. The command has NOT been run on staging. **FINDING — the ticket's own QA route cannot be followed on staging.** Its QA note says “QA verifies it on staging data with the preview and apply run”, and AC5 asks for the check query to return 0 after the run. **It already returns 0** (0 practices hold BSNR rows without a main; 0 hold more than one), so a 0 afterwards is the same 0 — the #3799 shape, where a probe is perfect *unless it had nothing to correct*. Worse, the fixed branch **cannot be reached**: #3859 needs a survivor owning no main row once `moveBsnrRows()` has brought the others in as secondaries, and `ensureMainBsnrRow()` returns early when one exists — **all 14 practices in all 7 duplicate groups already hold their own main row, so 0 of 7 groups could exercise AC1**. The reported fixture (production practices 1763/1764/1765, three same-named practices of which only a merged-away one holds the BSNR row) has no staging counterpart, so **AC1 is verifiable on production only**; the staging run is still worth doing for AC3/AC4. **AC5's check query IS client-reachable**, which is what makes any of this measurable: `GET /practice_bsnrs` is a full collection carrying `number` / `isMain` / `practice` (1,457 rows over 1,444 practices, three pages), so the Need Command's SQL is re-expressed over it exactly — including its deliberate exclusion of practices holding **no** rows at all (a different, pre-#3806 condition). `bsnrRows()` throws on a short read, because a truncated walk would silently report “0 practices without a main row”. **The command's own decision rule is ported so the preview's group list can be read against something**: same name → merge; a **placeholder** (`for updating` / `no name` / blank, AND no data) beside a real practice → merge; a leading-zero-only number difference → merge with a flag; otherwise **skip for a decision**. Predicted on staging: **5 merges (all `placeholder`) and 2 skips** of the 7 groups. **That prediction is REPORTED, never asserted**, because `hasData` is only a LOWER bound from a client — the command also counts CRM activities and prescription images, which no endpoint exposes, so a predicted merge can turn into a skip; nothing in the file depends on which branch a group takes. The two skips are instructive: `727504400` is two real practices with different names, and `830039500`'s “FOR UPDATING” row **has 3 VOs**, so it is not a placeholder-with-no-data and its group skips too. **DEPLOYMENT IS INFERRED FROM A SIBLING TICKET, and that technique is the reusable part.** A console-service fix has no route, no serialized field and no bundle trace, so `/status` cannot answer (#3704). But **#3848 (PR #3866) merged at 2026-09-29T23:45:17Z and this fix (PR #3876) at 23:45:40Z**, into the same branch — and #3848's archive scan dated the staging deploy to **after 2026-09-30T01:58**, so any image cut since carries both. The live half is re-derived each run rather than taken from a recorded date: a **Zahlungserinnerung renders on demand and writes nothing** (#3559), so the reminder for the one overdue PKV invoice whose patient has no Anrede is read and must carry #3848's full-name greeting. Stated honestly as an inference about the BUILD — 23 seconds separate the two merges — not an observation of #3876, which has none. Also verified: **#3285's mirror** (`practiceId` == the main row's number) on all 14 group members — note `practiceId` is on the **item** read only, absent from the collection and from `practice-list:read` / `billing:read`, so it cannot be checked in bulk; **AC2's gate** shipping in the bundle (the refusal string, ×1) with no practice tripping it today; and an **ARMED invariant** for after the run — exactly one main row per practice that holds any, and the 7 main-row numbers still shared by two practices being exactly the 7 groups, which is the state `EnforceBsnrUniquenessCommand` is waiting for and why `UNIQ_practice_bsnr_number` stays deferred. **One worry checked and dismissed rather than reported:** both practices in every staging group hold the SAME number, so a merge looked like it would leave the survivor with a duplicate secondary row — `moveBsnrRows()` DELETEs the losers' rows that duplicate one the survivor already holds before moving the rest, so it does not. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.practice-bsnr-merge.page.ts` |
| `sa_working_hours_abrechnung.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @WorkingHoursAbrechnung @ReadOnly` | RC 3.15 (#3725, PR #3780 merged into `release/3.15.0` 2026-09-22) the Therapeuten-Orga **Arbeitszeiten** table gains a third column mode, **Abrechnung**, with five per-therapist VO counts and an expandable per-facility breakdown. **Deployed; all six ACs verified, 8 passed, 0 `fixme`. Read-only** — every request a GET; the board is navigated, switched and expanded. **AC5 is the whole ticket** (“the facility sub-rows sum to the therapist row”) and it is exactly checkable, because the board's own `GET /kpis/management/working-hours` carries BOTH sides — so the payload is captured as the table loads (#3471) rather than re-issued, and the painted numbers are compared against the payload they were drawn from. **Measured over the whole population: 100 therapists with a breakdown, 0 mismatches on all five columns**, plus the painted sub-rows summing to the painted row on screen, iterated column by column so a sixth count added later cannot be left out of the invariant. **THE HEADLINE TRAP, and it is a cross-ticket one: the payload carries THREE completed-ish fields and only one of them satisfies AC5.** `completedUnbilledCount` — which PR #3780's own body says it “reused” — is now the **UNION of all three end statuses**, because **#3775 widened it the very next day** (`0f909db2a` changed that field from 3 to 5 in #3725's own integration test); the identity `completedUnbilledCount == completedOnly + cancelled + expired` holds on **100/100** rows. Binding the column to it would **break AC5 on 77 of 100 therapists** and triple-count against the Abgebrochen/Abgelaufen columns beside it — measured, so the test can fail. The frontend correctly reads **`completedOnlyUnbilledCount`** with a comment naming #3775, and the discriminating fixture is chosen live (a therapist where the two fields differ — **Andreas Rosky, 28 against 36**), because a therapist where they agree proves nothing. **FINDING** → reported as a green test: **the two “Fertig” columns disagree, and the one that is supposed to be a SUBSET reads LARGER on 77 of 105 therapists** — including **5 where Abrechnung reads 0 and Operativ does not** (Karsten Jahns 0 against 8). AC3 calls the new column “a different, therapist-level breakdown” and the PR records the only difference as the >30-day staleness filter; they now differ on **two** axes, since Operativ's `completedUnbilledOver30Count` is the stale subset of the **union** while Abrechnung's is the whole unbilled population of **Fertig Behandelt alone**. Each is correct under its own definition, so it is a PM decision — but the labels differ by one word (`Fertig n. abger.` vs `Fertig (n. abger.)`) and a TO user switching modes sees the subset exceed the total. **That one-word difference is also AC3's own trap:** the two headers differ ONLY by their parentheses, so an `includes` matches both and AC3 passes on a build still rendering the old column — the check is exact-membership, **in both directions** (Abrechnung has the new and not the old; Operativ the old and not the new), which also stops the assertion being satisfied by an empty header row. **TWO MORE READING TRAPS, each of which reads as the feature being absent.** (a) **The board opens in GRUPPEN view, whose rows are `team-toggle-g<teamId>`, not `working-hours-name-t<id>`** (#3718) — polling for the therapist id never resolves until the view is switched, and the 240 s timeout then looks exactly like the whole mode missing (that is how this file first failed); readiness accepts either, with a separate therapist-row gate after the switch. (b) **The headers are CSS-uppercased**, so the ticket and the PR quote `AKTIVE VOS` (which is `innerText`) while a DOM read returns `Aktive VOs` — a constant written in the painted form fails on a correct build; both readings are asserted, and `Maßnahme` survives the source reading where `innerText` gives `MASSNAHME` (#3718/#3770's ß→SS rule). Also verified: **AC1** the toggle offers **Details · Operativ · Abrechnung** — note #3770 renamed Standard to **Details**, so the PR's own evidence line (“Standard · Operativ · Abrechnung”) no longer matches and `Standard` is asserted GONE; **AC2** 12 painted rows agreeing with the payload field for field; **AC4** one sub-row per facility under `working-hours-facility-t<id>-fac-<facilityId>` with `├─`/`└─` connectors; **AC6** collapse removing both the sub-rows and their connectors; the **facility-less bucket** rendering as **`Ohne Einrichtung`** (`facilityId: null`, 5 of 105 therapists) — not an AC, but load-bearing for AC5 since `elderly_care_home_id` is nullable and dropping those rows would break the sum for anyone treating outside a facility; and **the PR's own browser-only defect**, that the sub-row numbers must share the therapist row's right edge — it records fixing a left-aligned render that made a correct breakdown read as broken, and that its unit tests asserted values rather than geometry and passed throughout, so the geometry is re-measured here (all three sub-rows and the therapist row at `[742, 878, 1014, 1150, 1286]`). Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.working-hours-abrechnung.page.ts` |
| `sa_search_vo_number_sort.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @SearchVoSort @ReadOnly` | RC 3.15 (#3872, PR #3883 merged into `release/3.15.0` 2026-09-30) while a search is active the Admin Board sorts results by **VO number, highest first** instead of by issue date — patient number then sequence, both as integers, so **9018-10 ranks above 9018-9**. **Deployed on both halves; all seven ACs verified, 11 passed, 0 `fixme`. Read-only** — every request a GET; the board is navigated, searched, sorted and read. On a copy of production the old order disagreed with VO order for **461 of 5,602 patients**. **The ticket is a sort order, so everything turns on telling the shipped order from the one it replaces, and TWO oracles do that.** (a) On the API, `order[voNumber]` against a **lexicographic** sort of the same rows — the orders are DISJOINT wherever a patient has a two-digit sequence, since `-10` ranks between `-1` and `-2` lexicographically and above `-9` numerically. Each fixture carries an **anti-vacuity guard requiring the two rules to disagree**, because on a patient with no two-digit sequence they agree and the comparison proves nothing. (b) On the board, the `order[…]` key in its **OWN request**, which IS `resolveSort()`'s output — `order[voNumber]=desc` while searching, `order[date]=desc` otherwise. **THE FIXTURES ARE NOT THE OBVIOUS ONES, because the board hides archived VOs** (`exclude[treatmentStatus][]=Archiviert`): the ticket's own screenshot patient **9018 has just 2 VOs on staging**, and **2960 — which holds -19 down to -3 — is archived in its entirety, so the board shows 0 rows for it**. Scanning the 7,414 board-visible rows gives **43** patients carrying both a ≥10 and a ≤9 sequence; the file uses **7354** (`-14 … -9`, six rows on one page with `-10` directly above `-9`) and **9634** (24 rows, `9634-99/17/16/15/13/11/10/5…1` plus four sibling patients — maximally discriminating), with **901** (29 VOs across **15 patients**, 3 pages) for AC2 row 3 and AC1's cross-page clause. Verified: AC1/AC2 all three rows; AC1's “across all pages” as monotonicity over three pages with no row repeated; AC3 clearing restores issue date; AC4 a header wins and its **third click returns to VO order**; AC5 a chosen column survives typing AND clearing a search; AC6 on **all seven tabs including Gelöscht** and at phone width; AC7 the arrow moving onto `VO #` and off `Ausst. Datum`. **FOUR TRAPS, and three of them fail a CORRECT build.** (1) **A repeat of a query the client already holds fires NO request at all** — React Query serves it from cache, and that is exactly what AC4's third click, a click on the already-active tab and AC3's clear all do; asserting a request there fails on a working board (it did here twice). The **arrow and the painted rows** are the signals that survive a cache hit, so AC3 additionally compares the painted rows against what the API serves for `order[date]=desc` — without that it would pass on a board that kept VO order and merely repainted the arrow. (2) **A search that matches nothing paints no table and therefore no header row**, so the arrow reads `null` — `Zur Prüfung` does this for the `901` search, which is not the feature failing; the arrow is asserted only where a table painted, with a count requiring several to have done so. (3) **The glyph follows the DIRECTION** — `↑` for asc, `↓` for desc, `↕` for “sortable, not active” — so asserting `↓` on an ascending column fails on a correct build. (4) An unknown `order` key is **silently ignored** (#3449), so “the filter exists” has to be *asc differs from desc* against that control, never a 200. Also: a **`socket hang up`** throws before any status exists, so the API helper retries on a thrown transport error and not only on a 5xx. **Two things the ACs do not cover and this file pins.** The PR's **malformed-VO-number decision**: the `uniqid('VO-')` fallback, unmatched imports and #3675's nulled numbers sort **last in BOTH directions** — verified on the real head and tail of all 35,067 rows (`ZZP…`/`ETITEST…` fill both tails, never a head). And a **collateral fix**: PR #3883 also repaired a pre-existing **500** on `/v2/prescriptions?order[orderingStatus]=…` whenever the request also carried a join — which **every real Admin Board search does**, since `SearchOrFilter` left-joins `patient` — because API Platform's `QueryChecker` misparsed the inlined `CASE WHEN o.orderingStatus …` as the alias `"CASE WHEN o"`; the joined combination now answers 200 and #3749's ranked order still works without a search. **The Out of Scope is checked mechanically rather than argued:** the filter exists on `/v2/prescriptions` and nowhere else, so the v1 `/prescriptions` path — which the Billing Validation Queue and every other searchable list read — **ignores `order[voNumber]` exactly like an unknown key** and cannot inherit VO order. **Also corrected here:** `AdminDashboardPage.search()`'s docblock claimed the search box goes `readonly` after submitting and offers no clear control; re-measured, it stays editable and grows a **“✕”**, which is AC3's own first route out. Uses `Pages/admin/admin.search-vo-sort.page.ts` |
| `sa_double_treatment_per_session.spec.ts` | `@SuperAdmin @DoubleTreatment @ReadOnly` | RC 3.12 (#3602) Doppelbehandlung must double only the sessions actually marked double. Both engines computed `$multiplier = ($isDouble && !$isV2) ? 2 : 1` from the **VO-level** `prescription->isDoubleTreatment()` (and `DOUBLE_TREATMENT_MULTIPLIER_EXPR` in `RawKpiCalculator`), so turning the VO setting on doubled every session; the fix substitutes the per-session `Activity::getDoubleTreatment()`. **Deployed; the core rule is verified on 6 VOs that can each tell the two rules apart.** **The whole file exists because the per-session flag is unobservable:** it is exposed by NO serialization group (`billing:read`, `activity:read`, `doku:read` all omit it), neither `/prescriptions` nor `/activities` registers a filter for it, and **`order[doubleTreatment]` is accepted and SILENTLY IGNORED** — asc and desc return byte-identical pages (#3449's trap) while `order[id]` is honoured, which the second test pins so a page ordered by it is never trusted. So the verdict comes from a discriminator that needs no flag at all: for a V1 double VO the OLD rule can produce only `fees + base` or `fees + 2*base`, while the new rule gives `fees + Σ(line × (double ? 2 : 1))` — **a served total strictly between the two is something the VO-level rule cannot produce.** A VO whose sessions are ALL double is *not* evidence (both rules agree) and is classified **`ambiguous`, never a pass** — 13 of the 19 priced VOs fall there by construction. Four strictly-between proofs: **1503-25** 469,57 < **499,60** < 794,30 (1/11 double), **1281-16** 458,48 < **516,30** < 776,49 (2/11), **4056-1** 774,27 < **820,19** < 1380,08 (1/13), **3568-9** 208,40 < **295,13** < 352,95 (3/5). Plus the purest case — setting ON, nothing ticked: **1482-16** serves 434,33 where the old rule gives 734,63 (a **300,30** overbill) and **1714-14** serves 841,47 against 1424,15 (**582,68**). AC8 holds on all six: fees are counted once, and doubling them would overshoot by exactly the fee sum — which is also the client's own rule, since the validation page's line builder filters `one_time_fee`/`per_treatment_fee` out before duplicating a double session's lines. **A second bundle finding makes the flag readable at all:** `activity.doubleTreatment` IS served, API Platform just **omits it when false**, so presence ⟺ marked double. **Enumeration is the expensive part and the spec does not pretend otherwise:** `/prescriptions` costs ~0.13 s/row, so a full 34.247-VO walk is ~74 minutes, three concurrent 1.000-row pages **504 every request**, and `/v2/prescriptions` is heavier still (it computes revenue per row). The evidence test samples pages across the id space — 1.400 VOs, **32 double-flagged, all V1, zero V2** — and reports prevalence rather than implying a census. **Findings** → `fixme`: (a) the ticket's named staging fixtures **6421-14 and 8708-3 do not exist** (`exact[prescriptionId]` returns 0 for both, 1 for controls); they were the only V2 examples given and the sample found **zero** V2 double VOs, so **AC6 has no fixture on staging**, and neither does AC7 (no Blanko double VO) — "we could not find a V2 VO" and "V2 VOs are correct" are very different statements and only the first is true here; (b) AC3 blocked — `optica-export` answers 422 on every pending batch (#3288), the same wall #3603 hits; (c) AC4 only partly reachable, and worth chasing on **#3603** rather than here: `/v2/prescriptions` serves an **`amount`** column beside `totalRevenue` and on VO 8616-1 they read **1283,40 and 1325,20** — the pre- and post-#3603 figures — so the Admin Board's two revenue fields do not always agree. AC2/AC5 are data-gated: **0** of the 19 V1 double VOs carries an invoice and only 1 has a copayment figure. Note `sa.per-treatment-fee.page.ts`'s `expectedRevenue()` still hardcodes the OLD `vo.doubleTreatment && !vo.doubleTreatmentV2 ? 2 : 1` multiplier — correct for #3484's own fixtures, but it should be revisited now this rule has changed. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.double-treatment.page.ts` |

`tests/Production/Therapist/sentry_environment.spec.ts` (`@Therapist @OfflineCache @SentryEnvironment`) covers #3385 AC6 from the production side: it reads the `environment` tag off the envelopes app.therapios.de sends unprompted and asserts `production`. It self-skips while production serves a pre-3.11 build (v3.10.0 today, tagging everything `staging` — the defect the ticket describes).

`tests/Production/SuperAdmin/sa_session_count_parity.spec.ts` (`@SuperAdmin @SessionCountParity @ReadOnly`, two tests also `@Slow`) covers **RC 3.13 #3704** — an old-style Doppelbehandlung must count as TWO completed sessions and a fee-only visit as ONE, on the five VO list views. **Verified on production 2026-09-16; the fix is live; 6 tests pass.** **Read-only — every request a GET except the one `POST /auth` that mints the read token**, and the on-screen test only navigates and reads. **Five views, one field:** the Admin Board list, its mobile card, the Dokumentation window, the Patient Management prescription table and the GKV Unbatch view all read **`activityCount` on `GET /v2/prescriptions`**, computed by its own SQL in `PrescriptionListProvider::batchComputed()`; `bd2da1cb6` rewrites that one expression and nothing else, so checking the field checks all five. **`/status` cannot settle deployment here** — both API halves report `3.13.0`, which is the release and not the commit — so BOTH expressions are ported and the served count is compared against each; the one it matches is the one deployed. The pre-fix `COALESCE(treatment_qty, 1-or-2)` reached its fallback only when the LEFT JOIN was NULL, i.e. when the visit had **no `activity_treatment` row at all**, so a V1 double at quantity 1 took `treatment_qty = 1` and never doubled and a fee-only visit took 0. **Censused, not sampled, on the ticket's main population:** a full walk of all **39,477** VOs finds **1,006** old-style double VOs, 893 with visits, of which **832 are affected (5,846 visits, +5,849 sessions)** — and **893 of 893 serve the post-fix count, 0 the pre-fix one, 0 mismatches**; a random **10,000-VO** sample of non-double VOs (25.3% of the book) adds **7,678 of 7,678 matching**. AC1's own example VO **8950-1** reads 10/10 with 0 open against a pre-fix 9, and on screen the Beh. Status column painted **`26 / 36`** for the Aktiv VO 8512-2 where the pre-fix rule gives 13 — read off the board's OWN captured `/v2/prescriptions` response (#3471's technique), so the painted number is compared to the payload it came from. AC3: all 9 V2 VOs with visits are unmoved. AC4: 1,300 rejected-without-signature and 81 planned visits all contribute 0, and the start/last date aggregates still derive from the same visits. **Two figures for the PM, both larger than the ticket's:** it states 663 VOs / 4,345 visits (measured: **832 / 5,846**) and **37 fee-only visits** — 65 turned up in a quarter of the book, ~250 book-wide — and the fee-only cases are worse than "one visit": VOs **3206-5** and **4838-7** have *every* visit fee-only, so pre-fix they read **0** completed sessions, 4838-7 being a finished 6-of-6 VO showing 0. **An edge no AC covers:** the shipped "no treatment-kind row" branch doubles on `double_treatment` **without** checking V2 — faithful to `Activity::getActivityCountContribution()`, but it would move a V2 VO, against AC3; **0 instances exist on production**, so it is latent and is reported rather than asserted. **Traps:** `activity.doubleTreatment` is omitted when false and arrives as `true`, `false` or absent on the SAME VO, so only `=== true` is safe (#3602); `/v2/prescriptions` returns a **bare array** with no `totalItems` (the book size comes from `/prescriptions` under `ld+json`); **`doubleTreatment`/`doubleTreatmentV2` are silently IGNORED as filters** there — filtered and unfiltered pages come back with byte-identical row ids (#3449's shape) — so the population must be walked; `/activities` registers **`prescription[]`**, which takes a batch of ids and is the only reason a census is affordable; and the collection is **id-ascending, so Aktiv VOs are at the END of the book** — a slice from page 1 is archived history, and a screen test fed from there skips for want of a live fixture (`walkRecentVos()` exists for exactly that). Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.session-count-parity.page.ts`

`tests/Production/SuperAdmin/sa_risk_rows_and_dates.spec.ts` (`@SuperAdmin @ProdRiskRows @ReadOnly`, two tests also `@Slow`) covers **RC 3.14 #3814** (the ready-for-billing dates count from the LAST SIGNED TREATMENT) and **#3803** (the risk table lists each VO ONCE with all its risks) from the PRODUCTION side. **Both verified live on 2026-09-29; 4 passed, 0 `fixme`, in 54 s.** **STRICTLY READ-ONLY, and that decides what is checkable:** every request is a GET and the board is navigated and read. **`POST /kpis/orga/risks/export` is deliberately not called** — it persists nothing, but it is a POST, so on production it is left for a human; the cost is stated rather than glossed, namely that #3814 AC5's *Fertig seit (Tage)* COLUMN and #3803's CSV can only be seen through it. Both rules are verified anyway, because that column IS `daysSince` and the CSV's one-row-per-VO is the same grouping the screen paints. **NO PATIENT DATA:** the payload carries real `patientName` values on production, and nothing in the file reads, asserts on or logs them — every assertion is a count, a date or a VO number, and the screen reader takes only the group header (therapist, count, value), never a VO row. **The production API is `https://api.app.therapios.de`** — `api.therapios.de` does not resolve, and the host is discoverable only from the deployed bundle (5 references). It reports **3.14.0**. **#3814 needs no export because the ticket reduces to one question:** `daysSince` is the Status/Frist figure and `statusDate` is the date it counts from, so the whole AC is *is `statusDate` the last signed treatment?* — answered by reading each VO's own activities. Measured over a 120-VO deterministic spread of the 396-row tile: **120 of 120 match the signed-treatment predicate while the naive "last treatment of any kind" matches only 115**, so **5 discriminating VOs** carry the evidence, and an anti-vacuity guard requires the naive predicate to do strictly worse (without it the test passes against a build that just took the last activity). AC2 over the whole tile: all three end statuses carry a value — **Abgebrochen 131/131, Fertig Behandelt 219/219, Abgelaufen 46/46** — where before #3814 the column was filled only for finished VOs; and the tile's own >30-day rule still holds on the corrected date (min `daysSince` **31**, 0 rows at ≤30). **THE TRAP THAT PRODUCED FOUR FALSE MISMATCHES before it was understood:** the signed-treatment fields are **`rejectedTreatment` / `rejectedTreatmentWithSignature`**, NOT `rejected` / `rejectedWithSignature`. The shorter names do not exist on an Activity, so a predicate built on them excludes nothing, silently degrades to "last treatment of any kind" and reports mismatches on a correct build — 4 in the first 60 VOs, every one a later REJECTED session. #3649's negative test with the field names that matter. **#3803 is verified on the screen against the payload as an oracle**, since the provider is unchanged (it still serves one row per VO PER TILE) and the change is the client-side grouping: **all 148 painted groups match the distinct-VO count, and 100 of them discriminate** — i.e. would show a different number under the pre-fix row counting (Σ distinct **5,869** against Σ rows **6,192**). The complement is asserted too: with the ready tile selected the two counting rules have **0** disagreements, because within one tile a VO appears once — which is why #3803's AC8 could freeze that download — and **323 VOs carry more than one risk**, the population that makes the no-tile view differ at all. Both anti-vacuity guards are explicit. **Traps:** `/kpis/orga/risks` wraps `tiles`+`rows` in a SINGLE Hydra member, so the ordinary `member` unwrap yields one element and a row scan finds nothing (#3774); the readiness gate waits for a painted GROUP, not for the section or the column headers, which paint first and make `paintedGroups()` return `[]` — reading exactly like the table not being grouped; and a deactivated therapist paints `"<name> (Inaktiv)"` while the payload carries the bare name, with stored names not clean (a double space collapses in HTML), so both sides go through `normalizeName()`. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.prod-risk-rows-dates.page.ts`

`tests/Production/SuperAdmin/sa_patient_merge_list.spec.ts` (`@SuperAdmin @ProdPatientMerge @ReadOnly`) covers **RC 3.14 #3804** (the patient merge command runs a reviewed list given at run time) from the PRODUCTION side. **Checked 2026-09-29: 3 passed, 2 gated. The verdict is that the fix is deployed and the one-time operations run is still outstanding** — which is the ticket's own post-deploy step, so it is reported rather than failed. **READ-ONLY and PII-minimising by construction.** Every request is a GET; nothing runs the command, which is console-only. A `patient_merged` log's `meta` carries a real person's **name, birth date and insurance number**, so the page object strips those at the boundary — `MergeLog` models only the numbers and the meta KEY NAMES, which means no assertion, log line or trace can carry them. Patient records are read for existence and VO count only. **The 'not yet run' verdict is established two independent ways, and they must agree:** (1) the audit trail — production holds **92 `patient_merged` logs and all 92 are from 2026-08-05**, #3131's one-off batch, with **0** naming AC6's pair and **0** merges since; (2) the records themselves — patient numbers **8181** and **8864** still resolve to **different** patient ids, each still holding its own VOs. A merge would collapse them to one. **THE NUMBER-VS-ID TRAP IS PINNED ON PRODUCTION DATA, because getting it wrong reads a stranger's record rather than failing.** The ticket and the list CSV both say "patient 8181 / 8864" and the CSV column is literally named `patientId` — but that is the UI patient NUMBER (`patient.patientId`), not the API id: on production **number 8181 is api id 6922** and **number 8864 is id 7630**, so `?id[]=8181&id[]=8864` cheerfully returns two unrelated real patients (it did here, mid-investigation). **`?patientId=` is silently IGNORED on `/patients`** — every value returns all **9,715** rows — so the cheap correct route is the VO number, since a VO is `<patientNumber>-<n>` and `?prescriptionId[]=` IS a registered multi-value filter; one request resolves both records without walking the collection. Same family as #3790, where the same mistake resolved to a different real patient. **The two AC tests are GATED on the run having happened, not `fixme`'d**, so the file begins verifying the moment operations executes the command with no edit: AC3/AC4 assert every post-#3131 merge log is system-written, records who was removed, and writes exactly one entry per merged record; AC6 asserts 8864 merges into 8181, the merged number no longer holds VOs of its own, and the survivor's VOs all sit on one record. Staging coverage of the same ticket (the dev team's 25 Sep run, 7 passed) is in `tests/Staging/SuperAdmin/sa_patient_merge_list.spec.ts`. **The operations list itself holds names, birth dates and insurance numbers and lives only in `s3://therapios-csv-files`** — it must never reach Drive, Slack, the ticket or this repo, and nothing in either spec touches it. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.prod-patient-merge.page.ts`

`tests/Production/SuperAdmin/sa_discharge_window.spec.ts` (`@SuperAdmin @ProdDischarge @ReadOnly`, one test also `@Slow`) covers **RC 3.14 #3830** (a discharge VO SHOWS day 7 / day 12) and **#3800** (it EXPIRES on those days) from the PRODUCTION side. **Checked 2026-09-29: 4 passed, 1 `fixme` — and the headline is a SPLIT between the two tickets.** **READ-ONLY:** every request a GET. The nightly command is never run, and #3830's AC6 creation-check preview is skipped here because it is a POST (it is transient and writes nothing, so it stays in the staging spec). **#3830 IS live on production:** of the discharge VOs issued since 2026-08-01, **all 24 in-scope ones show `treatmentStartDeadline` = issue+7 and `validityDate` = issue+12**, the **1 PKV** one carries neither (excluded from expiry entirely, #3709), and there are **0** BG / Privat Basis / no-insurance-type ones — exactly what #3830's own "Expected effect" forecast. AC2 holds on the closed population too (18 closed across Abgelaufen and Abgerechnet, all still +7/+12). **#3800 is NOT firing:** in a 1,672-entry expiry-log tail spanning 2026-07-28…09-28 there are **ZERO** `Entlassmanagement:` reasons, while **4 open discharge VOs sit past their window with no expiry** (10469-2 `window12` due 09-23, 10786-1 `start7` due 09-23, 10807-1 `window12` due 09-27, 10792-1 `window12` due 09-28) — three of them due BEFORE the last nightly run. **The control is what makes that evidence rather than a guess, and it is its own test:** the job demonstrably RUNS — **1,523 automatic expiries** in the tail, newest **2026-09-28T20:02:24** (one day old), **249** of them carrying #3651's German `Behandlungsbeginn nicht innerhalb von N Tagen` — so "no discharge expiry" cannot be explained by the job being switched off, and #3651 is deployed. Both commits are on `release/3.14.0` and the shared `App\Util\DischargeWindow` is demonstrably in the build, since the shown dates come from it — so the cause needs a developer and is not guessed at here. The AC1/AC6 test is `fixme`'d against that finding with the dates measured, and its standing form is the catch-up invariant (a discharge VO may only still be open if it became due AFTER the last run). **A CORRECTION worth keeping, because the first reading of this was wrong:** production's 10 expired discharge VOs looked at first like the job applying the ORDINARY rule to discharge VOs — but 8 of them read **`Manual expiration`** (admins clearing them by hand, 7 on 24–25 Sep, which is the very work #3800 exists to remove) and only 2 are automatic, both `Start deadline expired` at exactly 28 days and both BEFORE #3800 shipped. Read `meta.type` and the reason text before attributing an expiry to a rule. **Traps, all confirmed on production as well as staging:** `?isDischargeManagement=` is silently IGNORED so the population must be walked with the flag read per row (the FIELD is serialized even though the filter is not), which is the file's whole runtime — production paginates `/prescriptions` at ~10 s per 100 rows, so `SINCE` is 2026-08-01 (~46 pages, ~8 min) and that is sound because an OPEN discharge VO is inside or just past a 12-day window by construction; **`order[id]` is silently ignored on `/prescription_logs`** and the collection is id-ascending, so an `order[id]=desc` "newest N" scan returns the OLDEST N — on production that is 2019-era history with no discharge reason, reading exactly like the rule never firing; the signed-treatment fields are **`rejectedTreatment` / `rejectedTreatmentWithSignature`** (the short names do not exist, so a predicate on them excludes nothing); and **`test.setTimeout()` must be called INSIDE `beforeAll`** — neither `describe.configure({ timeout })` nor the describe-level `setTimeout` reaches it, and a killed hook surfaces as `Request context disposed` on every later request, which reads like an auth failure. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.prod-discharge.page.ts`

`tests/Production/SuperAdmin/sa_ready_for_billing.spec.ts` (`@SuperAdmin @ProdReadyBilling @ReadOnly`, two tests also `@Slow`) covers **RC 3.14 #3775** (one shared ready-for-billing population behind three surfaces) and **#3785** (those rows name the VO's real status and gain the issue date and sessions done) from the PRODUCTION side. **Checked 2026-09-29: 7 passed, 0 `fixme`, 38 s.** **READ-ONLY:** every request a GET, plus navigating and reading the board. The grouped view's download is a POST and is deliberately not called, so #3785 AC5's eleven CSV columns are not checked here — its DATA is, from the payload the export is built from. **No patient data** is read or logged. Verified: **#3785 deployed** — all six Thema texts ship (three Orga, three Admin-Performance) and the combined finished/cancelled label #3785 replaced is at **0**; the grouped view paints exactly `Patient:in · VO # · Ausst. Datum · Wert · HM · Thema · Stufe · Status/Frist · Beh. Status · Empfohlene Aktion`, with `Ausst. Datum` and `Beh. Status` asserted by ORDER rather than exact equality so a later ticket adding a column does not fail it. **#3775 AC1** — the tile's **396** rows equal its reported count, span exactly the three end statuses (Fertig Behandelt 219 / Abgebrochen 131 / Abgelaufen 46) and no other, **0 of 40** sampled VOs sit in a billing batch, and **0** rows lack a `daysSince` (which is derived from the last signed treatment, so a VO without one could not carry a value — the gate's observable consequence where the count is not exposed). **AC6** with both boundary lines live: **0 rows at exactly 30 days and 1 at exactly 31**. **AC5** therapist-for-therapist against the Arbeitszeiten column — **91 therapists on both surfaces, 0 mismatches** — and scoped that way deliberately, because the two surfaces hold different therapist populations (101 vs 143) so comparing TOTALS reports a mismatch that is not one. **#3785 AC3/AC4** over the whole tile: **396/396** carry an issue date, across all three statuses, and 0 of the 328 non-Blanko rows lack a session count. **THE TRAP THAT FAILED THIS FILE'S FIRST RUN, and it is one this suite has documented twice before:** the bundle **escapes non-ASCII**, so `für` is stored as `f\xfcr` and a literal search for *"Fertig behandelt, bereit für die Abrechnung"* returns **0** — reading exactly like "never shipped" (#3337, #3611). The three Orga texts are pure ASCII and passed, the three Admin ones are not and did not; `escapedOccurrences()` now counts both forms. **A difference from staging worth knowing:** production's Blanko rows carry `totalTreatments` of **0, 1 AND 24**, where on staging every Blanko VO reported 0 — so `n / BV` keys on the Blanko FLAG, not on the total being zero, and a spec built on staging's data would encode the wrong rule. **FINDING, reported as a green test rather than a `fixme` because it is an ACCESS gate and not a defect:** `GET /kpis/admin-performance/risks` answers **403** for this Super Admin on production (against a **404** control, which is what makes it a refusal rather than a missing route), and `/me` confirms **`canAccessAdminPerformanceBoard: false`** while `canAccessTherapeutenOrgaBoard` is true. So #3775's third surface — and with it AC2 (the differing age settings), AC3 (the PKV-with-active-invoice exception) and AC5's Admin-Performance half, plus #3785 AC6's own board — cannot be verified from production with this login; they are covered on staging, where the same account can open the board. The probe re-measures each run, so the gap cannot go stale. Uses `Pages/superadmin/sa.prod-risk-rows-dates.page.ts`

`tests/Production/SuperAdmin/sa_monday_row_pairing.spec.ts` (`@SuperAdmin @ProdMondaySync @ReadOnly`) covers **RC 3.14 #3783** (a facility follows the Monday.com row NUMBER it keeps, not its Einrichtungs-ID) from the PRODUCTION side. **Checked 2026-09-29: 5 passed, 0 `fixme`, 8 s — and the honest headline is that DEPLOYMENT IS NOT DECIDABLE on production**, which the file proves rather than guessing at. **Read-only:** every request a GET, including the report's signed URL; the nightly sync is a console command and is never run. **Why it is undecidable, and why that is a result rather than a gap:** the only thing the fix ADDS to a run report is a `conflict` row, and rule 9 raises one only when a Monday row is kept by **two ACTIVE facilities**.
